# approval-atlas

Static site exploring approved medicines across jurisdictions. Milestone 1 (EMA end to end) is done; **Milestone 2 is in progress** (phases below, each stops for review).

**Primary use case:** a phone lookup during conference talks/posters — type a drug, substance or condition, see EU approval status (what for, since when) and open the SmPC / EPAR. Charts are secondary. Keep first load small and the lookup usable offline.

## Scope
- M2 phases: (1) U.S. labels, filters + URL state, MeSH branches, ATC names, "Authorized now" view, authorized-over-time series — done; (1b) pocket lookup: search, medicine/substance cards with document links, condition results + timeline, regulatory-protection estimates, orphan exclusivity, offline — **current**; (2) ChEMBL enrichment: modality, mechanism, ATC cleanup, development-code synonyms in search; (3) regulatory events from EMA "procedural steps" PDFs + Lifecycle view; (4) MAH country (Union Register) + map tab. Hosting (public repo + GitHub Pages) after 1b. Companies (holder names folded to companies and current-owner groups; the user asked 2026-09-28, see "Companies" below): part 1 (pipeline, GLEIF matching, review lists) done; part 2 (frontend: company pages, holder filter by group, monograms) next. **Pivotal results** (owner request 2026-09-30, from a colleague: "how good do you have to be"; spec `.remember/efficacy/efficacy-spec.md`, see "Pivotal results" below): phases A0 (gold set and model choice), A1 (extractor and verifier), A2 (pipeline step and card UI) built on `feature/pivotal-results`; A0's paid model runs and A1's first extraction are the owner's (API key); A3 (every Authorised medicine) next; B (EPAR section 3) and C (the NSCLC condition benchmark) later, only when asked.
- Do NOT start without being asked: FDA (Drugs@FDA / openFDA, Purple Book, Orange Book patents), cross-jurisdiction matching (INN/UNII), approval-lag views, other regulators, ownership history (companies show the current owner only), EU patent/SPC data (no open EU-wide source exists — show regulatory-protection estimates only, never call them patents).
- No backend, no database. Everything precomputed; filters run in the browser.

## Architecture decisions (with why)
- **Frontend: Vite + D3**, not Observable Framework. Framework is in maintenance mode (Observable, 2026-03-02: "not developing new features … recommend that users migrate to Notebook Kit"). Vanilla JS modules, no UI framework.
- **R pipeline is independent of the frontend**: writes files into `site/public/data/`; the frontend only reads them.
- **JSON, not Parquet**: small data; JSON needs no JS library. Revisit when events or FDA data make files large.
- **EMA column names kept verbatim** (British spelling). Columns we derive use U.S. spelling (`authorized_from`).
- **Approval date = `marketing_authorisation_date`.** `european_commission_decision_date` is the latest EC decision of any kind — never an approval date.
- **"Authorized now" tile vs over-time series**: tile = status `Authorised` with an approval date; series = dates (`authorized_from` ≤ d < `authorized_until`). Excluded from the series (`series_exclusion`): never-authorized statuses; ended status without an end date (88 on 2026-09-26 — prefer unknown to a guess); no approval date. The build fails if the tile ≠ the series' last point (snapshot date) and lists the rows.
- **Series computed twice on purpose**: R writes the unfiltered series; the browser recomputes it from per-product intervals so filters apply; a node test asserts they match.
- **Distinct active substances** = distinct normalised INN sets (`international_non_proprietary_name_common_name`, or `active_substance` where the INN field repeats the medicine's name (Vysribli), split on `;` and on ` / ` outside parentheses — e.g. "(rdESAT-6 / rCFP-10)" is one antigen — lower-case). Salts not merged.
- **Regulatory-protection estimate** (`R/protection.R`; step 3 of the 2026-09-28 roadmap, scrutiny #6; estimates only, never "patent"): 8 + 2 (+ 1) years from the first central approval of the product's substance set. The set: the keys of `ema_medicine_substances.json` (the INN field's, or the `active_substance` field's where EMA's INN field repeats the medicine's name: `build_substances_table()`, `inn_names_medicine()`, name compared without its parentheses; Vysribli's "Vysribli" is denosumab in the substances table, search index and `substance_set_key` alike; only Vysribli and Garenoxacin mesylate on 2026-09-28, the latter unchanged), with keys joined by `curated_substance_equivalents()` (`R/curated-substances.R`: 33 pairs, each checked by hand against an EPAR page (active substance beside INN, "generic of"/"hybrid of"/"same as"), with a note; never a salt-stripping rule: fluticasone furoate/propionate and tenofovir disoproxil/alafenamide are different substances, a test guards them; sevelamer carbonate left out; transitive, so sitagliptin fumarate and sitagliptin hydrochloride monohydrate meet through sitagliptin; published as `ema_substance_equivalents.json`, pairs whose keys are not in the data warn and are left out). The first product (`first_products()`): among the set's non-generic, non-biosimilar products of the first approval's company group approved within 30 days of it, an authorised one, then the lowest product number (Humira over Trudexa, Plavix over Iscover a day earlier; the group limit keeps Ebixa (Lundbeck) the reference of Axura (Merz), Pritor (Bayer) of Micardis (Boehringer Ingelheim), Glustin (Takeda) of Actos (Cheplapharm)); `counted_from` is the first approval's date, as protection runs from the first authorisation (global marketing authorisation), not the named product's own (Humira's counts from Trudexa's 1 Sep 2003; the frontend must not show it as the named product's approval date). A generic or biosimilar follows the first product of its set as EMA spells it, else of the equivalent set (Inpremzia keeps Novo Nordisk's insulin, not Sanofi's Insuman; Dasatinib Accord Healthcare gets Sprycel). Another medicine counts from the equivalent set's first product; when that belongs to another company group (`ema_medicine_companies.json` `group_key`; groups are current owners, so licensees and copies of another company's medicine are flagged too, e.g. Roteas, Susvimo; curated copies are not: see below) and the first product of the medicine's own group came later (`own_counted_from` > `counted_from`), unless both estimates have ended, `basis` is `other_company_reference` with `own_reference_product_number` and `own_counted_from`; the market protection range then runs from the reference estimate's minimum to the own estimate's maximum and `status` is read from it, so it is `unclear` only where the two estimates' statuses differ (Opzelura: ended counted from Jakavi, protected from itself) and `protected` where both are (Qdenga, Spikevax: 12 on 2026-09-28; 6 on 2026-09-29, after the curated copies below: Kinpeygo and Ogluo are hybrids, and Sixmo and Cufence count from themselves, as Buvidal and Cuprior are hybrids). EMA's data flags only generics and biosimilars as copies. **Curated copies** (step 4, 2026-09-29; generalized from hybrids in the step 4 review): `curated_copy_medicines()` (`R/curated-copies.R`: `ema_product_number`, `copy_type` (`hybrid` | `generic` | `biosimilar`), `reference_product_number` (null when the reference medicine the EPAR names is authorised only nationally), `reference_name` (the reference as the page names it, national ones too: Hydrea, Subutex), `evidence_url` (the EPAR page whose overview says so), `evidence_quote` (verbatim, at most 20 words; it must name the copy type), `checked_date`, `note` (null unless needed: Riulvy's other substance, Lumeblue's former name, Okedi's reference form)): 41 rows, 39 hybrids plus a generic and a biosimilar whose EMA flags are false (Sugammadex Adroiq: Bridion, Tuznue: Herceptin), from the EPAR pages of the 56 `other_company_reference` products of 2026-09-28 (withdrawn ones without an overview on the page: their overview PDF) plus Riulvy, Colchicine Agepha Pharma, Cuprior, Buvidal, Okedi and Camcevi, and (owner request 2026-09-29, "complete the hybrid list") the 12 hybrids of the EPAR copy scan of every Authorised product counted as its own and protected (464 pages; 17 call the medicine a hybrid, the other 5 are PUMAs, below) and, in review, Nordimet (Lantarel FS), the one hybrid among the 44 counted as their own and unclear, all of nationally authorised references (Ledaga: Caryolysine, Nyxoid: Naloxon HCl B. Braun, Phelinun: Alkeran 50 mg/10 ml, Ryjunea: Atropin-POS, Trepulmix: Remodulin…); each quote and reference name checked against the saved page. **Paediatric-use marketing authorisations** (PUMAs, Regulation (EC) No 1901/2006; 2026-09-29): a PUMA has 8 + 2 years of data and market protection of its own (EMA's PUMA page), even where its application is a hybrid one, so it is never a copy: `curated_puma_medicines()` (`R/curated-copies.R`: `ema_product_number`, `evidence_url` (its EMA public assessment report, whose introduction says the application was for one; Tuzulby's is filed as a variation report), `evidence_quote` (verbatim, at most 20 words; it must name a paediatric-use marketing authorisation), `checked_date`, `note` (the six whose EPAR page calls them hybrids say so)): 11, Buccolam, Hemangiol, Sialanar, Alkindi, Slenyto, Kigabeq, Pedmarqsi, Aqumeldi, Neoatricon, Tuzulby and Bopediat. EMA publishes no list of PUMAs, so the candidates checked were the scan's 17 hybrids, every curated copy (by indication; Alkindi, a curated hybrid before, left the copies), and older substances in medicines for children (Verkazia, Amglidia, Ryjunea and Gencebok are none). A PUMA has basis `paediatric_use`: counted from its own approval (`reference_product_number` itself, `counted_from` its own date, never `other_company_reference`; Alkindi from 9 Feb 2018, not Plenadren's 2011; Slenyto not from Circadin's 2007), `copy_source` null, even where EMA flagged it generic or biosimilar; it stays another medicine's possible first product, and a curated copy of a PUMA follows the PUMA's own date. On 2026-09-29 Alkindi (unclear) and Slenyto (ended) became protected; Sialanar is unclear either way (from its own 15 Sep 2016). A PUMA that is also a curated copy, or a malformed row (product number, evidence, a quote not naming a paediatric-use marketing authorisation, duplicates), stops the build; a row whose medicine is not in the data warns and is left out. The rows in the data are published as `ema_curated_pumas.json` and credited in `meta.json` (`curated_pumas_source_entry()`); the EPAR copy scan leaves them out of its candidates. The medicine card's protection section says so in place of the counted-from line, never naming the PUMA its substance's "first central EU approval" (Alkindi's is Plenadren): "A pediatric-use marketing authorization (for children): protection counted from its own approval, 9 Feb 2018" (`UI.protection.paediatricUse()`, `protectionSummary()`), with a "Source" link to its `ema_curated_pumas.json` row's report (the file loads `{ optional }` with the protection files; the sentence alone without it); the caveats ignore pediatric rewards "other than a pediatric-use marketing authorization's own protection". A curated copy is a follower (never another medicine's first product: Sixmo, counted from Buvidal before, counts from itself): with a central reference it follows it (`follows_reference`, `counted_from` = the reference's own `counted_from`, whatever its substances: Riulvy (tegomil fumarate) follows Tecfidera (dimethyl fumarate); 12 on 2026-09-29, e.g. Liraglutide STADA: Victoza, Ablymico: Saxenda, Armisarte: Alimta, Sugammadex Adroiq: Bridion), with a national one it has `reference_not_found` (no dates, `unclear`; 29 on 2026-09-29, e.g. Xromi: Hydrea, Buvidal: Subutex, Colchicine Agepha Pharma: Colchicine Tiofarma). The protection row's `copy_source` names what made it a copy (`ema_flag` | `curated`); the curated rows in the data are published as `ema_curated_copies.json` with their evidence and credited in `meta.json` (`curated_copies_source_entry()`, version "Checked {latest checked_date}"). The frontend must not describe a curated copy's `counted_from` as the first approval of its own substance (Riulvy's is Tecfidera's, dimethyl fumarate), but as its reference medicine's. Malformed rows (product numbers, a copy type the quote does not name, a reference that is the medicine itself or another curated copy, no reference name, evidence) stop the build; rows whose medicine or central reference is not in the data (dated) warn and are left out. Still counted as medicines of their own: informed-consent and duplicate copies (Roteas: Lixiana, Senstend: Fortacin) and copies not yet checked (README caveat); `scripts/scan-epar-copies.R` lists candidates (see "Downloads"). The run summary lists every product whose status differs from the file the last run wrote (`report_protection_changes()`).
- **Therapeutic-area broad category** = every top-level MeSH tree branch of the matched descriptor (NLM XML; exact heading → entry term → curated → unmatched). A medicine counts in every branch it touches. **Sub-areas** (user decision 2026-09-27: an expandable tree to MeSH level 3, branch › level 2 › level 3, counts at each level) = every level-2 and level-3 tree node above or at the matched descriptor (`ema_therapeutic_area_subtree.json`); a medicine counts once in every node it touches, so children do not add up to their parent; a term matched at a branch root ("Neoplasms") has no sub-area (the UI needs a "not more specific" row, as `atcExactCounts()`). Node names from the descriptor holding that tree number (unique at depths 2–3 in MeSH 2026; the build stops if that changes).
- **ATC**: EMA codes kept as published (`atc_code_human`; `atc_incomplete` when not a valid level-5 code: levels 1–4, or malformed like `LX1XX02`, `VO4D` with `atc_level` null). **`atc_code`** is the code the site should use: EMA's, or — when EMA's is incomplete or malformed — the code(s) in section 5.1 of the product information (SmPC) PDF (`atc_code_source` `ema_smpc`, user decision 2026-09-27, EMA reuse with credit; fass.se is not a source). An SmPC code completes EMA's when it is a valid code deeper than EMA's and fits it (starts with EMA's valid prefix, or WHO moved codes from under that prefix to it, e.g. EMA L01XE → SmPC L01EA01); an EMA level-5 code is never replaced. **Conflicts** (user decision 2026-09-27, phase 4d): when no SmPC code fits an incomplete (or malformed) EMA code, a deeper SmPC code that fits none of the product's EMA codes replaces it, marked `atc_code_conflict` true (5 on 2026-09-27: Yttriga V09 → V10X, Giapreza C09 → C01CX09, Vocabria J05AX → J05AJ04, Kerendia C09 → C03DA05, Libmeldy N07 → A16AB21); a shallower or equal misfit keeps EMA's (Pombiliti: EMA A16AB, SmPC A16A, rejected). Ambiguous SmPC codes are not used (several for one EMA code, or one conflicting code for several), so there stays one row per EMA code; the run summary lists conflicts, rejections and ambiguous codes separately. **Products with no EMA code** (missing or "Not yet assigned"; user decision 2026-09-27, phase 4d) get one row per distinct SmPC code (`source` `ema_smpc`, `atc_code_human` and `atc_level` null, `atc_incomplete` false, as `atc_level` and `atc_incomplete` describe EMA's published code only; the SmPC code may be at any level, so its own level tells whether it is complete; 16 products on 2026-09-27, all level 5). **Curated codes** (user decision 2026-09-27, phase 4e): `curated_atc_codes()` in `R/curated-atc-codes.R` (`ema_product_number`, `atc_code`, `evidence_source` `whocc_index` | `whocc_temporary` | `ema_smpc_text`, `evidence_url`, `checked_date`, `note`) holds 114 codes checked by hand for products whose code was still incomplete or missing: the proposals a second, independent check confirmed with high or medium confidence against saved ATC/DDD Index 2026 pages, the WHOCC temporary list (9 codes; `status` `temporary` in `atc_classes.json`, they can still change) or the SmPC text (all re-checked against that evidence); not the ones judged wrong (Tacquell, Imagify), doubtful (Rotashield) or low-confidence, rows without a proposal, or codes the product already had (Giapreza, Kerendia, Vocabria, Libmeldy, Ontilyv). A curated code is used (`atc_code_source` `curated`) only for an EMA code still incomplete after the SmPC step, i.e. one the SmPC step left as it was or gave a code not at its final level (see "Final level" below: level 5, or a level-4 code WHO no longer subdivides), judged against EMA's published code under the rules for SmPC codes (valid, deeper, fits or replaces it as a marked conflict; never in place of an EMA level-5 code or an SmPC code at its final level); such an SmPC code then gives way to the curated code (the SmPC's verdict `curated`) and stays where none is used (Strimvelis: EMA L03, SmPC L03AX, no curated code). LeukoScan (2026-09-29): EMA publishes the malformed VO4D, its SmPC reads V04D, which is no WHO class (WHO's V04 has V04B and V04C), and the curated V09HA04 (technetium (99mTc) sulesomab, ATC/DDD Index 2026) replaces it, no conflict, as it fits VO4D's valid start V (a malformed code keeps its valid start only; V09HA04 would not fit the SmPC's V04D, but the conflict flag is about EMA's code). And a curated code is used for a product without an EMA code whose SmPC gives no code at its final level: in place of an incomplete SmPC code under the same rules (the SmPC code in EMA's role; `source` then `curated`, the SmPC's verdict `curated`), or as its code when the SmPC gives none (none either way on 2026-09-28). The run summary lists the curated codes used and not used (with why: `smpc`, the product's SmPC code is at its final level; `ema_complete`; `rejected`, including one not used against an SmPC code not at its final level; `not_in_data`) and the Authorised products without a complete code. On 2026-09-29: 88 used (9 conflicts, e.g. Aduhelm N07 → N06DX03, Natalizumab Elan Pharma L03AD → L04AG03; LeukoScan's V09HA04 again, as on 2026-09-28, after the first check of its SmPC had displaced it), 26 not used as their SmPCs now give the same code (22) or the retired code WHO moved to it (4: Incivo J05AE11 → J05AP02, Victrelis J05AE12 → J05AP03, Portrazza L01XC22 → L01FE03, Lumoxiti L01XC34 → L01FB02) (2026-09-28: 109 used, 5 not used); 14 Authorised products left: Yttriga, EndolucinBeta, Lutetium (177Lu) chloride Billev, Theralugand, Ilumira (V10X), GalliaPharm, GalenVita (V09X), Lumeblue (V04CX), Strimvelis (L03AX), and Cuprymina, Vimkunya, Myqorzo, Boey, Wayrilz (no code anywhere). Every curated code has a WHO name in `atc_classes.json`. **Final level** (phase 4e): `atc_final_level` is true when `atc_code` is complete: a level-5 code, or a level-4 code WHO no longer subdivides, i.e. one the WHOCC alterations list moved level-5 codes under it up to ("ATC 5th levels deleted, all products classified on the 4th level"; only B03AC, iron parenteral preparations, 2014: Feraheme's EMA B03AC, Rienso's curated B03AC). The rule reads only the alterations list: were WHO to add level-5 codes under such a code again, the rule would need a check against the yearly lists. The frontend treats a code as incomplete when `atc_final_level` is false, not by its level. **Retired codes** (user decision 2026-09-27): `current_atc_code` = the code WHO moved a retired code to, transitively (L01XX28 → L01XE01 → L01EA01), from the WHOCC cumulative alterations list (level 5; split or partial moves keep the old code in use) or the curated table (`curated_retired_atc_levels` in `R/whocc.R`, a reason per row; its rows win over the list's): L01XC → L01F, as every L01XC code but L01XC04 (moved to L04AA34 in 2015) moved to L01F in 2022; L01XE → L01E, all in 2021; J07BX03 covid-19 vaccines → J07BN (level 4; user decision 2026-09-27: WHO deleted J07BX03 in 2023 without a successor, and COVID-19 vaccines are classified in J07BN); group and filter by `current_atc_code` (else `atc_code`), display the published code with its old name and "retired {year}, now {code}". Level names (user decision 2026-09-27, phase 4d: WHO's over ChEMBL's): the name alterations of the WHOCC yearly lists (sheet "ATC level name alterations"; this year's list, then last year's), then the curated WHO names (`curated_atc_name_corrections` in `R/whocc.R`: the 13 codes where ChEMBL_37's name differs from the ATC/DDD Index 2026 (version 2026-01-20), e.g. ChEMBL's "hemophilus" and "stems cells"; J07BX01 and V09IX15 are also renamed in the 2026 list; copied verbatim from the index pages and checked by hand), each replacing another name only where it differs (the run summary lists them); then ChEMBL's ATC table; then, for codes ChEMBL lacks, the new codes of the yearly lists (this year's first), single WHOCC index pages, the temporary list (`status` `temporary`), the alterations list (old level-5 names) and the curated archived names (all WHOCC names, verbatim in the data — a declared exception to the U.S.-spelling rule; Title Case is display-only). Display only: names in Title Case at every level, data verbatim (user decisions 2026-09-26 and 2026-09-27; see Frontend, `atcDisplayName()`); the footer credit stays short, the About disclosure says the names are shown in title case (legal review 2026-09-30), README has the detail; interpretive risk under WHOCC's "no changes" term accepted.
- **ATC class explanations** (owner decisions 2026-09-29; `R/atc-explanations.R`): a plain-language explanation of what each ATC class at levels 1–4 the data uses is for, not a restatement of WHO's name (543 on 2026-09-29: 14 at level 1, 72 at 2, 138 at 3, 319 at 4; level 5 has none, its substance's name says what it is). Our own texts, written and reviewed by hand in two rounds (`.remember/atc-explanations/review.md`), in the committed input `data-raw/atc-class-explanations.json`; WHO's codes and names stay WHO's (`atc_classes.json`, excluded from our licence), the explanations are ours (part of the CC BY-SA 4.0 compilation; credited so in the About disclosure's sources, README and `meta.json`). **Owner rule** (standing): each text is generic to its WHO class, never naming or describing the medicines in it now (no brand; a substance only where the class's WHO name holds it or it is what the class acts on, e.g. "testosterone" for anti-androgens), so a class never needs rechecking when a medicine joins. The texts are final: never edit them; report one that seems wrong to the owner. The pipeline (`read_atc_explanations()`, `check_atc_explanations()`, `build_atc_explanations_table()`) stops the build (`cli::cli_abort()` listing offenders) on a code that is not an ATC class at levels 1–4, a repeated code, a level that is not the code's, an empty text, a text over 20 words, an em-dash, a text naming a medicine name, substance key or EMA active-substance text of the data (`atc_explanation_terms()`; the texts catch salts and spellings the keys lack: Neoatricon's key is "dopamine hydrochloride", its text "Dopamine") (whole words, case-insensitive: `fold_explanation_words()`) unless it is in the class's WHO name or on `atc_explanation_allowed_words()` in the classes named there (each word with its `codes`, the classes it is allowed in and their descendants, and its reason; review of the explanations: a word allowed everywhere would let "testosterone" pass in G03B, the androgens, where it names a medicine; derived by running the check on the texts of 2026-09-29: "amino acids" in A16AX (it names what it is not, its sibling A16AA's WHO name), "angiotensin ii" in C09A (what ACE inhibitors stop the body making), "dopamine" in C01CA, N04, N05A and N06BA (the messenger and receptor type they act on), "levodopa" in N04BA (its "dopa" by its INN), "parathyroid hormone" in H05B (what it lowers), "testosterone" in L02AE and L02B (what they act on); add a word or class only with its reason; a test checks every word is needed in every class listed). Other names of a substance (search.js `SPELLING_VARIANTS`, e.g. adrenaline for epinephrine, which 14 texts use as a receptor and messenger name) are not checked, a checked date that is no date or no source. It warns (the rows are left out) about classes the data uses without an explanation (one WHO does not name says "(no WHO name)": a new class needs a new text under the owner rule) and about explanations of classes the data no longer uses. The classes in use: every level 1–4 prefix of the code each ATC row is shown by, `current_atc_code` ?? `atc_code`, valid codes only (`atc_explained_classes_in_use()`), as the frontend's `atcCode()`; the prefixes of a retired code no row is shown by (L01XC) are not in use (none on 2026-09-29, every class in use has one).
- **Companies** (user decisions 2026-09-28; `R/companies.R`, `R/curated-companies.R`, `R/gleif.R`): two levels. A **company** folds spelling and legal-entity variants of holder names (rules at the "k2" tier: diacritics with German umlauts spelled out, "&"/"+" as "and", address tails ("c/o …", "… at Fieldfisher LLP") and parenthesised parts dropped, legal forms at the end (and "N.V." at the start), place and role words anywhere (Ireland, Europe, International, Registration, Holding, branch…); generic words such as "pharma" stay, as stripping them broke names; `company_match_key()`, plus curated aliases for renames and misspellings; an alias whose target has the same key only fixes the name, e.g. EMA's "Theravance Biopharma Ireland Umited" ("Umited" folds as a legal form) → "…Limited": it is no link of an alias chain, and a name that is an alias's (folded) is never the company's name); key `c.<slug>`, name = not a curated alias (a misspelling or an old name) while the alias's target or another spelling is at hand, then the spelling of most medicines, then EMA's; an address tail dropped ("Kamada BioPharma Limited"). A **group** is the current owner (never an ownership history), as the operating company people know (Chiesi, STADA, Boehringer Ingelheim, LFB), not a family holding, private-equity vehicle or state agency; GLEIF's literal ultimate parent is kept as provenance. Groups are curated for the ~60 largest and for smaller companies GLEIF or an announcement shows under one owner (Jazz with Gentium, Perrigo with HRA and Galpharm, Bracco, Cosmo with Cassiopea, Pharming, Dompé, Merz, Glenmark, Acino, Dr. Reddy's with betapharm, Haleon, Zydus with Sentynl (a sponsor, not a holder), Guerbet, Techdow; Otsuka with Avanir (a sponsor), Novartis with the IDB Group's I.D.B. Radiopharmacy (Lumark)) (`curated_company_groups()`, `curated_group_members()`: key `g.<slug>`, name, unique monogram (1–4 capitals or digits: a full ticker such as ABBV, VRTX, VTRS, user decision 2026-09-28 "4-letter badges are ok"; derived ones stay 1–3), LEI, members by any holder spelling, a note and evidence URL for each ownership change, as-of date). **Current owner of the business** (2026-09-28): a holder goes to the group that now owns the business its medicines belong to; where all of a holder's medicines went to one buyer, that buyer's group even when the entity went elsewhere (Novartis Vaccines and Diagnostics S.r.l.: four influenza vaccines → CSL/Seqirus 2015, the entity → GSK); a holder whose medicines went to different buyers stays with the entity's owner, and **per-medicine group rows** (user decision 2026-09-28; `curated_medicine_groups()`: product, the holder checked against, group key (a curated group), note, evidence URL, verbatim quote ≤ 20 words, checked date) put a medicine whose business went elsewhere in its current owner's group while its holder and company stay as published (`source` `curated_medicine`): Abbott Laboratories Ltd's Trudexa (adalimumab) and Ozespa (briakinumab) → AbbVie (Uprima unclear, stays Abbott); Elan Pharma International's natalizumab → Biogen; Viatris's biosimilars (Mylan's withdrawn Fulphila and Ogivri applications, Lextemy) → Biocon; MSD's women's health and established brands (Fertavid, Opulis, Allex, Zenhale) → Organon; Wyeth Consumer Healthcare's ibuprofen combination (Advil) → Haleon; these extra moves are user-confirmed (2026-09-28: BMS's copies → Sanofi, Viatris biosimilars → Biocon, MSD → Organon ×4, Wyeth's Advil → Haleon). Ioa → Theramex (user decision 2026-09-28: MSD, its holder when withdrawn in 2014, sold its further rights to Zoely to Theramex in 2020, keeping the U.S. and Canada). Later holders only the Union Register names, for products it no longer lists as Active (so EMA's older holder is used; user decision 2026-09-28, each confirmed by EMA's medicine page naming the holder at withdrawal, lapse or expiry): Panretin → Advanz (Amdipharm), Optimark → Guerbet (which bought Mallinckrodt's contrast media business in 2015), Thorinane → Techdow (Techdow Pharma Netherlands; a duplicate of its Inhixa); Lumark's later holder I.D.B. Holland is of the same IDB Group as EMA's I.D.B. Radiopharmacy, Novartis's since 2018 (via Advanced Accelerator Applications; the Dutch nuclear safety authority, 2024: "IDB (part of Novartis Netherlands)"), so I.D.B. Radiopharmacy is a Novartis member, not a per-medicine row; Hepacare and Tecnemab K1 stay, as the register names the earlier holder (Medeva before Evans Vaccines; Sorin Radiofarmaci before Amersham Sorin). A row is checked against the holder used (EMA's, or the Union Register's where it decides; never a sponsor, as sponsor rows are) and applies only while that holder still holds the medicine (else listed as stale) and it names another group than the company's (else redundant: not applied, and the run summary says so); a company none of whose medicines stays in its group joins the group of most of them (so no group is left empty; the run summary lists curated groups left without medicines). **Medicine notes** (user decision 2026-09-28; `curated_medicine_notes()`: product, note, https evidence URL, checked date): a note about a medicine's later ownership that does not move it fills its `group_note`/`group_evidence_url` while `source` stays as it is (Udenyca stays with Coherus (now Coherus Oncology), its sponsor; the note says Coherus sold the U.S. Udenyca business to Intas in April 2025, now Accord BioPharma's); a medicine with both a note and a per-medicine group row stops the build, a note naming no medicine in the data only warns. **Provenance** (user decision 2026-09-28): the note and evidence URL of the curated row that decided travel with the data: per medicine `group_note`/`group_evidence_url` (a per-medicine row, or a medicine note), `sponsor_note`/`sponsor_evidence_url` (a sponsor row) and `sponsor_rename_evidence_url` (that sponsor's rename since), and on each group's row its members' ownership notes and its renamed sponsors' old names (`ownership`). Notes are UI copy: U.S. spelling ("authorized"), no em-dashes, each claiming only what its one evidence link shows (a sponsor note naming a rename: with the rename's evidence as a second link) (the current-owner step first: I.D.B. Radiopharmacy's link is the Dutch regulator's 2024 page, Advil's GSK's 2022 introduction of Haleon); evidence quotes stay verbatim; the build checks every member note has an https evidence URL and every member evidence URL a note. Joint ventures are their own group only while live (user decision 2026-09-28; each still holds an authorised medicine: Bristol-Myers Squibb / Pfizer EEIG (Eliquis), MCM Vaccine (Vaxelis), Vifor Fresenius Medical Care Renal Pharma (Velphoro, Veltassa)), `joint_venture` with both partners: `partners` (group keys) and `other_partners` (names of partners without a group: Fresenius Medical Care); the run summary warns about a joint-venture group without an authorised medicine. Dissolved ones are no group: each medicine goes to the partner that took it, by a per-medicine row with evidence (Sanofi Pasteur MSD, ended 2016: Procomvax → MSD, Hexavac → Sanofi; the Sanofi/BMS alliance, unwound 2013: DuoPlavin → Sanofi, as are Bristol-Myers Squibb Pharma EEIG's own copies of its clopidogrel and irbesartan: Clopidogrel BMS, Irbesartan BMS, Irbesartan Hydrochlorothiazide BMS, DuoCover), the joint venture staying the holder; a medicine without evidence stays with the joint venture's company, now its own group (Primavax, user decision 2026-09-28: it stays its own company, as no evidence names a partner; EMA's withdrawal statement names only the joint venture, its EPAR was removed and the Union Register has no annexes, so no manufacturer is known); Pharmacia - Pfizer EEIG is Pfizer's alone since 2003. Every other company is its own group (monogram derived, `monogram_source` `derived` as against a curated group's `curated`: only a curated monogram names a group people search for (search item #4, 2026-09-28: "CAR", "TB", "ALL" are Carisma's, Theravance's and Allos's derived ones); initials of its brand words (Laboratorios, Pharma, The… skipped), then letters, unique; never a code or word that reads as something else (`monogram_blocklist`: EU, US, THE, SOB…) or the first three letters of a curated group's name ("NOV")); a company that is not a curated member but whose own group key would be a curated group's stops the build, listing it with its medicines (add it to the members or aliases). A holder's company goes to a curated group also when an applied GLEIF match's ultimate parent is that group's LEI (and neither registration has lapsed). **Holder used**: the Union Register decides when its holder (linked product, Human rows only) names another company than EMA's (after folding and curated aliases: transfers, renames such as Mylan → Viatris; not a misspelling such as "Gentium S.r.I." or another entity of the same company) and, user decision 2026-09-28, the register lists the product as Active (only "Active" is in force, as in the register status table): a product no longer active can name an older holder (Ecokinase's Galenus Mannheim) or a newer one (Ioa's MSD), so EMA's field decides there (register-decided medicines 56 → 37 on 2026-09-28, as the run summary counts them: every medicine with a `holder_register`, Zokinvy's included, though its sponsor row decides its company; the run summary counts the register holders of another company not used, 16; EMA's older spellings got aliases: "Medac", "Takeda Europe R Centre Ltd.", "Mitsubishi Pharma Europe Ltd", and "Pharmacia - Pfizer EEIG" is a Pfizer member); without an EMA holder the register's name is the only one and is used whatever its status (Zabdeno, Mvabea); EMA's name stays with every medicine (`holder_ema`); a register "name" that is an address is ignored. Country: ISO code from the register holder address's last part, never the address; left out where the register names another company that does not decide. **Regulatory representatives** (FGK, Norpharm, YES, ERA, CATS, TMC, Integral Pharma Solutions, Sciencepharma, …: `curated_representatives()`) stay "held via a regulatory representative" until `curated_medicine_sponsors()` names the sponsor per medicine (note, evidence URL, quote ≤ 20 words, checked date); only rows with `reviewed` TRUE apply. User decision 2026-09-28 ("I confirm the medicine sponsor list"): 22 rows from the review list, each checked against the holder used (the Union Register's where it decides: Zokinvy's Integral Pharma Solutions), the sponsor by its own name then (AcelRx), while a renamed sponsor shows its current name (user decision 2026-09-28: `curated_sponsor_renames()`, aliases from the old name with an https evidence URL, a verbatim quote ≤ 20 words and a checked date; the sponsor note names both: AcelRx Pharmaceuticals → Talphera (2024), Sesen Bio → Carisma Therapeutics (2023), Discovery Laboratories → Windtree Therapeutics (2016), Cempra Pharmaceuticals → Melinta Therapeutics (2017), Cleveland BioLabs → Statera Biopharma (2021), and, user decision 2026-09-28, Advaxis, Inc. → Ayala Pharmaceuticals (2023, merging with Ayala; SEC 8-K) and Coherus BioSciences → Coherus Oncology (2025, the current SEC filer name; SEC 8-K), whose notes (Raligize, Udenyca) name the rename too, Udenyca's Accord/Intas medicine note unchanged; the old name and the rename's evidence ship in its group's `ownership`, so its company page shows them and the search finds it by the old name, and the rename's evidence also on each of its medicines (`sponsor_rename_evidence_url`), so the medicine card links it after the sponsor note), its group a curated one where the current owner is (Avanir → Otsuka, Sentynl → Zydus) or else its own (Henlius for Bildyos and Bilprevda, not its licensee Organon; Bio-Thera for Avzivi, not Sandoz); Kamada and Genta (address tails) and Clopidogrel Krka (the register decides) need none; 5 stay held via a representative (Memantine FGK, Sildenafil FGK, Mevlyq, Ivabradine JensonR, Ablavar: unresolved). **GLEIF** (CC0): matched on demand by `scripts/match-gleif.R` into the committed `data-raw/gleif-holder-matches.json` (no GLEIF requests in CI); the pipeline applies a match only when `reviewed`, or by rule (`is_auto_gleif_match()`, not by score alone, as shared words add to it): the exact name of an active entity but not a former name of a lapsed record, or the same name without legal form of an active entity in the holder's country, at confidence ≥ 0.95; and only when it does not contradict the curated group (its top entity is another curated group's, or the group's known LEI/parent is not it); a lapsed registration's parent is not shown. Unreviewed stem/sibling matches are never applied. The build fails, listing offenders, on a medicine with a holder but no company or group, duplicate or non-id-safe keys, duplicate monograms, a group row in `companies.json` without medicines (curated groups without medicines are left out of it, with a warning), unknown partner groups (or groups of a company, medicine or per-medicine row), a joint venture without two partners, a company in two curated groups, a holder name in two companies, an own group key that is a curated group's, chained aliases, malformed alias evidence, a member note without an https evidence URL (or a URL without a note), a malformed sponsor, per-medicine group or medicine note row, a medicine with both a per-medicine group row and a note, or a Union Register status it does not know; stale curated rows, medicine notes naming no medicine in the data and redundant per-medicine rows only warn.
- **Modality** (M2 phase 2; spec `.remember/modality/modality-spec.md` 2026-09-29, with `rules.md` and `verification.md`; owner-side decisions on its §13, 2026-09-29: Peptide inside "Protein and peptide"; the peptide explainer without a 40-amino-acid limit; designed amino-acid chains are Peptide, natural-product peptides and peptidomimetics Small molecule; sugammadex and porfimer Small molecule, Polymer kept; function over fusion, romiplostim Fusion protein; no radiopharmaceutical carrier line; no new modalities (ferumoxytol, iron oxide, microbiota, cancer vaccines, PEG-drug conjugates stay Not classified); efgartigimod alfa Other protein, lerodalcibep Fusion protein; glioma cells and lysates Not classified; ChEMBL small molecules without a structure applied and listed; static rows count-only; `R/modality.R`, `R/curated-modalities.R`, `R/chembl-molecules.R`): what kind of medicine each active substance is, as 8 groups and 30 modalities (`modality_taxonomy()`, `modalities.json`; `small_molecule` is a group without modalities whose rows carry modality `small_molecule`, so modality null means group only). Every value names its `source` and `rule`; what no source states stays "not classified", never guessed. **Inputs** per medicine (`modality_inputs()`), the first that yields: its INN keys (EMA's INN-field slip, Vysribli, is already the active substance in `build_substances_table()`); EMA's active-substance texts, lower-case (R1); the keys of a medicine with the same name (`normalise_medicine_name()`: Ogivri, Graspa, Cerepro; R3); the WHO name(s) of its one level-5 ATC code, split on ", " and " and ", never a J07 vaccine's (its name names disease and kind: the vaccine class tells that; Kyndrisa → drisapersen, Cokiera → 4 keys; 7 products; R2); else nothing (product-level sources only). **Tiers**, highest first: T0 reviewed curated rows; T1 a radionuclide in the name (`[(\[]\d{1,3}m?[a-z]{1,2}[)\]]`, "ra 223"); T2 ATC V09/V10; T3 definitive WHO INN stems and schemes (`-mab`/`-tug`/`-bart` word 1, `-ment`, `-mig`, a payload second word `-dotin`/`-tansine`/`-tecan`/`-micin`/`-tox`/tesirine/duocarmazine → ADC, a chelator second word → antibody without modality; `-cabtagene` + `-cel` → CAR-T, `-gene` + `-cel` → gene-modified cells, `-gene` + `-vec`/`-plasmid`/`-bac` → gene therapy, one-word `-cel` → other cell therapy; `-meran`, `-siran`, `-rsen`; `-bep`, `-cog`, `-ase` (≤ 3 words, not "kinase", "inhibitor", "gene"), `-poetin`/`-stim`/`-ermin`/`-kin`/`-tropin`/`som-` and the INN groups insulin, (peg/ropeg)interferon → hormone, cytokine or growth factor; `-cept`/`-fusp` after them (function over fusion); `-parin(ux)`, `-mer`, `-motide`/`-vatein` (vaccines), `-rev` (group only); a Greek-letter second word → protein, group only; stems only on INN-like keys (≤ 4 words, no "vaccine"); infix stems never alone (`-apt-`)), EMA's `advanced_therapy` flag (group only) and "mrna" in EMA's text; T4 checked stems (`-tide`, `-relin`, `-relix`, `-pressin`, `-tocin`; `-irudin`, `-kinra`, `-cogin` group only), which lose to a ChEMBL "Small molecule"; defibrotide is never `-tide`, and a `-tide` with a large partner (a ChEMBL protein chain over 100 amino acids, or "fc"/"albumin" in the text) is a fusion protein (R11); T5 ATC classes WHO defines by product type (J07 with the kind from its WHO names, J06A/J06BA/J06BB/L04AA03/L04AA04 polyclonal immunoglobulin, V01 allergen, B02BD/B02BC06/B02BC10 coagulation factor, C10AC polymer, A09AA enzyme; R9) and EMA text naming a vaccine (kind: vector ad26./chadox1/mva-bn/rvsv first, then live, then inactivated words) or an immunoglobulin (not monoclonal); T6 ChEMBL `molecule_type` (applied matches: exact, or `reviewed`; "different_molecule_types" only when reviewed; a salt ChEMBL types Unknown takes its parent's type, rule "(parent)"), with the guards: a "Protein" with a MOL/BOTH structure and no peptide stem proposes nothing (R6), an "Oligosaccharide" without a heparin stem is a small molecule (R7; rule "chembl:Oligosaccharide (no heparin stem: small molecule)", Sugammadex Lorien's "sugammadex sodium"), a "Small molecule" without a structure is applied and listed (R8); T7 EMA substance-text hints (extract, enzymes, plasma proteins, toxin, hormone, monoclonal antibody, cell descriptions with the cell kind, gene + vector); T8 ATC classes by use (L01F/L04AG/J06BC/J06BD antibody, L01XL cell and gene, A10A/L03AA-AC/H01AC/B03XA01-03 hormone, A16AB/B01AD/M09AB/D03BA enzyme, H01BA/BB/CA/CB/CC/L02AE/L02BX02/H04AA/H05BA peptide, B01AB heparin; never L04AB, L04AC, L04AJ or B03XA as a whole) and the pharmacotherapeutic group text. **Proposals** (`modality_proposals()`: one helper per tier, concatenated highest first: `radiopharmaceutical_proposals()` T1/T2, `inn_scheme_proposals()` T3, `checked_stem_proposals()` T4, `definitional_proposals()` T5, `chembl_proposal()` T6, `text_hint_proposals()` T7, weak ATC classes and `group_text_proposals()` T8; `decide_modality()` keeps the list order within a tier, so the order inside a helper decides ties). **Combine** (`decide_modality()`): the highest tier's group (a T4 top loses to a T6 small molecule); the modality of the highest proposal in that group that names one (`leaf_source`/`leaf_rule` when not the group's own proposal); proposals of other groups are conflicts, listed in the run summary, never shown. **Radiopharmaceuticals** (R4): a T1/T2 decision applies to every medicine with that key (Scintimun 000653 follows 001045; rule "… (same substance as …)"); diagnostic or therapeutic by V09/V10, else the pharmacotherapeutic group text; a precursor ("<element> (nuclide) chloride", a generator) with a code shallower than level 3 by its nuclide (90Y, 177Lu, 223Ra, 153Sm therapeutic; 18F, 68Ga, 68Ge, 64Cu, 99mTc, 111In, 123I, 89Zr diagnostic: Theryttrex, Ytracis). **Several substances**: each classified; a medicine counts once per group and once per modality (children need not add up). **Curated rows** (`curated_modalities()`: `substance_key`, `ema_product_number` (NA: every medicine with the key), `modality_group`, `modality`, https `evidence_url`, verbatim `quote` ≤ 20 words, `checked_date`, `reviewed`, `note`; every block sets `checked_date` and `reviewed` itself, no defaults, so a row without them stops the build): 85, all reviewed: 44 key rows and 1 product row (Scintimun 000653) whose evidence verification.md checked (bispecifics and fragments with old `-mab` names, tisagenlecleucel, dulaglutide, albiglutide, defibrotide, imetelstat, pegaptanib, polymers, EMA spelling slips…), 12 policy rows (the decisions above), and 28 fills quoted on 2026-09-29 from the EMA document named (product information, EPAR, scientific discussion, withdrawal assessment report or Q&A, CHMP summary of opinion; fetched ≥ 20 s apart, cached in `.cache/downloads/ema-modality-evidence/`): the spec's 26, lerodalcibep and birch bark extract (ChEMBL types it "Small molecule" through its WHO ATC name, "betulae cortex"). Rotashield's fill is a product row (EMEA/H/C/000192, so a later "rotavirus vaccine", an inactivated one say, does not inherit "live"), quoted from the PubMed abstract of the ACIP 1999 recommendations (PMID 10219046: "an oral, live, tetravalent, rhesus-based rotavirus vaccine (RRV-TV)"), as EMA removed its EPAR and its medicine page names the one rhesus and three reassortant serotypes (RRV-TV) but not "live". Reviewed ChEMBL variants in the matches file: formoterol (fumarate) → FORMOTEROL, plazomicin (sulfate) → PLAZOMICIN. **Build stops** (`cli::cli_abort()` listing offenders): a modality outside its group or an unknown key, an advanced therapy outside cell and gene therapy, a biosimilar classed small molecule, a malformed curated row (key not a substance key, unknown or misplaced modality, no https URL, no date, no review status, reviewed without quote) or a duplicate (key, product). **Run summary**: medicines per group and modality (all, Authorised); medicines with an unclassified substance (Authorised first); group only; namesake and ATC-name inputs; conflicts; review lists (R6, R8, ambiguous matches, variant candidates); keys missing from the ChEMBL file (new medicines: the other tiers classify them meanwhile); curated rows used, those the rules already agree with (candidates to drop), those matching no medicine. On 2026-09-28: 2,351 medicines, 2,632 rows; small molecule 1,416 (968 Authorised), protein and peptide 350 (233), antibody 312 (238), vaccine 101 (54), cell and gene therapy 44 (24), radiopharmaceutical 40 (24), nucleic acid 30 (21), other 19 (11); not classified 40 (0 Authorised: the 33 withdrawn applications without substance data, Rienso, Feraheme, Sinerem, Onzeald, Lympreva, Sitoiganap, Xervyteg); group only 1 (Emerflu, ATC J07BB); 83 conflicts. The spec's expected counts (§12) hold but for lerodalcibep (Lyrokaul, Opinion), Fusion protein by the owner's decision (fusion 34, other protein 16). The daily pipeline makes no ChEMBL molecule requests: it reads the committed matches file (see Downloads).
- **Pivotal results** (owner decisions 2026-09-30; spec `.remember/efficacy/efficacy-spec.md`, pilot `.remember/efficacy/pilot-report.md` (NSCLC, 18 medicines, 87 verified rows); build rulings R1–R16 in `.superpowers/sdd/efficacy-plan/progress.md`; `R/efficacy*.R`, `R/claude-api.R`): per medicine and indication, the pivotal trials' efficacy results as the product information prints them, so a card says what a medicine achieved; no claim across medicines (the condition benchmark is phase C). **Source**: SmPC section 5.1 of the current product information PDF (`slice_smpc_efficacy()` in `R/efficacy-section.R`: from the "5.1 Pharmacodynamic properties" heading, as some SmPCs put results under pharmacodynamic effects (R5), to 5.2 of the first SmPC, with the PDF page it starts on (R7)); the EPAR's benefit-risk section is phase B. **Extractor** (on demand, `scripts/extract-efficacy.R`, never in CI or the daily pipeline): the Claude API over httr2 (`R/claude-api.R`, no SDK; key `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in `.Renviron`), one Message Batch per run (half price, no request timeout; its id shown as soon as it exists and saved in `.cache/downloads/ema-efficacy/pending-batch.json` (written to a `.tmp` file, then renamed over it) before polling, so a stopped run's next run collects it instead of paying again; a failed save warns with the id, and the run still collects it; a resumed batch reads its sections from the text cache only, never EMA, and stops before collecting, keeping the pending file, when a product's cached text is missing), `max_tokens` 32000 (R8), a strict JSON schema without union types (absent text "", counts and CI level as printed strings parsed in R, `is_primary` yes / no / not_stated; R6), 1–3 verbatim quotes per row; default model `claude-sonnet-5-5` (`APPROVAL_ATLAS_EFFICACY_MODEL`), to be confirmed by A0 (`scripts/eval-efficacy-gold.R`, `R/efficacy-gold.R`: a model's rows scored against the pilot's 78 hand-verified SmPC rows, one batch per model; accepted with 0 numeric errors among rows kept, ≥ 95% agreement on the lead row per indication, ≤ 5% missed rows, every call ok and no row lost to parsing (R14), and the pilot's pitfalls caught: ALEX column order, IMpower110 primary vs tabulated, MARIPOSA-2 significance (R15), Lumykras range). **Verification** (`verify_efficacy_row()` in `R/efficacy-verify.R`, pure, a port of the pilot's `verify.py`), blocking (a row failing it is dropped, counted as failed): a value (no value, no row); every quote occurs in the section text (its layout or flow rendering, R1; whitespace and page furniture normalised); every number of the row (value, CI, p-value, arm values) occurs in a quote, never as part of a longer number (nor three digits as the end of "1,234"); the value and its CI in one quote; `comparator_column_label` (case aside) in the same quote as `arm_control` when the row has both (the ALEX guard against arms read from the wrong column; a swap within one quote that holds both columns is not caught here, the gold evaluation's ALEX pitfall tests it); the trial name in the section, each "+" part and each one-word bracketed part (the registry number) as a whole word ("KEYNOTE-02" is not in "KEYNOTE-024"; bracketed phrases are not checked, as the pilot's gold rows describe trials there); the arm sizes as "n=" text (a thousands comma allowed). What is not checked verbatim, only looked up: population, regimen, comparator, comparator column label, endpoint and assessment (case and spacing aside) in the section, the indication in section 4.1, the CI level as "95%" or "95 %" in a quote or the section, the arm sizes in the quotes; each miss is a flag, not a block, as the pilot's gold rows paraphrase (on 2026-09-30 its 87 rows all pass the blocking checks, and 72 have text not in the section, 73 arm sizes outside the quotes). **Flags** (`efficacy_flags()` in `R/efficacy-rows.R`, with the verifier's own: the row is kept as `review` `flagged`, hidden until a human decides): numbers reassembled across wrapped table cells, a CI level other than 95%, `is_primary` not stated, a population broader than or other than the indication (a matching subgroup is not flagged), a range printed like a CI, "NR" / "NE", a single-arm row with an HR, a comparator without its column label, no page, and the look-ups above: `text_not_in_source`, `indication_not_in_source`, `ci_level_not_in_source`, `n_not_in_quotes`. The prompt asks for population, regimen and comparator verbatim and for the arm sizes in the quotes. **Reviews**: `auto_ok` (no flag) and `reviewed_ok` are shown, `flagged` and `reviewed_rejected` never; `scripts/efficacy-review-sample.R` lists every flagged row and a 10% sample of the `auto_ok` ones (in turns across products, seeded by the date) in `.remember/efficacy/review-{date}.md` (gitignored), each with every field the card shows (indication, trial, population and its match, regimen vs comparator with n, the comparator's column label, arms and measure, endpoint, assessment, primary or not, analysis role and text, CI level, the effect as worded), its flags, source page and quotes, and the decisions go into `data-raw/efficacy-rows.json`; a rerun keeps `reviewed_ok` only while a hash of every model field is unchanged (R10) and keeps `reviewed_rejected` rows as tombstones, so the same row found again comes back rejected; the row key includes assessment, effect type, indication and comparator (R11). Only transient batch failures are retried (an `api_error` or `overloaded_error`, an error without a type, an expired or canceled request, no result in the batch); a deterministic one (another errored type, such as `invalid_request_error`, a truncated, refused or invalid answer, no row verified) waits for a changed product information, another model or `APPROVAL_ATLAS_EFFICACY_ONLY` (R9). **Pipeline step** (`R/efficacy.R`, daily, no API call): reads the committed rows and stops the build on malformed ones (`check_efficacy_rows()`, `cli::cli_abort()` listing them: an unknown review, effect type, population match or analysis role, a malformed product number, no quote, a source URL not https, no source date, model, row key or extraction date, a repeated row key); `build_efficacy_table()` keeps the shown rows of medicines in the data, marks `stale` the rows extracted from a product information older than the medicine's current one (they stay until it is extracted again) and writes `ema_medicine_efficacy.json` (the file is written empty until the first extraction), with, when it has rows, a `meta.json` source entry (`efficacy_source_entry()` of the site table, so the shown rows only, none when every row is hidden: "Pivotal results (extracted from EMA product information SmPC section 5.1 with Claude)", the models and the latest extraction date); the run summary counts medicines, rows shown, flagged, rejected and stale. **Lead row** per product and indication (`choose_lead_rows()`, tested): among the shown rows of a primary endpoint (else of the SmPC's first trial for that indication), the pre-specified primary analysis, then the population that matches best (`subgroup_matches` over `whole_trial_matches` over `whole_trial_broader`), then the SmPC's order; so a single-arm trial leads with its primary response rate. **Owner decisions 2026-09-30**: first release a "Pivotal results" section on every medicine card, per indication, the condition benchmark later; the pre-specified primary analysis leads, later and exploratory analyses under "More results"; the subgroup matching the EU indication leads, flagged "subgroup (matches EU indication)", the whole trial under "More results"; rows that pass the checks show, labelled "Extracted automatically from the product information; check the source.", flagged ones stay hidden until reviewed by hand, with a 10% spot check per run; a one-line teaser in the Indication block plus the section in More details, the document buttons staying on the first phone screen; the extractor runs on the owner's machine with the owner's key. Rules: every number and trial name is verbatim in the source (blocking), population, regimen, comparator, endpoint, assessment and indication are looked up in it (a miss hides the row until reviewed), and what it does not state stays null; results are never ranked or coloured across medicines, and a CI excluding 1 is never called significant unless the source says so (`significance_stated` holds its words; the card does not show it). Before A3 ships widely: a legal review of the block (decision support? MDCG 2019-11).
- **Internal R package at repo root** (DESCRIPTION + one-line NAMESPACE, never built). `devtools::check()` is deliberately not a gate.
- **Hosting: GitHub Pages at approval-atlas.vrognas.com** — the repo is public (since creation; GitHub Free serves Pages only from public repos) and CI's `deploy` job publishes the site on every run on `main`. Needed for phone use. Outward changes (visibility, repo/Pages settings, DNS) only with the user's explicit OK. See "Trust baseline" and "Hosting".
- **Data committed ("git scraping")**: `site/public/data/` is tracked. CI's `commit-data` job commits it on scheduled/manual runs when anything other than `meta.json` and `ema_authorized_series.json` changed (both change daily with the snapshot date; they ride along with real changes). Gives a history of EMA changes and keeps the schedule active (60-day rule). Data files are regenerated by CI: after local pipeline runs, don't commit data by hand (`git restore site/public/data`) unless intended.

## Conventions
- **U.S. spelling in the UI.** Raw EMA values stay unchanged in data files; `site/src/labels.js` is the single map from raw values and UI copy to display text. A test rejects "authoris…" in UI text (function bodies included, so EMA field names such as `marketing_authorisation_date` belong in other modules, e.g. `statusDate()` in `approvals.js`).
- **No em-dashes in UI copy** (user decision 2026-09-27): commas, colons or parentheses instead; en-dash ranges ("1995–2026") stay. A test rejects U+2014 in `labels.js` values and `index.html`.
- **CSP-safe styling.** No `style=""` in HTML and no `.attr("style", …)`: CSS classes, or d3 `.style()` (CSSOM) for computed values such as bar widths.
- **Provenance**: every enriched value (ATC, therapeutic-area branch, company and group, modality (with its `rule`), ATC class explanation; later events) has a `source` column (`companies.json`: a `sources` list per row).
- **Caching**: external downloads live in `.cache/` (gitignored); per-source files in `.cache/downloads/<source>/`, refetched only when the source changes (MeSH: Last-Modified; ChEMBL: release). EMA medicines JSON: 24 h. Requests are throttled per host (`R/http.R`: `req_throttle(capacity = 1, …)`, no retry on 429).
- **Licensing**: code MIT. Data files: compilation CC BY-SA 4.0 (ChEMBL-derived values adapted from CC BY-SA 3.0); third-party values keep their own terms (see README; GLEIF LEI data CC0, credited anyway, never implying GLEIF provides the site). Our own texts (the ATC class explanations, curated notes) are part of the compilation; WHO's ATC codes and names are not, and the credits say which is whose. Credit every source in the footer (every third-party source in its visible first line, with the licence links CC BY 4.0 and CC BY-SA 3.0 require; see "Footer and About") and README.

## Data contract (`site/public/data/`)
`NOTICE.txt` (legal review 2026-09-30) is the one file not written by the pipeline: static text, committed by hand and never loaded by the site, with EMA's acknowledgement (its terms want it in every copy) and every source's credit and licence for anyone downloading the JSON files. The pipeline only writes its own files there, and the build uploads the whole folder as the data artifact, so `commit-data` (which mirrors that artifact) keeps it; Vite copies it to `site/dist/data/`. Update it when a source or licence changes (2026-09-30: the pivotal results' credit, extracted from EMA product information with Claude (Anthropic), not checked by EMA or Anthropic).
JSON arrays of row objects; dates `"YYYY-MM-DD"` or `null`; flags `true/false`; missing `null` (never `""`); ids/codes strings; rows sorted (by `ema_product_number`, lookups then by value) so identical input gives identical output. Tables are written one record per line (`[`, rows joined by `,\n`, `]`), UTF-8, LF on every OS, so git diffs show changed rows; `meta.json` is pretty-printed.
- `ema_medicines.json` — one row per Human medicine; EMA columns minus `category`, the 3 vet-only columns and the 3 split fields; derived: `medicine_type` (Advanced therapy > Biosimilar > Generic > Other), `authorized_from`, `authorized_until`, `series_exclusion`, `substance_set_key`.
- `ema_medicine_therapeutic_areas.json` — `ema_product_number`, `therapeutic_area_mesh`.
- `ema_therapeutic_area_branches.json` — `therapeutic_area_mesh`, `mesh_descriptor_ui`, `mesh_descriptor_name`, `branch`, `branch_name`, `source`.
- `ema_therapeutic_area_subtree.json` (sub-areas; one row per term and level-2/3 tree node, distinct; sorted by term, then node) — `therapeutic_area_mesh`, `branch`, `node` (tree number), `level` (2 | 3), `parent` (the branch or level-2 node), `node_name` (descriptor name; null if MeSH has no record), `source` (the term's match source).
- `ema_medicine_active_substances.json` — `ema_product_number`, `active_substance` (EMA field).
- `ema_medicine_substances.json` — `ema_product_number`, `substance`, `substance_key` (from the INN field; from the `active_substance` field where the INN field repeats the medicine's name: Vysribli, step 3 review).
- `ema_medicine_atc_codes.json` (one row per product and EMA code; since phase 4d also one row per distinct SmPC code of a product without an EMA code (since phase 4e, when its SmPC gives none, per curated code; a curated code also replaces an SmPC code it completes when none of the product's SmPC codes is complete); SmPC codes not used stay in `ema_medicine_smpc_atc.json`; sorted by product, EMA code (null last), code) — `ema_product_number`, `atc_code_human`, `atc_level`, `atc_incomplete`, `source` as published (`source` "ema"; "Not yet assigned" dropped), or for a product without an EMA code `atc_code_human` null, `atc_level` null, `atc_incomplete` false and `source` "ema_smpc" (or "curated") (`atc_level` and `atc_incomplete` describe `atc_code_human` only; an SmPC-only code can be level 1–4); `atc_code` (the code to use, never null), `atc_code_source` (`ema` | `ema_smpc` | `curated`), `atc_code_conflict` (true when an SmPC or curated code replaced an incomplete EMA code it does not fit, else false), `atc_final_level` (phase 4e: true when `atc_code` is complete: level 5, or a level-4 code WHO no longer subdivides (B03AC); whether a code is incomplete is `!atc_final_level`), `atc_code_document_url`, `atc_code_document_date` (the product information used; for `curated` the evidence page (ATC/DDD Index page, temporary list or product information PDF) and the date it was checked; else null), `current_atc_code` (the code a retired `atc_code` moved to, else null), `current_atc_code_source` (`whocc_alterations` | `curated` | null). Row kinds (2026-09-28: 1,976 EMA + 126 completed and 5 conflicts from SmPCs + 100 completed and 9 conflicts from curated codes + 16 SmPC only = 2,232 rows): EMA's code `{"atc_code_human":"L04AB02","atc_level":5,"atc_incomplete":false,"source":"ema","atc_code":"L04AB02","atc_code_source":"ema","atc_code_conflict":false,"atc_final_level":true,…}`; completed (Aucatzyl) `{"atc_code_human":"L01XL","atc_level":4,"atc_incomplete":true,"source":"ema","atc_code":"L01XL12","atc_code_source":"ema_smpc","atc_code_conflict":false,"atc_final_level":true,"atc_code_document_url":"https://…aucatzyl-epar-product-information_en.pdf",…}`; conflict (Kerendia) `{"atc_code_human":"C09","atc_level":2,"atc_incomplete":true,"source":"ema","atc_code":"C03DA05","atc_code_source":"ema_smpc","atc_code_conflict":true,…}`; SmPC only (Entyvio) `{"atc_code_human":null,"atc_level":null,"atc_incomplete":false,"source":"ema_smpc","atc_code":"L04AG05","atc_code_source":"ema_smpc","atc_code_conflict":false,…}`; curated, temporary code (Tryngolza) `{"atc_code_human":"C10AX","atc_level":4,"atc_incomplete":true,"source":"ema","atc_code":"C10AX21","atc_code_source":"curated","atc_code_conflict":false,"atc_final_level":true,"atc_code_document_url":"https://atcddd.fhi.no/filearchive/documents/temporary_atc_and_ddd.xlsx","atc_code_document_date":"2026-09-27",…}`; curated conflict (Aduhelm) `{"atc_code_human":"N07",…,"atc_code":"N06DX03","atc_code_source":"curated","atc_code_conflict":true,"atc_final_level":true,"atc_code_document_url":"https://atcddd.fhi.no/atc_ddd_index/?code=N06DX&showdescription=no",…}`; complete at level 4 (Rienso) `{"atc_code_human":"B03","atc_level":2,"atc_incomplete":true,"source":"ema","atc_code":"B03AC","atc_code_source":"curated","atc_code_conflict":false,"atc_final_level":true,…}` and (Feraheme) `{"atc_code_human":"B03AC","atc_level":4,"atc_incomplete":true,"source":"ema","atc_code":"B03AC","atc_code_source":"ema","atc_final_level":true,…}`.
- `atc_classes.json` — `atc_code`, `level`, `name` (verbatim), `source` (`chembl_atc_class` | `whocc_updates` (a yearly list: new codes and name alterations) | `whocc_index` (index pages, and the curated WHO names) | `whocc_alterations` (old level-5 names) | `whocc_index_archived`), `status` (`current` | `retired` | `temporary`), `replaced_by` (current code of a retired one; null when deleted), `changed_year`, `status_source` (where a retired code's `status`, `replaced_by` and `changed_year` come from: `whocc_alterations` | `curated`; null otherwise; `source` names where the name comes from, e.g. ChEMBL for a code ChEMBL still lists), `source_url` (null for ChEMBL). WHOCC rows only for codes (and their prefixes) the data uses that ChEMBL lacks.
- `atc_class_explanations.json` (owner decisions 2026-09-29; see Architecture "ATC class explanations": the explanations of the classes at levels 1–4 the data uses, one row per class, sorted by `atc_code`; 543 on 2026-09-29) — `atc_code`, `level` (1–4), `explanation` (our own text: at most 20 words, no em-dash, ending with a period (a node test checks it: a tip's two lines read apart as one description), U.S. spelling), `checked_date`, `source` = `"approval_atlas"`. WHO's name is in `atc_classes.json`. Example: `{"atc_code":"L04AC","level":4,"explanation":"Block interleukins, immune messenger proteins that drive inflammation, or their receptors, calming overactive immune responses.","checked_date":"2026-09-29","source":"approval_atlas"}`.
  - Committed input, not a site file: `data-raw/atc-class-explanations.json` (one record per line, sorted by `code`; 543 rows on 2026-09-29, every class the data used then) — `code`, `level`, `explanation`, `checked_date`, `source` ("Approval Atlas (plain-language summary)"); WHO's name left out. The test fixture `tests/testthat/fixtures/atc-class-explanations-sample.json` holds its rows for the fixture EMA data's 46 classes.
- `ema_medicine_smpc_atc.json` (SmPC checks: provenance, and the resume state CI commits; sorted by product, code) — `ema_product_number`, `document_url`, `document_last_updated_date`, `checked_date`, `reader_version` (phase 4e: the SmPC reader that made the check, `smpc_reader_version` in `R/smpc-atc.R`; checks written before versions read as 1), `smpc_status` (`code_found` | `incomplete` | `not_assigned` | `no_code` (no "ATC code" statement) | `no_text` | `not_found`), `atc_code` (one row per distinct code; null when none), `source` = `"ema_smpc"`. Codes as the SmPC gives them; validation against EMA's code happens at build time.
- `ema_authorized_series.json` — `date` (month-ends from 1995-01-31, then the snapshot date), `authorized_products`, `authorized_substances`.
- `meta.json` — `source_url`, `source_timestamp` (EMA verbatim), `snapshot_date`, `row_counts`, `attribution`, `opinion_to_decision` (how long the EC takes to decide after a positive CHMP opinion, from EMA's dates, `summarise_opinion_to_decision()` in `R/authorization.R`: days from `opinion_adopted_date` to `marketing_authorisation_date` of every medicine that received an authorisation (current or ended; not the never-authorized statuses), for opinions adopted from `opinions_from` (the snapshot date less 5 years) on; an opinion dated after the authorisation is a later one and left out; pending opinions have no decision yet, so they are not counted. `median_days`, `p90_days` (90th percentile; both rounded to whole days, R's default quantile; null without any medicine), `medicines` (how many were counted), `opinions_from`; 2026-09-28: median 57, p90 69, 439 medicines), `sources` (name, url, version, retrieved, licence, attribution; after the ATC sources (the WHOCC lists, the ATC/DDD Index, the product information checks) "ATC class explanations" (`atc_explanations_source_entry()`: version "Checked {latest checked_date}", licence CC BY-SA 4.0, our own texts; when any is in the data); then the company entries: Union Register holders, GLEIF with the matching run's date, curated company groups with their as-of date; then the curated copies, "Checked {date}", when any is in the data, and likewise the curated paediatric-use marketing authorisations ("EMA paediatric-use marketing authorisations (PUMAs; …)"); last the modality entries: "WHO INN stems" (the WHO documents the rules read, retrieved the date the rules were checked), "ChEMBL molecules" (the matches file's release, and its oldest response as `retrieved`) and "Curated modalities" ("Checked {date}"); then, once there are pivotal results, "Pivotal results (extracted from EMA product information SmPC section 5.1 with Claude)" (`efficacy_source_entry()`: version "Extracted {date}; models …"), which About's sources read as `efficacy`), `licence` (`data_licence`: it also says the ATC class explanations are our own summaries, not WHO's, and part of the licence).
- Modality (see Architecture "Modality"):
  - `ema_medicine_modalities.json` (one row per medicine and classified text; a medicine without any: one row, `substance_key` null; sorted by `ema_product_number`, `substance_key` (null last)) — `ema_product_number`, `substance_key` (the text classified: INN key, active-substance text, namesake key or WHO ATC name, lower-case; else null), `substance_basis` (`inn` | `active_substance` | `namesake` | `atc_name` | `none`), `modality_group` (a group key; null = not classified), `modality` (a modality key, `small_molecule` for small molecules; null = group only or not classified), `source` (`curated` | `inn_stem` | `ema_atmp` | `ema_text` | `atc` | `chembl`; null when not classified), `rule` (`stem:-siran`, `stem:-mab + payload vedotin`, `stem:-mab + chelator tiuxetan`, `stem:-gene + -vec`, `stem:-tide + large partner`, `inn_group:insulin`, `inn:greek second word (alfa)`, `inn:radionuclide (177lu)`, `atc:V09IA01` (a product's matching codes joined by ","; "… (same substance as EMEA/H/C/001045)" when taken from another medicine with the key), `chembl:Small molecule` ("chembl:… (parent)" for a salt typed by its parent; `chembl:Oligosaccharide (no heparin stem: small molecule)` for guard R7), `text:vaccine live`, `text:mrna`, `text:cells`, `pg:vaccines`, `ema_atmp:advanced_therapy`, `curated`), `evidence` (ChEMBL ID and release, "CHEMBL941 ChEMBL_37"; the first matching ATC code; a curated row's `evidence_url`; for a namesake or ATC-name key whose decision has none of its own, the namesake's product number or the ATC code; else null: stems and texts are named by the rule), `leaf_source`, `leaf_rule` (when the modality came from another proposal than the group's, e.g. `atc` / `atc:V10` for a radionuclide's use, `ema_text` / `text:cells` under EMA's ATMP flag; else null). Example (Leqvio): `{"ema_product_number":"EMEA/H/C/005333","substance_key":"inclisiran","substance_basis":"inn","modality_group":"nucleic_acid","modality":"sirna","source":"inn_stem","rule":"stem:-siran","evidence":null,"leaf_source":null,"leaf_rule":null}`.
  - `modalities.json` (the taxonomy in tree order) — `key`, `kind` (`group` | `modality`), `group_key` (null for groups), `order`. Names and explainers are UI copy (`labels.js`).
  - Committed input, not a site file: `data-raw/chembl-substance-matches.json` (one row per key the pipeline classifies, sorted by key; ChEMBL_37, responses of 2026-09-28; 1,324 keys on 2026-09-28, 1,212 with a molecule, 2 of them reviewed variants) — `substance_key`, `query` (the text that matched: the key or an alternate), `match_method` (`pref_name` | `synonym` | `active_substance` (the medicines' one other active substance) | `equivalent_key` (`curated_substance_equivalents()`) | `comma_tail` | `spelling` ("hydrogen carbonate" → "bicarbonate") | `atc_name` (the WHO name of the medicines' one level-5 code, when the key is their only one) | null), `matched_syn_types` (the synonym types that matched; `[]` for a name), `molecule_chembl_id`, `pref_name`, `molecule_type`, `structure_type`, `max_phase`, `parent_chembl_id` (ChEMBL's hierarchy parent, the molecule itself for a parent), `parent_pref_name`, `parent_molecule_type` (salts only), `biotherapeutic_description`, `biocomponent_max_protein_length`, `chembl_atc`, `ambiguity` (null | `same_parent` | `same_molecule_type` | `different_molecule_types`), `candidates` (all, in pick order, when more than one), `unmatched_reason`, `variant_candidates` (hits of spelling, qualifier, salt and isotope variants, or of names starting with the key, for an unmatched key: for review only), `reviewed` (false until checked by hand; a reviewed unmatched row carries the chosen `molecule_chembl_id`, its type read from its variant candidate; a rerun keeps a review for the same key and ID, and keeps a reviewed row whose key now matches another molecule or none, with a warning), `chembl_release`, `retrieved` (the oldest cached response of the run), `source` = `"chembl"`.
- Companies (see Architecture "Companies"):
  - `ema_medicine_companies.json` (one row per medicine, holder or not) — `ema_product_number`, `holder_ema` (EMA verbatim, the "original"), `holder_register` (the Union Register's holder when it decides: it names another company than EMA's, after folding and curated aliases, and lists the product as Active, or EMA names no holder; else null), `holder_used` (the register's when it decides, else EMA's), `holder_basis` (`ema` | `register` | `curated_sponsor`: a reviewed sponsor row decides the company), `company_key` (the holder's company, or a sponsor's), `group_key` (the company's group, or a per-medicine row's: it can differ from the company's `group_key` in `companies.json`, so group medicines by this column; null only without any holder), `country` (ISO code of the linked register product's holder address; null when not linked, or when the register names another company that does not decide), `source` (how the group was decided: `curated` | `gleif` | `holder_name`, the company's own group | `curated_medicine`, a per-medicine row: the business went to another owner than the holder's, e.g. Trudexa: holder Abbott Laboratories Ltd., group AbbVie), `group_note`, `group_evidence_url` (a curated note and its https evidence URL, else null: when `source` is `curated_medicine`, the per-medicine row's, saying why this group (Ioa: "Nomegestrol acetate with estradiol, as Zoely; MSD … sold its further rights to Zoely to Theramex in 2020 …"); with any other `source`, a medicine note (`curated_medicine_notes()`) about its later ownership that did not move it, a plain note, not "why this group" (Udenyca, `source` `holder_name`, group Coherus Oncology: "Coherus sold its U.S. Udenyca business to Intas in April 2025: Accord BioPharma, Intas's U.S. specialty division, now owns it")), `sponsor_note`, `sponsor_evidence_url` (the sponsor row's note and evidence URL when `holder_basis` is `curated_sponsor`, else null; a medicine can have both notes, Udenyca does), `sponsor_rename_evidence_url` (the https evidence URL of the sponsor's rename since, when its name in the sponsor row is a curated alias with evidence (`curated_sponsor_renames()`, matched after folding), else null: the sponsor note names the sponsor as it was then, the sponsor evidence is that older document; the same URL as the group's `ownership` entry for the old name; 7 on 2026-09-28, e.g. Zalviso: `"sponsor_note":"The rights to Zalviso reverted from its licensee Grünenthal to AcelRx (renamed Talphera in 2024) in 2021; …","sponsor_evidence_url":"https://www.sec.gov/…acrx20210930_10q.htm","sponsor_rename_evidence_url":"https://www.prnewswire.com/…talphera-inc-302029288.html"`). Notes are curated UI copy: U.S. spelling, no em-dashes.
  - `companies.json` (one row per company and per group, sorted by key; keys id-safe: `c.<slug>`, `g.<slug>`) — `key`, `kind` (`company` | `group`), `name`, `group_key` (companies: the company's group, where its medicines are unless a per-medicine row moved some (then those medicines' rows name their group); a company all of whose medicines were moved joins the group of most of them; null for groups), `monogram` (groups: 1–4 capitals or digits, unique (curated ones up to 4, a full ticker; derived ones up to 3); null for companies), `monogram_source` (groups: `curated` (`curated_company_groups()`) | `derived` (an own group's, from its name); null for companies; 80 curated and 374 derived on 2026-09-28), `joint_venture`, `partners` (group keys; `[]`), `other_partners` (names of joint-venture partners without a group; `[]`), `representative` (a regulatory service firm holding for others), `lei`, `gleif_legal_name`, `gleif_ultimate_parent`, `gleif_ultimate_parent_lei` (provenance: GLEIF's literal ultimate parent, the entity itself when it reports none; null when unknown or its registration lapsed), `member_holders` (holder names, EMA's and register names used, of the row's medicines that fold or alias to its company(s) (a group's: to its member companies); each name is in one company only, so a holder name maps to one company), `original_holders` (EMA holder names of the row's medicines that name another company: the Union Register decided a later holder (Cinacalcet Viatris: "Mylan Pharmaceuticals Limited" under Viatris Limited), a curated sponsor stands behind a representative, or a per-medicine row moved the medicine to this group (AbbVie: "Abbott Laboratories Ltd."); never an alias of the row), `ownership` (any group: one object `{ "holder", "note", "evidence_url" }` per curated member with a note (an acquisition, rename or spin-off) whose company is in the group, and per curated alias with evidence (a renamed sponsor's old name, `curated_sponsor_renames()`) whose company is in the group, own groups too, by holder (Talphera: `{"holder":"AcelRx Pharmaceuticals","note":"Renamed Talphera in January 2024","evidence_url":"https://www.prnewswire.com/…"}`), e.g. Otsuka: `{"holder":"Avanir Pharmaceuticals","note":"Avanir acquired by Otsuka in 2015","evidence_url":"https://www.sec.gov/…"}`; `[]` for every other row, companies too), `sources` (`ema`, `union_register`, `curated`, `gleif`, in that order; `curated` too for a group with `ownership` notes; `gleif` whenever the row's `lei` or `gleif_legal_name` is filled, a curated group's too, or a group's member company's is), `as_of` (curated groups: the curation date; others: the snapshot date).
  - Committed input, not a site file: `data-raw/gleif-holder-matches.json` (one row per company matched, by its name at matching time; mapped back through `company_match_key()`) — `holder`, `country_hint`, `method` (`exact` | `core` | `holder_name_within` | `sibling` | `stem_top_entity` (a hint)), `confidence`, `lei`, `legal_name`, `matched_name`, `lei_country`, `entity_status`, `registration_status`, `ultimate_parent_lei`, `ultimate_parent_name`, `ultimate_parent_registration_status`, `ultimate_parent_exception`, `why`, `reviewed` (false until checked by hand; a rerun keeps it for the same holder and LEI, and keeps a reviewed row it does not find again, with a warning), `retrieved` (the oldest cached response the run used), `source` = `"gleif"`.
- Pivotal results (see Architecture "Pivotal results"):
  - `ema_medicine_efficacy.json` (the shown rows (`review` `auto_ok` or `reviewed_ok`) of medicines in the data; one row per result, sorted by `ema_product_number`, `indication`, the lead first, `row_order`, `row_key`;
