`[]` until the first extraction; the site columns of ruling R12, which replace the spec's `subgroup`, `matches_indication` and `data_cutoff`) — `ema_product_number`, `row_key` (stable across reruns, R11), `row_order` (the order in the SmPC), `indication` (the 4.1 text the row supports, or its short form; null when not tied to one), `trial` (as printed), `trial_key` (folded, without the registry number: MARIPOSA in Rybrevant and Lazcluze alike), `population` (as printed), `population_match` (`whole_trial_matches` | `subgroup_matches` | `whole_trial_broader` | `other` | null), `regimen`, `comparator` (null: single-arm), `comparator_column_label` (the table column the control values come from), `n_treatment`, `n_control`, `endpoint` (as printed, "PFS", "OS", "ORR"…), `assessment` ("investigator", "BICR"…), `is_primary` (true | false | null: the SmPC does not name the primary endpoint), `analysis_role` (`primary` | `later` | `exploratory` | null), `analysis` (the source's words: data cut, interim or final), `effect_type` (`hr` | `hr_noninferiority` | `rate_difference` | `single_arm_rate` | `single_arm_median`), `value`, `ci_low`, `ci_high` (strings as printed, "NR" kept), `ci_level` (number; null when not stated), `ci_is_range` (true: the bracketed numbers are a range, not a CI), `p_value`, `significance_stated` (the source's words, else null), `arm_treatment`, `arm_control`, `arm_measure` (per-arm values as printed, e.g. medians with their CI, and what they are), `lead` (true for the one row per product and indication the card leads with: `choose_lead_rows()`), `quotes` (1–3 verbatim quotes), `source_url` (https, the product information PDF), `source_date` (its `last_updated_date` when extracted), `page` (the PDF page of its longest quote), `review`, `stale` (true: the product information has changed since), `extractor_model`, `extracted_at`, `source` = `"ema_smpc"`. Example (Alecensa, ALINA): `{"ema_product_number":"EMEA/H/C/004164","indication":"Adjuvant treatment of ALK-positive NSCLC after tumour resection.","trial":"ALINA (BO40336)","trial_key":"alina","population":"stage II-IIIA","population_match":"subgroup_matches","regimen":"alectinib (2 years)","comparator":"platinum chemotherapy","n_treatment":116,"n_control":115,"endpoint":"DFS","assessment":"investigator","is_primary":true,"analysis_role":"primary","effect_type":"hr","value":"0.24","ci_low":"0.13","ci_high":"0.45","ci_level":95,"ci_is_range":false,"lead":true,"page":14,"review":"auto_ok","stale":false,…,"source":"ema_smpc"}`.
  - Committed input, not a site file: `data-raw/efficacy-rows.json` (written by the extractor, read by the pipeline; one record per line, LF; every row the verifier kept, whatever its review) — the site columns but `trial_key`, `lead`, `stale` and `source`, plus `verification` (`exact` | `reassembled`), `flags` (the reasons a human must look, `efficacy_flags()`; `[]` for none) and `review` (`auto_ok` | `flagged` | `reviewed_ok` | `reviewed_rejected`: the owner sets the last two by hand).
  - Committed input, not a site file: `data-raw/efficacy-extractions.json` (one record per product extracted; what the next run plans from) — `ema_product_number`, `document_url`, `document_last_updated_date` (the product information extracted), `status` (`ok` | `no_rows` | `no_section` | `not_found` | `failed`), `reason` (why a product `failed`, or which rows the parser dropped; else null), `rows_kept`, `rows_failed` (rows the verifier dropped), `extractor_model`, `extracted_at`.
- Phase 1b (lookup):
  - `ema_search_index.json` (small first-load index) — `ema_product_number`, `name_of_medicine`, `substances`, `substance_keys`, `active_substance`, `medicine_status`, `marketing_authorisation_date`, `medicine_type`, `orphan_medicine`, `conditional_approval`, `exceptional_circumstances`, `additional_monitoring` (EMA's Yes/No flags as in `ema_medicines.json`, so the card's first screen can show them before that file loads; 2026-09-28: 963 KB raw, 87 KB gzipped, from 746 KB and 83 KB without them).
  - `mesh_entry_terms.json` (folded entry term → descriptor, for EMA terms and their ancestors; sorted by `entry_term`) — `entry_term`, `mesh_descriptor_ui`.
  - `mesh_descriptor_areas.json` (descriptor → EMA terms in its subtree; sorted by `mesh_descriptor_ui`) — `mesh_descriptor_ui`, `mesh_descriptor_name`, `therapeutic_area_mesh`.
  - `mesh_descriptor_notes.json` (NLM definitions for the therapeutic area tree; `build_mesh_descriptor_notes()`: the descriptors of the matched EMA terms and of their level-1 to 3 tree nodes, a branch root being the descriptor holding its code (every branch has one in MeSH 2026); sorted by `mesh_descriptor_ui`) — `mesh_descriptor_ui`, `mesh_descriptor_name`, `tree_numbers` (every tree number of the descriptor, sorted; `[]` when it has none), `scope_note` (the preferred concept's ScopeNote, verbatim, whitespace collapsed; null when MeSH has none), `source` = `"nlm_mesh"`. The parsed MeSH cache (`.cache/downloads/mesh/descriptors-{year}.rds`) is reused only for the same Last-Modified and `mesh_parser_version` (2: scope notes), so a reader that learns more re-parses.
  - `ema_medicine_documents.json` (EPAR documents index: SmPC/product information, assessment reports, variation reports, summary, RMP summary, procedural steps) — `ema_product_number`, `document_type`, `title`, `url`, `first_published_date`, `last_updated_date`, `link_method` (`product_number` | `url_slug`), `source`.
  - `ema_medicine_primary_documents.json` (audit 2026-09-30, S5: the rows the medicine card's product information / EPAR / overview buttons and the tables' PI and EPAR links can show, so a phone need not load the whole documents index for them; `build_primary_documents_table()` in `R/documents.R`, from `ema_medicine_documents.json`; per medicine and `document_type` (`product-information`, `assessment-report`, `overview`) the newest https row that is not an "(archive)" file and, for assessment reports, the newest standard EPAR ("… EPAR - Public assessment report") and the newest refusal report (a title of its own naming a refusal) apart; ties on the date by title, then the index's order (by URL), as the frontend sorts; the frontend still picks by the medicine's status (`primaryDocuments()` in `documents.js`, which reads these rows as well as the index's; a node test checks, on the real data, that both give every medicine the same buttons for every status); no `source` column: it indexes the documents index; sorted by `ema_product_number`, `document_type`, `refusal_report`; 5,574 rows, 1.3 MB raw, 75 KB gzipped on 2026-09-29, against the index's 5.3 MB and 301 KB) — `ema_product_number`, `document_type`, `refusal_report` (true for a refusal report, else false), `url`, `last_updated_date`.
  - `ema_medicine_protection.json` (regulatory-protection estimate: counted from the first central approval of the same substance set, equivalent spellings joined; 8 y data exclusivity, 10–11 y market protection; see Architecture "Regulatory-protection estimate") — `ema_product_number`, `basis` (`own`: counted from the set's first product, itself or another; `follows_reference`: a generic, biosimilar or curated copy; `reference_not_found`: a generic or biosimilar without a central reference, or a curated copy of a nationally authorised medicine, no dates; `other_company_reference` (step 3): the set's first product is another company group's and the product's own group's first approval came later, unless both estimates have ended; `paediatric_use` (2026-09-29): a paediatric-use marketing authorisation (a row of `ema_curated_pumas.json`), counted from its own approval, the reference itself, 11 on 2026-09-29), `copy_source` (step 4 review: what says the medicine is a copy: `ema_flag` (EMA's generic or biosimilar flag) | `curated` (a row of `ema_curated_copies.json`, which then decides the reference, even where EMA flags it: Riulvy); null for `own`, `paediatric_use` and `other_company_reference`), `reference_product_number`, `reference_name` (the named first product: see Architecture), `counted_from` (the set's first central approval date, the start of the reference's 30-day window: not always the named product's own date, e.g. Humira's rows count from Trudexa's 2003-09-01), `own_reference_product_number`, `own_counted_from` (step 3 review: for `other_company_reference` only, else null: the first product of the product's own company group and its first approval date, always later than `counted_from`), `data_exclusivity_end` (from `counted_from`), `data_exclusivity_end_max` (step 4: for `other_company_reference` only, else null: from `own_counted_from`, so `data_exclusivity_end` is the earlier end), `market_protection_end_min` (from `counted_from`), `market_protection_end_max` (from `counted_from`; for `other_company_reference` from `own_counted_from`, so the range covers both estimates), `status` (`protected` | `ended` | `unclear`, from the market protection range; always `unclear` for `reference_not_found`; for `other_company_reference` `unclear` only where the two estimates' statuses differ, `protected` where both are), `source` = `"estimate_from_ema_dates"`.
  - `ema_substance_equivalents.json` (step 3; substance keys naming the same active substance, e.g. "dasatinib" and "dasatinib (anhydrous)"; only curated pairs, both directions, so a group of three keys may lack its third pair (join chains); sorted by `substance_key`, `equivalent_key`) — `substance_key`, `equivalent_key` (both keys of `ema_medicine_substances.json`), `basis` = `"curated"`, `evidence_url` (https, the EPAR page checked), `checked_date`, `source` = `"curated"`.
  - `ema_curated_copies.json` (step 4 review; copies EMA's data does not flag, checked by hand against their EPAR pages: the rows of `curated_copy_medicines()` whose medicine and central reference are in the data (41 on 2026-09-29); see Architecture "Regulatory-protection estimate"; sorted by `ema_product_number`) — `ema_product_number`, `copy_type` (`hybrid` | `generic` | `biosimilar`), `reference_product_number` (null when the reference is authorised only nationally), `reference_name` (as the EPAR page names it, also for a national reference, e.g. "Hydrea"), `evidence_url` (https, the EPAR page), `evidence_quote` (verbatim, at most 20 words, naming the copy type), `checked_date`, `note` (null unless needed), `source` = `"curated"`.
  - `ema_curated_pumas.json` (2026-09-29; paediatric-use marketing authorisations, checked by hand against their EMA public assessment reports: the rows of `curated_puma_medicines()` whose medicine is in the data (11 on 2026-09-29); see Architecture "Regulatory-protection estimate"; sorted by `ema_product_number`) — `ema_product_number`, `evidence_url` (https, the public assessment report), `evidence_quote` (verbatim, at most 20 words, naming a paediatric-use marketing authorisation), `checked_date`, `note` (null unless needed), `source` = `"curated"`.
  - `ema_medicine_orphan_exclusivity.json` (Union Register orphan links; end = register EndDate or LinkDate + 10 y + extension) — `ema_product_number`, `eu_designation_number`, `condition`, `link_date`, `extension_months`, `exclusivity_end`, `end_source` (`register` | `computed`), `designation_status`, `source`.
  - `ema_medicine_register_status.json` (Union Register status per product; EMA status stays primary) — `ema_product_number`, `eu_number`, `register_status`, `register_last_decision_date`, `register_url`, `match_method` (`link` | `name` | `link_and_name`), `agrees_with_ema` (only register status "Active" counts as authorized; an unknown status stops the build, as the register is BETA), `source`.
  - Search folding (lower-case; one left-to-right pass `ae|oe` → `e`; hyphen-minus and U+2010–U+2015 → space; trim and collapse whitespace) is identical in R (`fold_search_text()`) and JS, guarded by a shared fixture used by both test suites.
- Lookup/index files (`ema_search_index`, `mesh_entry_terms`, `mesh_descriptor_areas`, `ema_medicine_primary_documents`) have no `source` column: they index data, they don't enrich it.

## Frontend (phase 1b)
- URL keys: filters (phase 1; `atc` is a list since phase 4a, repeated keys like the other lists) plus `q` (free text), `med` (EMA product number), `sub` (substance_key), `cond` (MeSH descriptor UI), `co` (company group or company key: the company page; companies part 2), `show` (navigation fixes 2026-10-01, lookup.md #7: `show=all` is "Show all statuses" on a condition, indication-text or company page, so a reload and a shared link keep it; decoded only with such a view (`showsEveryStatus()` in `url.js`), another value or view drops it without a note; the toggle replaces the entry (`createLookup()` `onShowAll`); opening another view clears it, Back to the page keeps it). `mah` (companies part 2) is one list of company group keys (`g.roche`), company keys (`c.roche`), EMA holder names (older links: `?mah=Roche%20Registration%20GmbH` still loads) and, for a tree row whose value also shows under another group or company, the row's path (`g.organon/c.merck-sharp-dohme`, `g.viatris/c.mylan/Mylan S.A.S`; keys are id-safe slugs, so `/` only separates; `companyPath()`, `matchesCompany()` in `companies.js`, used by `filters.js`), combined with OR; every value loads as the filter values of the rows standing for it (`canonical()`: a company whose group has only it, under the same name, as the group; a company under two groups, `?mah=c.mylan`, as both rows' paths; a name the tree does not show stays itself, e.g. "Not stated"), so a row selects exactly the medicines it counts (review of companies part 2: the Organon row of "Merck Sharp & Dohme B.V." counted 1 and filtered 63; a data-file test checks every row), and a value within another selected one is dropped (`ancestors()`: every medicine of it is under the other), as `toggleCompany()`. Status (owner decision 2026-09-29, "Authorized by default"): the default is `["Authorised"]` (`DEFAULT_STATE.status`), written without a key, so a link without `status` shows the authorized medicines (links from before, which then meant every status, now open authorized; an explicit `?status=Authorised` is the default, its key dropped); every status is `?status=all` (`STATUS_ALL`; the state's empty list), and "all" wins over values listed with it; other values are the statuses chosen (`?status=Withdrawn`, repeated keys), kept as before; unknown ones are reported and, with none left, leave the default (`decodeState()`, `encodeState()`; `isDefaultStatus()`, `filterIsSet()` (a key other than its default: for the status any list but the default, every status included) and `activeFilterCount()` (the dimensions set, the years one, the default status none; an optional dimension left out) in `url.js`, tested). `tab` (F · Spacious, phase 2) is the dashboard's tab, a view like `by`, written only when not the Overview (see "Tabs"). `view` (the removed "Authorized now" / "Approvals per year" tabs) is ignored without a note, so old links still load (`IGNORED_KEYS` in `url.js`). Therapeutic areas (phase 4f, one tree): `area` is one list holding MeSH category letters (`C`; owner decision 2026-09-29), branch codes (`C04`), tree numbers (`C04.588.180`) and EMA's terms (`Psoriasis`), combined with OR; there is no `branch` state key any more: a link's `branch` values load into `area` (so `?branch=C04&area=Psoriasis` still works and is rewritten to `?area=C04&area=Psoriasis`), an EMA term that is a tree node itself loads as that key (`domain.areaCanonical`, `canonical()` in `areas.js`: `?area=Anemia` → `?area=C15.378.050`, `?area=Breast Neoplasms` → `C04.588.180`; phase 4f review: so the tree shows one checked row; this widens such a link from the term alone to the term and everything under it, as MeSH means it), a tag matched at a branch root stays that tag (phase 4g, user decision 2026-09-28: `?area=Cancer`, `?area=Neoplasms` filter the medicines tagged with it and show it as a sentence pill, "tagged Cancer" (phase 4g review; see "Therapeutic area tree"); phase 4f loaded them as `C04`), and an area under another selected one is dropped (`domain.areaAncestors`, as `toggleArea()`). Modality (M2 phase 2): `mod` is one list of group keys (`antibody`) and modality keys (`bispecific_antibody`), repeated keys, combined with OR; a modality under a selected group is dropped (`domain.modalityAncestors`, as `toggleModality()`), unknown values (and every value without the modality data) are dropped and reported; `by=mod` is the modality breakdown; a modality opened from a card is `modalityState()` (the value alone, `by=mod`, on the Classes and areas tab, `tab=classes`; pushState). Opening a card/result or a drug class uses pushState (back button works); filter edits use replaceState and announce the new headline. **Scroll** (navigation fixes 2026-10-01, dashboard.md #1, lookup.md #1 and #6; fix-up of their review the same day; `history-scroll.js`, tested): the page restores scroll positions itself (`history.scrollRestoration` "manual"; with "auto" Back landed at the top, as the entry was pushed after the new view had scrolled to its heading, or 8,000–10,000px down, as the position was restored before the render and scroll anchoring then moved it by the height of the card put back above). Each entry's state carries an id and where the entry was left, its place (`placeAt()`): the part at the middle of the screen, the lookup result (`#result`) or the overview below it (`#app`), else neither, with the position from its top, as the parts above can come back other heights (the intro card closes after the first lookup: Back came back one card lower); the result's height; and the result's open disclosures (`details[data-key]`: More details, the full indication), which the card made anew reopens (`lookup.js` `render()` `reopen`, each once it is in the card; review: Back from a link in More details landed in the overview below the shorter card). A push is written at once (`scheduleUrlWrite()`, before the render; replaces stay one per frame and keep the state), noting the place of the entry left (in memory and in its state); a second after scrolling stops the entry notes its place, and the tab's session storage (`approval-atlas:scroll`: the entry's id and place) when the page is left or hidden (`pagehide`, `visibilitychange`; a reload within that second came back at the top; Chrome drops a history state written then); a reload restores it, disclosures reopened. Popstate notes the place of the entry left. One that leaves the path and query as they are (a fragment navigation, as Chrome fires popstate for `location.hash` and `#` links, or Back and Forward over one) is the same view: nothing closes or renders, and the page goes to the entry's place when known (the browser scrolls to a new fragment itself; review: it went to the top). Otherwise it closes an open filter popover or sheet (`closeFiltersNow`, dashboard.md #2) and after the render goes to the place of the view shown (the top when unknown; `restoreStep()`), once its part is shown and, for a place in the result, the result is as tall as when left or the view's data has loaded (`lookup.loading()`, the datasets its render and controls asked for; then the result's end mid-screen at most), scroll anchoring off until then (`html.restoring-scroll`), on otherwise, so data rendering above keeps the page still; a wheel, touch, key or pointer press ends a restore still waiting. When it closed a popover or sheet, focus (the chip the sheet gives it back to, or the page) then goes to the view's heading when in view, else to the page (`settleFocus()`; review: the chip stayed focused off-screen). A new view still focuses its heading, scrolled clear of the sticky top bar (`scroll-padding-top`).
- Loading order: meta + `ema_search_index` + `mesh_entry_terms` first (search usable immediately; `index.html` preloads meta and the search index, `<link rel="preload" as="fetch" crossorigin>` before the theme script, so they download during the theme script's round trip, not after the page's script: bug hunt 2026-10-01, on Slow 4G with CPU x4 at 390px they started 2.9 s in, now 0.6 s; not the MeSH terms (fix-up: with all three, 166 KB, they shared the bandwidth with the stylesheet, first paint 2.14 → 2.38 s; 89 KB fit in the idle line): Chrome 154, medians of 10, the search usable 4.9 → 4.45 s and the first paint unchanged (2.14 → 2.15 s; all three: 4.05 s and 2.38 s); Fast 4G 1.65 → 1.54 s (all three: 1.54 s), 3G 15.8 → 14.0 s (all three: 12.2 s, first paint +0.9 s); main.js's fetch reuses each, one request per file, through the service worker too; Playwright's Chromium 153 build fetches them twice under the worker, "cross-world service worker resource mismatch", Chrome and Edge 154 do not); everything else in the background (`companies.json` and `ema_medicine_companies.json` with the dashboard files, and as the lookup's `need("companies")`, started with the conditions: the Companies suggestions, the cards' holders and the company page; one `loadFile` promise; `ema_medicines.json` likewise as the lookup's `need("medicines")`, step 2: EMA's opinion in the suggestions' meta line); the primary documents (`ema_medicine_primary_documents.json`, the lookup's `need("primaryDocuments")`, `{ optional }`: without it (older data) the buttons and links come from the documents index, `quickDocumentRows()` in `lookup.js`; on a shared `?med=` link every other data file waits for it once the search works, `firstFile` in `main.js`, so the buttons do not share the network with the dashboard's files: on the audit's phone profile they show 0.2 s after the headline instead of 1.5 s; the others wait 1.5 s at most (`FIRST_FILE_WAIT_MS`, `settledOrAfter()` in `datasets.js`), so a stalled small file cannot hold the rest; its 404 (older data) is kept for the page load, not asked for again) and the protection/orphan files (and `ema_curated_copies.json` and `ema_curated_pumas.json`, optional) on first card open (a dataset whose optional file fails to load, offline or on flaky Wi-Fi, is built without it and asks for it once more on the next card or list that uses it (a new lookup view marks it, its next `need()` asks; not the overview in between), keeping what it has meanwhile, so older data costs one extra request, not one per card; a needed file that fails shows "Not available right now." and loads again on every new view, as before: `createDatasets()` in `datasets.js`, tested) (substance, condition and indication-text result tables count as cards: their rows' PI/EPAR links and ATC codes), the protection dataset (protection, orphan exclusivity, curated copies and PUMAs: about 61 KB gzipped) also once the page is idle after the dashboard's first render (#13: `whenIdle()` in `main.js`, `requestIdleCallback` or 2 s without it), so a card opened later offline or on slow Wi-Fi has its protection section; `ema_medicine_register_status`, `ema_medicine_primary_documents` (the table's PI/EPAR links, added in place when it arrives) and `mesh_descriptor_notes` (the lookup's `need("meshNotes")`, also started by a card that shows therapeutic areas: the MeSH explainers, added in place, and the area tree's order of EMA's terms; see "MeSH explainers") after the dashboard's first render (table marker, register note under the top row). A missing notes file (older data) leaves the explainers out and the terms by name. `modalities.json` and `ema_medicine_modalities.json` (M2 phase 2) load with the dashboard files but are optional (`MODALITY_FILES` in `main.js`, the lookup's `need("modalities")` with `{ optional }` files): without them the page works and shows no modality (section, chip, breakdown and stack buttons stay hidden, a link's `mod` values are reported as ignored). `atc_class_explanations.json` (owner decisions 2026-09-29) likewise loads with the dashboard files, optional (`ATC_EXPLANATION_FILES` in `main.js`), and as an `{ optional }` file of the lookup's `need("atc")` (the result tables' badges; one `loadFile` promise): without it (older data) the ATC tips are as before. Step 4: `ema_substance_equivalents.json` (the lookup's `need("equivalents")`, small) also loads after the dashboard's first render, for the conditions card's treatment counts (EMA's spellings until it arrives, or without it); the conditions card's rows also need the lookup's `need("conditions")` dataset (`mesh_descriptor_areas.json` with the area files, so each row counts as its condition page; asked for when the lookup starts, so usually there at the dashboard's first render; "Loading…" until then; review 2026-09-29); `meta.json` `opinion_to_decision.median_days` and `p90_days` (#12) come with meta (older data: none, the sentence leaves it out); the search index's `conditional_approval`, `exceptional_circumstances` and `additional_monitoring` (#9) put the qualifiers on the first screen (older index: once `ema_medicines.json` has loaded). Pivotal results (2026-09-30): `ema_medicine_efficacy.json` is the lookup's `need("efficacy")`, `{ optional }`, asked for by the medicine card (its teaser and section) and once the page is idle (`whenIdle()` in `main.js`, for offline cards); without it (older data, or before the first extraction) the card shows neither, and nothing waits on it.
- Offline: `site/public/sw.js` (production builds only) — cache-first for hashed `/assets/*` in a per-build cache (`approval-atlas-assets-<version>`, version stamped by `site/vite.config.js`; activate deletes older asset caches), network-first with cache fallback for pages, `/data/*.json`, manifest and icon in one cache kept across deploys (`approval-atlas-data`); with a cached copy at hand, a network answer slower than 3 s (`NETWORK_TIMEOUT_MS`) or a failed one is not waited for: the copy answers and the request goes on to update the cache (`waitUntil`) for the next load (#13, conference Wi-Fi; without a copy the network is awaited as before; hashed assets stay cache-first; the page itself (`mode` "navigate") is not raced, only answered from the cache when the network fails (review of step 4: a cached page can be an older build's, whose hashed assets a new worker has deleted and GitHub Pages no longer serves: a blank page, and, as its asset URLs posted to the new worker fail, a blank page offline later; index.html is about 4.5 KB gzipped); files answered from the cache can be a day older than those the network answered in time, as offline); `/theme-init.js` (render-blocking, see "Theme") is answered from that cache at once when it holds a copy, the network refreshing it for the next load (`cacheThenRefresh()`); tested in `service-worker.test.js` (fake caches, network and timers); never cross-origin (PDFs). Offline works after one online visit for what that visit loaded (card files and condition areas load on first use). `manifest.webmanifest` makes it installable.
- ATC names at every level display in Title Case (user decision 2026-09-27; data stays verbatim): `atcDisplayName()` in `labels.js`, used by `atcClassLabel()` ("{code} {Title Case name}", e.g. "L Antineoplastic and Immunomodulating Agents", "L04 Immunosuppressants"), `atcName()` and every aria-label built from them, so badges' hidden names and tooltips, ladders, ATC tree, breakdown, activity card, search suggestions, headline and filter chip all agree. Rules (tested on real names): minor words (and, or, and/or, nor, but, for, of, the, in, on, at, as, to, from, with, by, per, via, vs, a, an, excl., incl.) lower-case unless first or last ("Agents Acting on the Renin-Angiotensin System", "Arteriolar Smooth Muscle, Agents Acting On"), a lone capital "A" (vitamin) excepted; hyphen and slash parts each capitalized ("Musculo-Skeletal", "TNF-Alpha"); parts with digits ("GLP-1", "beta-1a" -> "Beta-1a", "B12") or inner capitals ("mTOR", "CoA", "ARBs") as written; in all-capitals names only the acronyms in `ATC_ACRONYMS` (scanned from `atc_classes.json`) stay capitals ("SURGICAL AIDS" -> "Surgical Aids"), in mixed-case names every all-capitals word is an acronym.
- **Landing** (user-approved design 2026-09-28, roadmap step 2; the conference lookup stays primary, a first-time non-expert should see in ~5 s what the site is for): the top bar (F · Spacious, phase 1; see "F · Spacious redesign"): the wordmark with the data's date under it (`UI.dataDate()`: "EMA data as of 29 Sep 2026", "EMA data" until meta.json has loaded), the search, and a "What is this?" button (`#intro-link`, `.inline-button`: reads as a link; 36px tall, 44px on phones). The tagline ("Look up the regulatory status of any drug in the EU.") and the scope line ("Human medicines, EMA central procedure") left the bar (F · Spacious, phase 1: beside the 560px search they did not fit one clean line; `UI.tagline` and `UI.scopeLine` are gone; the tagline, reworded "Look up the EU approval status of medicines authorized through EMA." (owner call on the copy review, 2026-09-30), lives on as the page's `<meta name="description">` and the manifest's description; the scope line the owner chose shows since 2026-09-30 under the page heading, "Human medicines authorized EU-wide through EMA": see "Page header"): the intro card's cards and its quiet scope line, and the About disclosure, say what the site is for and covers. The search's placeholder is "Drug name, active substance or condition", its (hidden) label "Search by drug name, active substance, condition, company or ATC code" (copy review 2026-09-30: "active substance" everywhere, owner call) (ATC codes still work; `UI.lookup.placeholder` / `label`, set by `renderAbout()` before any data loads; the placeholder is 11.5px under 360px to fit 320px phones); each Try example names its kind in muted text after the link, kept on one line with it and with the "·" after it ("Keytruda (brand) · semaglutide (active substance) · psoriasis (condition) · L04AC (drug class)"; `examples[].kind`, `UI.lookup.exampleKind()`; `.try-sep` inside `.try-item`, bug hunt 2026-10-01: a separator of its own began the second line at 390px; it lies in the 20px gap after its example without counting toward the line's width, so the line breaks where it did and a line's last separator hangs into the gutter). **Intro card** (`intro.js`, `#intro` between the Try line and the result/headline; owner decision 2026-09-29: non-expert onboarding cards, as PandaDoc's, in place of the two lists "Look up a drug or active ingredient to see:" and "Or explore all EMA medicines:" and their phone disclosure): three cards (`UI.intro.cards`; a `<ul>`, each title bold text, not a heading: the card comes before the page's h1, audit 2026-09-30, S2), each an inline-SVG icon (`ICONS` in `intro.js`, aria-hidden: 24px, 2px strokes and solid parts in the hue's text shade, a soft part in its mid; the lookup icon's capsule a 1.5px outline with one half filled, review 2026-09-29: without an outline its soft half, 1.6:1 on the tile, read as a smudge at 1x) on its hue's level-1 tint (`.hue-green`, `.hue-gold`, `.hue-blue`; both modes, text shade on level 1 is a checked pair), a bold title, one plain sentence and an example link (`lookup.link`, pushState as the Try line's; the cards are made on the first render, when the lookup exists): "Look up a drug" / "Type a brand or active substance: is it authorized in the EU, what for, since when, and who owns it?" (owner decision 2026-09-29, was "and by which company?"; copy review 2026-09-30) / "Try Keytruda" (its card); "Protection and copies" / "See roughly how long market protection runs, and whether generics or biosimilars are authorized yet." / "Try Humira" (its card: the Protection and copies block, its lead and copies lines, is its third block, right after the document buttons, its lead on the first screen of a 390×844 phone (646px; at 320×640 it starts at 630px, its line cut by the screen's edge), so no jump to the protection section); "Explore the landscape" / "Which conditions have the most medicines, which companies are active where, and how approvals change over time." / "Explore cancer medicines" (the overview filtered to Neoplasms alone, broken down by therapeutic area: `areaState("C04")` in `url.js`, `?area=C04&by=area&tab=classes`, on the Classes and areas tab (F · Spacious, phase 2)); `introCardPatch()` (pure) gives each example's state, and a test checks the examples are in the data. Then one quiet line under a hairline (`UI.intro.scope`, `--muted`): "Covers medicines authorized EU-wide through the European Medicines Agency (EMA). Many older medicines, such as paracetamol, are authorized country by country and aren't here. For information only, not medical advice." (what "authorized" means is the About disclosure's scope). Frame (owner decision 2026-09-30, "sized properly and aligned with the rest of the site"): the dashboard cards' (Common Region): the main column's full width (it was 880px at most, so at 1024 and 1440 its right edge stopped 80px and 320px short of the page header and cards), 24px padding (16px on phones; it was 20px 20px 16px, and 10px 10px 8px on phones), 12px corners, a hairline, the surface, 24px below at every width (16px below 1024px before). Layout by the card's own width (`.intro` is a size container): three columns from 640px (44px icon tiles, 16px titles, 14px sentences, the examples level at the cards' foot), else rows, icon left and text right, the example running on after the sentence (one line, `nowrap`; a `::before` 13.5px above and below its text makes it 44px tall without taking room in the line, and its focus ring stays on the text); 14–15px type in rows from 480px, 13px below (phones), where the × sits level with the first title, which keeps clear of it. Copy in `UI.intro`, a region named by a visually hidden "What is this?" (a paragraph, not a heading, for the same reason). It shows on the untouched overview only (`isOverview()`: no lookup, no filter (`filterIsSet()`: the default status, authorized, is none; every status is one); before the dashboard's data loads the URL's kept filter keys count, every key but `by`; a `tab` key names a tab other than the Overview) and on the Overview tab (review of F · Spacious phase 2: the tab is a view, not a filter, `url.js` `FILTER_KEYS`, but a switch to another tab leaves the untouched overview, so the card has had its turn and, back on the Overview, stays collapsed) on the viewer's first visit only (2026-09-29, the paradox of the active user: people start using a site at once, so the card gets one turn): until the viewer does any lookup or filter (a view that is not the untouched overview, a link's included, so a first visit through a shared link is its turn too (owner decision 2026-09-30); back on the overview it stays collapsed) or closes it; later visits start with it collapsed to "What is this?" (`introSpent()`, `introVisible()`, pure and tested; the visit is kept per viewer in localStorage, `approval-atlas:intro-seen` = "1", written when the page starts and read before, `readIntroSeen()` / `storeIntroSeen()`, every access in try/catch: with storage blocked every visit is a first one), and wherever the viewer asked for it with "What is this?" (which also undoes the close) until the view (`encodeUrl()`) changes or it is closed. × ("Close the introduction", tooltip saying "What is this?" brings it back; 44px, top right) hides it and moves focus to "What is this?"; "What is this?" shows it and focuses the card (`tabindex="-1"`, focus ring only for keyboard users). The close is kept per viewer in localStorage (`approval-atlas:intro-closed` = "1", removed when brought back; `readIntroClosed()` / `storeIntroClosed()`, every access in try/catch, the page works without it). **Try line** (owner decision 2026-09-29): hidden while the card shows, whose examples stand in for it; back once the card is closed and on the filtered overview, and hidden on lookups as before, so a result starts right under the search on a phone (`tryLineVisible(introShown, lookupOpen)` in `intro.js`, pure and tested; `createIntro()` sets `#lookup-try` with the card on every render and on × / "What is this?", so both switch at once and focus moves as before; `index.html` keeps it hidden until the first render). Heights with Geist (2026-09-30, production build, the card from its top at 97px under the top bar on desktop, 141px at 390 and 145px at 320; before the frame / after): 1440 880 × 307px / 1200 × 298px (both edges now the page header's and cards' 120 and 1320), 1024 880 × 307 / 960 × 298 (32 and 992), 390 358 × 346 / 358 × 360 (the padding), 320 288 × 413 / 288 × 427, ending at 572px, within a 640px phone screen; the page header then starts 24px under it everywhere (16px below 1024px before). The × keeps level with the first title in rows (its padding-top 11px, was 5px).
- **EMA central scope in the answers** (step 2, #1; 284 of 1,278 substances have no centrally authorized medicine, e.g. celecoxib, testosterone): a substance's headline says "{X} is in {n} medicines authorized EU-wide through EMA." (copy review 2026-09-30: medicines are authorized, not substances) or, with none, "{X}: no medicine is currently authorized through EMA (national authorizations are not included)." (the parenthesis a headline part of tone `aside`: its own line in the dek's size, `.tone-aside`); its dek "First central EU approval: {date} ({name})" ("No central EU approval date"); the protection line "Counted from the first central EU approval of {substance}: …"; the About disclosure's scope (`UI.about.scope`, after "What this is": EMA's central procedure, every status; national-only medicines not here; valid in the EU, Iceland, Liechtenstein and Norway, not the UK or Switzerland; sale and reimbursement national). A labels test checks that substance and condition answers and the scope lines (the page header's too) name EMA or the central procedure. The medicine headline stays "{name} is (not) authorized in the EU." (a withdrawn central authorization is correctly "not authorized").

## Frontend design ("E · Sage" redesign; phase 1 = look and feel, done on `feature/redesign-e`)
- Fonts: Geist and Geist Mono (OFL), bundled from `@fontsource-variable/geist` and `…/geist-mono` via `@import` at the top of `site/src/style.css`; Vite emits hashed `.woff2` files in `/assets/` (same origin, CSP `font-src 'self'`; the service worker caches them like any hashed asset, and its cache version changes with the file names). Geist Mono for codes (ATC); numbers use `tabular-nums`.
- Palette: tokens on `:root` with a dark override in two blocks of the same declarations (`color-scheme: dark` included): `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) { :root:not([data-theme="light"]) {…} }` (Auto on a dark device) and `:root[data-theme="dark"] {…}` (Dark chosen; see "Theme"); edit both (`palette.test.js` fails when they differ; `readPalette()` in `main.js` reads the second). Page `#F3F3EE`, surface `#FAFAF7`, ink `#282828`, accent `#2B4A31` (vrognas.com), link text `#576A39` with `#8C957B` underline. Fourteen damped hues (`--{hue}-1…5`, `-mid`, `-text`): level-1…5 shades for ATC levels, mids for chart marks, text colour on fills. `.hue-{name}` classes pass a hue to badges and bars. Medicine types: Other blue, Generic gold, Biosimilar teal, Advanced therapy purple, Orphan pink badge. Statuses (phase 4a review, `statusHue()` in `badges.js`): each its own hue, Authorized green, Withdrawn red, Expired orange, Lapsed brown, Suspended pink, Revoked plum, Refused purple, Application withdrawn and Withdrawn from rolling review slate, Opinion and Opinion under re-examination gold (unknown: slate); an 8px dot in the hue's mid plus the label in its text colour, pills on its level-1 fill, the segments of "Approvals per year" stacked by Status in the mid (1px gaps between them; the approval-years strip's until phase 4f). `statusKind()` stays for tone and copy (the headline's "not" / "not yet": `--status-ended-text`, `--status-pending-text`). Stacked by Status (and in its legend) Authorized, the bottom segment of every column, is `--status-authorized` (`--green-text`) instead of the green mid (`statusColor()` in `badges.js`), so it differs in lightness from every status stacked on it (≥ 1.5:1, tested; red and green mids alone are 1.08:1, WCAG 1.4.1). `palette.test.js` checks every text pair ≥ 4.5:1 and every chart mark and text-field border (`--field-border`; `--axis` is for chart axes) ≥ 3:1 in both modes; add new colour pairs there.
- **Theme** (owner request 2026-09-28; `theme.js`, `site/public/theme-init.js`): an icon button at the right end of the top bar (`#theme-toggle` in `.topbar-actions`, after "What is this?"; F · Spacious, phase 1) shows the scheme in use, Light (sun) or Dark (moon), and every press shows the other (`nextTheme()`; bug 2026-09-29: the earlier Auto › other › own › Auto cycle had a press that changed nothing): a press onto the device's own scheme is Auto again (the default: nothing stored, the page follows the device), onto the other one stores it. Named "Theme: Dark" (`UI.theme.button()`, the scheme shown: `shownScheme()`), tooltip "Theme: Dark (follows your device). Select to switch to Light (remembered on this device)." (the parenthesis only when the press stores a choice) (`UI.theme.hint()`, "(follows your device)" under Auto only); 36px with a hairline on desktop, 44px without one on phones. The choice is `data-theme="light|dark"` on `<html>` (none under Auto; the dark tokens follow, see Palette; `.sheet::backdrop` too), kept per viewer in localStorage (`approval-atlas:theme`, Auto removes it; every access in try/catch, the page works without it); the `color-scheme` meta tag ("light dark" under Auto) and both `theme-color` tags (the chosen scheme's colour on each; under Auto their own, kept in `data-device-content` once changed; `palette.test.js` checks they are each mode's `--page`) follow. Before the first paint: `public/theme-init.js`, a classic same-origin script in `<head>` before the stylesheet (the CSP allows no inline script, and a module runs after the first paint), sets all three from storage, so a chosen theme never shows the device's scheme first (no flash); `theme.js` (first in `main.js`) keeps them in step afterwards; `theme.test.js` runs the script against `theme.js`'s results. The service worker answers the script from its cache at once and refreshes it (it holds up the first paint; see Offline). Charts' colours are CSS `var()`s, so they follow at once; a press also re-renders (`scheduleRender()`), as after a resize.
- Display helpers: `formatDate()` shows every date as "8 Feb 2018" (data, URL and state keep ISO); answer headlines are `UI.headline` parts (`{ text, tone }` for the accent number / the "not" of "not authorized" / an `aside`, a closing qualifier on its own line in smaller type); the dashboard headline counts every matching medicine and those currently authorized (see "One dashboard"), is announced after filter edits (`#filter-result`) and is hidden while a lookup result is open; a medicine with an opinion but no decision is "not yet" authorized (pending tone), unless EMA's opinion was negative (step 2, #11: Kinselby, Qezzaqar, Meplyffa, Xervyteg; Yartemlea and KemSu under re-examination): then "not" in the negative tone (`UI.headline.medicine(name, kind, opinion)`, `opinion_status` once `ema_medicines.json` has loaded), the dek "Negative opinion on {date}: EMA recommended refusal." (a positive one "Positive opinion on {date}: EMA recommended approval.", copy review 2026-09-30) ("…; under re-examination at the company's request." for a re-examination), and "Opinion (negative)" in the Status block's pill and the search's meta line (`statusOpinionLabel()`); `badges.js` splits ATC codes into level segments (`atcSegments()`, malformed codes stay whole) and picks type badges.
- Medicines table: eight columns; first a status dot (owner decision 2026-09-30, the "status dot", in place of the "Approved · Status" column of before; `renderStatusDot()` in `table.js`; header "Status" visually hidden): a 12px mark in `statusColor()` (the per-year chart's status colours: Authorized `--status-authorized`, the others their hue's mid; ≥ 3:1 on page and surface, `palette.test.js`) with a shape per status kind (`statusShape()` in `badges.js`, from `statusKind()`; inline SVG in currentColor, so forced colors keep the shape in the text colour; WCAG 1.4.1: the kind is not told by colour alone, the status within a kind is, the tip and name say it): a filled circle authorized, a ring ended (withdrawn, expired, lapsed, suspended, revoked; unknown statuses), a half-filled circle pending (an opinion, a negative one too, and its re-examination), a cross never authorized (refused, application withdrawn, withdrawn from rolling review); in a 24px carrier (tabindex -1: a tap shows its tip, no tab stop) named by visually hidden text, `statusDotLine()` in `labels.js`: "Authorized since 6 Jan 2022" ("Authorized, no approval date"), else the status (`statusOpinionLabel()`: "Opinion (negative)") and its end date where EMA has one ("Withdrawn 16 Jan 2009", "Refused"; review of the status dot: not the approval date, the Approved cell's); its tooltip (a data-tip, `.tip-lines`) that line with the approval date of a status other than Authorized (`statusDotTipLine()`: "Withdrawn 16 Jan 2009 (approved 19 Jun 2006)") and, under it, the status's explanation (`statusTip(status, opinion)` in `badges.js`: `UI.statusTips`, a negative opinion's own), which also describes it (`aria-describedby` the hidden copies of `renderTypeTips()`, `status-tip-negative-opinion` for `NEGATIVE_OPINION`); on hover it waits the explainers' 600 ms pause (`.mesh-tip`, so sweeping down the column opens none per row; `.tap-tip`, so a tap still shows it) and opens 12px above the pointer (`.tip-pointer-above`, `atPointer(…, above)` in `tips.js`; below only without room above), as below it covered the row's PI and EPAR links and the first click on them only hid it. Then a narrow "Approved" column (width 1px, the date on one line): the approval date (an ended medicine's too), "No date" without one (`UI.table.noDate`), then, for every status but Authorized, the status and its end date as muted text (`.approved-status`, `--muted`; aria-hidden, the dot's name says it; review of the status dot, 2026-09-30, must-fix: keyboard and colour-blind readers had them only in the dot's tip, and within a kind the statuses differ by colour alone), so an authorized row, the default view, is the dot and date alone; then the Union Register marker (wrapping, 6px corners, not a pill). When the rows have a status other than Authorized, the caption ends with a key of the shapes present ("Status shapes: authorized, ended, awaiting decision, never authorized", `UI.table.shapesKey`, `renderShapesKey()`; the marks in `--ink-secondary`, aria-hidden, the words read). The expanded indication row starts at the Medicine column's text (54px since the design sweep's A1, 2026-10-01; 50px before) from 600px. The medicines table shows no approval qualifiers, as before. The result tables of the lookups keep their merged "Approved · Status" column (see "Condition pages"): their short lists read better with the status as a word, and their qualifier markers sit under its date. Type and orphan as badges; ATC as a segmented badge whose segments are filter buttons (phase 2). The medicine name opens our medicine card (`?med=`, pushState, as every lookup link; phase 4c, user feedback: it used to open EMA's page, unmarked); EMA's medicine page is the card's "EMA medicine page" link. Therapeutic area terms link to their condition page (below), each followed by its branch chips (see "Branch chips"). The caption is a paragraph above the scroll area (`#table-caption`, the table's `aria-describedby`; inside it, a wide table cut it off on phones). **Download CSV** (#18; the card head's button, described by the caption, and since F · Spacious, phase 1, the page header's at the right of the heading, hidden on phones, where the table's stays; both one look since the design sweep of 2026-10-01 (B2): `.more.download`, a download icon then the text, 14px, the `--input` fill; one handler, `downloadCsv()` in `main.js`): every medicine the table lists (all matching the filters, not only the pages shown), in its order, built by `medicinesCsv()` in `csv.js` (pure, tested on the real data) and saved through a Blob link (`saveFile()` in `main.js`; same origin, nothing sent): UTF-8 with a byte order mark (Excel), CRLF, RFC 4180 quoting; a first line `# Source: European Medicines Agency (EMA) medicines data as of {date} (…). © EMA. ATC codes © WHO Collaborating Centre for Drug Statistics Methodology (https://atcddd.fhi.no); not for commercial distribution. …` acknowledging EMA and the WHOCC (review of step 4: its terms require the reference, and the file travels without the footer) (one unquoted cell a tool can skip as a comment, so `UI.csv.source()` has no comma or quote); then the columns `UI.csv.headers`: EMA product number, Medicine, Active substances (EMA's field, else its INN field: 71 medicines have no active substance row), Status (as the site says it, `statusOpinionLabel()`), Approval date (ISO), Medicine type, Orphan (Yes/No), Company group, EMA holder name, ATC codes (`atcCode()`, never WHO's names), Therapeutic areas; values joined with `; `; a cell starting with = + - @, a tab or a carriage return gets an apostrophe in front (CSV injection, `csvCell()`). File name `approval-atlas-{data date}.csv`. Excel with a semicolon list separator (e.g. Swedish settings) opens it in one column: Data › From Text/CSV reads it. Column floors (companies part 2 review): Medicine 18ch, Company · Holder 9.25rem, Therapeutic area 10rem (`hyphens: auto`; a longer word widens it), so at 1440px with the 320px sidebar the first page (1,013px at min-content on 2026-09-28) fitted the 1024px scroll area without a sideways scroll; later pages can still hold wider words (every page loaded: 1,039px). Status dot (2026-09-30, 2026-09-29 data): the dot column 38px (14px, the 24px carrier), the Approved one 108px, the first page 1,020px at min-content (1,018px with "Approved · Status"); at 1440px (no sidebar since F · Spacious, the scroll area 1,198px) no sideways scroll; at 1024px (958px) the table scrolls 62px sideways inside its card, as it did before (60px; the table's min-width is 1,000px), never the page. Design sweep A1 (2026-10-01): the card's head, caption, "Show next" and rows on the other dashboard cards' text edge (`--card-pad`: 24px, 16px on phones; 20px before, so the title sat 4px inside the other cards' at 1440 and 4px inside its own stacked rows at 390), the dot's mark in line with them (the dot column 42px: 18px, the 24px carrier), the first page 1,028px at min-content, 70px of sideways scroll at 1024px. Narrow cards (`.table-card` is a size container; under 600px) stack each medicine: name (substances, PI/EPAR), holder (`order: -1`, as the dot cell comes first in the row), then the status dot with its status as text ("Authorized", else the Approved cell's status text, "Withdrawn 12 Jun 2026", which the stacked row leaves out; in the hue's text colour, aria-hidden: the dot's hidden name says it), "Approved {date}" ("Approved" aria-hidden: the column header says it; "No approval date" without a date), type and ATC side by side, the therapeutic areas and "Show indication" (the toggle names what it shows); no height limit, so no box scrolls both ways inside the page; explicit table roles keep the semantics. Wider, the header row is sticky in the scroll area and every focusable control of the body has `scroll-margin-top` of its height plus 8px (`--thead-h`, set by a ResizeObserver in `table.js`), so Shift+Tab never leaves focus under it (audit 2026-09-30, M2).
- **Condition pages** (the `?cond=` lookup; phase 4c, user feedback: hard to find): therapeutic area terms link there wherever they appear: the table's Therapeutic area cells (text links; the term's MeSH explainer once the notes have loaded, until then or without one its branch names as the name's tooltip (a data-tip since 2026-09-29, on the name only, `.term-name`, not over its branch chips), never both), the medicine card's areas, the conditions card (see "Conditions card"), and, as a small outlined link with a page icon after the row ("Open condition page: {name}", 24px, 44px on phones and in sheets; `.cond-link`, `openIcon()`; a page with a folded corner since the phase 4f review, not a chevron, which in tree rows is the expand button), the rows of the therapeutic area tree (phase 4f: a third grid column of the row, outside the `<label>`, so not part of the checkbox's name; a link followed from a popover or sheet closes it without handing focus back to the chip: `closeFilters()` in `main.js`) and the breakdown's therapeutic area bars (a term by its descriptor, a branch or tree node by the descriptor of its name: `buildConditions()` `uiByName`, once the conditions data has loaded, `areaDescriptor()` in `main.js`; each bar and its link in a `.bar-item`; none after "tagged at this level"). Condition, indication-text and substance results are tables (`resultTable()` in `lookup.js`, `UI.results.headers`): Medicine (the name opens its card, substances, PI/EPAR), ATC (display-only segmented badges; level names as tooltip and visually hidden, incomplete flagged), Approved · Status (dot + status, then the date; ended: "{end} · approved {approval}", `statusDateLine()`; the medicines table's status dot column is its own since 2026-09-30; step 4, #9: an authorized medicine's compact markers under its date, see "Approval qualifiers"), Type (badges), Holder; a matched indication snippet in a full-width row under its medicine; one `tbody` per medicine, named by the heading above (`aria-labelledby`). Phones (≤ 720px) stack each row (medicine; ATC codes beside the status; type badges; holder), the header visually hidden, explicit table roles keeping the semantics; the Type cell holds its tooltip (ends at the cell's right edge; on phones starts at the row's left edge: the badge stays static, raised above the phones' tap-target rims as a flex item of `.badges`; bug hunt 2026-10-01: the rims' rule made the badge the tip's containing block, 76px wide, a word a line; that rule leaves it out inside `:where()`, so it stays under the rule raising a carrier to z-index 5 while its tip shows, else every table tip goes under the next rows' controls: `result-table.test.js` compares their specificity). A condition page (phase 4c review: it overclaimed, "52 medicines are authorized for Psoriasis" counted leflunomide, tagged only "Arthritis, Psoriatic"): headline "{N} authorized medicines are tagged by EMA with {name} (or a narrower condition)." (EMA's tags, not indications; phase 4f: "or a narrower condition" only when some authorized medicine it counts is tagged only with a narrower one, i.e. the narrower list is not empty); the dek names the narrower conditions as condition links (the first 5, then "and {n} more"), then (phase 4f) both counts of the lists shown ("Authorized: 6 medicines tagged by EMA and 5 more mentioned in the indication text."; "Every status: …" with "Show all statuses"; `UI.condition.counts()`; step 4, #10: with the tagged medicines' distinct active substances, "Authorized: 47 medicines tagged by EMA (16 active substances or combinations) and 2 more …" for Arthritis, Rheumatoid, i.e. distinct substance sets with the `ema_substance_equivalents.json` spellings joined, a combination counting on its own, hence "or combinations" (review of step 4: HIV Infections has 40 sets of 27 substances), `substanceSetCount()` in `copies.js`, once those have loaded), then (owner request 2026-09-28) NLM's full scope note as a small paragraph, "MeSH definition: {note} Tree numbers C04.588.180, C17.800.090.500. From MeSH® (MeSH 2026), courtesy of the U.S. National Library of Medicine." (`meshDefinition()` in `lookup.js`, `UI.mesh`; the version from `meta.json`; none without a note); the headline's condition name is its own part (`UI.headline.condition()`, tone `term`) carrying the name's explainer (tabindex -1, so a tap shows it; the paragraph is the keyboard path); the tagged medicines are two lists, "Tagged by EMA with {name} ({n})" and "Tagged with a narrower condition ({n})", each row of the second saying which ("Tagged with Arthritis, Psoriatic"; `buildConditions()` `ownProducts`, `narrowerTerms`, `narrowerByProduct`). A substance card's table adds a "Therapeutic area" column after Medicine (what each brand is for; its terms with their branch chips, see "Branch chips") and leaves out the substance line where it is the card's own substance; with six columns it stacks its rows below a 780px card (`.with-areas`; the five-column tables below 600px, the container queries on `.result-table`; chips review 2026-09-29: its columns' minimum reaches 751px (elivaldogene autotemcel, 2026-09-28 data), so at 1024px with the 320px sidebar 237 of the 1,273 substance tables, 624 to 751px, crossed their 623px card and adalimumab's (667px) scrolled the page 8px sideways; now none at 1024, 1440 (320px and 560px sidebars), 390 or 320, and the five-column tables unchanged; its block repeats the five-column one, `result-table.test.js` keeps them alike; adalimumab's table at 1024 is 4,633px tall stacked, 7,204 as a table). Medicines found only through their indication text (not tagged by EMA with the condition or a narrower one; phase 4f, user decision) are the list "Also mentioned in indication text ({n})", each row saying which words matched, in the indication's own spelling ("Indication mentions “neuropathic pain”", `UI.condition.rowMentions()`; Neuralgia: Pregabalin Viatris Pharma, tagged Anxiety Disorders and Epilepsy only). The results timeline has a caption ("One dot per medicine; lines join medicines with the same active substances …"; step 4, #17: on condition and indication-text pages it adds "Dots show each medicine's first approval, not when this use was added to its indication.", `UI.timeline.firstApproval`; "this use", as an indication-text search is not a condition), draws those medicines as hollow dots (and says so in the dot's tooltip), with a legend of the kinds present on condition pages ("Tagged by EMA", a filled dot; "Mentioned in the indication", a ring; `.dot-legend`, `UI.timeline.legend`; forced colors keep them apart), and is left out below 3 dated medicines.
- **External links** (phase 4c, `links.js`): every link to another website (https only, `target="_blank"`, `rel="noopener noreferrer"`: PI/EPAR, the card's SmPC/EPAR buttons and documents list, "EMA medicine page", the register chip, Espacenet, the footer; not the operator's `mailto:` link, which is no website: `isWebLink()`) goes through `markExternal()`: an arrow-out-of-a-box icon (inline SVG, aria-hidden, `.link-icon`; in the SmPC/EPAR buttons after the document name), "(opens {destination} in a new tab)" after the link text for screen readers (visually hidden, or appended to an existing `aria-label`, e.g. "PI, product information PDF for Wegovy (opens EMA website in a new tab)") and a tooltip "{destination} ({host})". Destinations by host in `UI.external.destinations` (EMA website, European Commission website, Espacenet, GitHub, Creative Commons website; for the companies' evidence links every host of `companies.json`, `ema_medicine_companies.json` and `R/curated-companies.R` on 2026-09-28, e.g. "PR Newswire", "GlobeNewswire", "Business Wire", "ClinicalTrials.gov", "SEC website", "Pfizer website"; another host is named by itself: `destinationOf()`, tested; add a name when the data brings a new host). The footer's and About's links are built by `renderFooter()` (`main.js`) from the `UI.footer` / `UI.about` parts and marked there; `links.test.js` checks index.html's links (https, new tab, noopener noreferrer) and that every footer and About link is https with a named destination.
- **Footer and About** (legal review 2026-09-30, `.remember/legal/footer-review.md`, not legal advice; `UI.footer`, `UI.about`, `renderFooter()` in `main.js`; parts are text or `{ text, url }` links built in the DOM, never HTML): the footer is three lines, (1) "Sources:" EMA (linked to its data page, © EMA, data as of {date}) · MeSH® courtesy of the U.S. National Library of Medicine ({MeSH version}) · ATC © WHOCC (linked) · Union Register (linked) © European Union, CC BY 4.0 (linked), modified · ChEMBL (linked, {release}), CC BY-SA 3.0 (linked): every third-party credit visible and equally prominent (CC BY-SA 3.0: ChEMBL's credit as prominent as the others; CC BY 4.0 and CC BY-SA 3.0 require the licence link); (2) "For information only: not medical, legal or regulatory advice. Not affiliated with or endorsed by EMA, the European Commission, WHO or its Collaborating Centre, NLM, EMBL-EBI, GLEIF or Anthropic." (Anthropic since the pivotal results, 2026-09-30); (3) data CC BY-SA 4.0, code MIT, no cookies or tracking, run by Viktor Rognås, "Contact: viktor@vrognas.com" (a mailto link; owner decision 2026-09-30). "About this site" (the `<details>` above them) has run-in `<h3>` heads (heading navigation; styled inline): What this is (a free, non-commercial personal project covering the medicines that went through EMA's central procedure, whatever their status, rebuilt daily, run by Viktor Rognås (Sweden); owner decision 2026-09-30, GDPR Art. 13, footer review §2b), Scope, Use with care (not meant for decisions about any patient's care, so not a medical device (MDCG 2019-11); does not recommend any medicine; data processed automatically and partly by hand, can lag or contain errors, provided as is; protection dates are rough estimates, not patent or SPC data, not legal advice; company groups as curated, not an official record), Sources and licenses (a list: EMA; the Union Register, CC BY 4.0, modified; MeSH with its version; ATC codes and names © WHOCC, verbatim in the data, shown in title case, not for commercial distribution, the class explanations ours when credited; ChEMBL with release, licence and the Mendez et al. 2019 citation (molecule types, and ATC names mapped); the modality sources; GLEIF, CC0, no endorsement; Geist fonts © The Geist Project Authors, SIL OFL 1.1; the site's own licences; trademarks), Privacy (GitHub logs visitors' IP addresses and requested page addresses; what this browser keeps: theme, intro seen or closed, recently viewed, the offline cache; clearing site data removes them) and Contact (viktor@vrognas.com as a mailto link, then the repository's issues and the security policy; owner decision 2026-09-30; SECURITY.md and README have no contact slot, so they are unchanged). An email link is no other website: `appendParts()` in `main.js` opens a `mailto:` url in place, without a new tab or `markExternal()` (`isWebLink()` in `links.js`, pure and tested; `links.test.js` allows only the operator's email besides https links in the footer and About). Storage (owner decision 2026-09-30: `intro-seen` stays stored automatically, and disclosed in the privacy line). The storage next to its control: the theme button's tooltip says "(remembered on this device)" when its press stores a choice (not when it returns to Auto), the intro ×'s always, the recent list "Recently viewed (kept on this device)". Before `meta.json` loads the footer shows no date or versions.
- **Quick PDF links** (phase 4a): every medicine row's Medicine cell, in the lookup's result tables and the medicines table (under the substances), has two compact external links, "PI" (the current product information PDF) and "EPAR" (the latest standard public assessment report PDF): `quickDocuments()` in `documents.js` = the URLs of `primaryDocuments()` (its SmPC and EPAR: the overview is a card button only, step 4), https only; a missing document has no link. Both take the medicine's EMA status: a medicine never authorized (`statusKind()` refused or pending) has no PI link, and only a refused one an EPAR: its refusal report (older ones carry the standard title); EMA's documents index can list an authorized namesake's documents under it (Mylotarg EMEA/H/C/000705, refused, gets 004204's), so the card's buttons follow the same rule and its documents list leaves the namesake's out (see "Medicine card and lookup"). 44px targets on phones. Names put the visible text first (WCAG 2.5.3): "PI, product information PDF for Wegovy", "EPAR, European public assessment report PDF for Wegovy" (`UI.documentLinks`, `documentLinks()` in `lookup.js`), then the new-tab note (see "External links"). The links and the card's buttons come from the primary documents (`ema_medicine_primary_documents.json`, the lookup's `need("primaryDocuments")`, loaded with the first card or list and, for the table, after the dashboard's first render; the documents index where it is missing); the table adds the links in place when they arrive (no rebuild, pages shown are kept). The documents index (~5 MB, 301 KB gzipped) is the lookup's `need("documents")` dataset, for the documents list only: loaded when a card's More details opens, at once for a card whose buttons the primary documents cannot give (a medicine with no button: its list is the Documents block; one with a later namesake, whose documents `splitNamesakeDocuments()` leaves out), and once the page is idle (`whenIdle()`, so the lists work offline after one visit; so a visit that is not a `?med=` link still loads the full index then, and the small file's about 75 KB gzipped comes on top); once loaded, the card takes everything from it.
- **Medicine card and lookup** (phase 4c review): order for a talk: answer headline, status sentence (none for an authorized medicine since 2026-09-30: its qualifiers are the chips beside its status, see "Approval qualifiers"; a positive opinion adds "The EU decision usually comes about {n} days after the opinion.", #12, from `meta.json` `opinion_to_decision.median_days`, and, when the days from its opinion to the data's date are more than that, "This one has waited {n} days so far." (review of step 4: 9 of the 21 pending on 2026-09-28), with "; 9 in 10 decisions of the last 5 years came sooner" past `p90_days` (Nylaspeg and Onswik, 95 days); passed to `createLookup()` as `decision` (`{ median, p90 }`), `statusSentence(…, { decision, asOf })`, left out in older data), any namesake ("Another medicine named Mylotarg (EMEA/H/C/004204) has been authorized since 19 Apr 2018.", a link to its card; any medicine with the same folded name), then (F · Spacious, phase 4; see "Medicine card in blocks", which supersedes this order where they differ) the Status block (status pill, since when, qualifiers, the status sentence, company), the Documents block with the SmPC / EPAR / overview buttons (step 4, #15: "Product information (SmPC and package leaflet)", "Public assessment report (EPAR)" and "Plain-language overview", EMA's medicine overview or older "Summary for the public", the newest one; `UI.card.buttons`, `PRIMARY` in `documents.js`, never for a medicine never authorized; side by side from 721px, as many as fit at 13rem; at 320px the product information's name takes two lines, no overflow; the documents list keeps `UI.documents`, since the copy review 2026-09-30 with the same names, "Public assessment report (EPAR)" and "Plain-language overview" (owner call: 1,081 of EMA's 1,973 overview rows are titled "Medicine overview")), then the Protection and copies block (see "Protection and copies on the medicine card"), then the Indication block: the indication's lead (`indicationLead()` in `labels.js`: the whole text up to 200 characters, else its first sentence, else 200 characters cut at a word, with "Show full indication"; what it is for is part of the answer, so it stays outside More details) and the first three therapeutic areas with their branch chips; then More details: every therapeutic area, substances, company notes, type, modality and ATC ladders, the protection estimate and every document. A medicine never authorized with a namesake approved later leaves out the documents first published from that approval on (`splitNamesakeDocuments()` in `documents.js`; EMA lists 004204's under the refused 000705) and says "{n} later documents EMA lists here belong to the other Mylotarg." Enter in the search without an option picked opens the suggestion the text names (folded label, or a class code) or the only suggestion (`submitChoice()` in `search.js`: "wegovy" + the phone's Search key opens Wegovy's card), else the indication-text search (see "Search"). The wordmark links to the overview (every lookup and filter cleared, pushState, focus to the headline); since phase 4f it starts with the site icon (user decision: option C, "Check-A monogram": an off-white A with a gold check on an accent square, 64×64 viewBox), drawn inline in `index.html` (`.brand-icon`, 28px, aria-hidden; the link is named "Approval Atlas, home"). The same drawing is `public/icon.svg` (favicon, manifest icon; the service worker caches it as before), with raster copies rendered once from it in headless Chrome (2026-09-29, owner request: the live site had no `/favicon.ico` (404), which clients that do not read the page or cannot draw SVG ask for): `public/favicon.ico` (16, 32 and 48 px PNGs in one ICO, linked first with `sizes="32x32"` so the SVG wins where supported; cached by the service worker like the SVG), `icon-192.png`, `icon-512.png` and `icon-maskable-512.png` (the glyph at 80% on the accent, inside the maskable safe zone) in `manifest.webmanifest` (named "Approval Atlas") and, full bleed at 180px, `public/apple-touch-icon.png` (rendered once from the SVG in a browser canvas, no build dependency; iOS rounds the corners); theme colours unchanged. `document.title` names the view ("Wegovy · Approval Atlas", "Psoriasis · …", "“wegovy” · …", "L04AC Interleukin Inhibitors · …"; WCAG 2.4.2). While a lookup result is open the dashboard below is headed "Explore EMA medicines" (owner decision 2026-09-29: "all" went, as it shows the authorized ones by default) with "The filters apply to this overview, not to the result above." Below 1024px the search section is sticky at the top (a compact bar on the page colour across the gutters; the Try line moved out of it; `scroll-padding-top` keeps focused elements clear of it).
- **Search** (step 2 of the 2026-09-28 roadmap, findability; pure and tested in `search.js`, list in `main.js` `searchSuggestions()`, `search-box.js`): words split letters from digits in names and queries alike (`searchWords()`: "glp1" = "GLP-1" = "glp 1"), a typed word's parts kept together (`queryWords()`); a typed word matches where its parts are consecutive words of the name, the parts before its last whole words (`matchesWords()`, step 2 review: "h1n1" no longer lists H5N1 vaccines, "b12" no longer opens Hepacare by "hepatitis B" + "C127I", nor "a1c" Clopidogrel 1A Pharma), its last part a prefix ("hum" finds Humira as it is typed, "hep b" hepatitis B, "ins gla" insulin glargine), or a whole word when it is under 4 characters and the next typed word is a number ("il 17", "pd 1": "IL-17" no longer finds Lutetium Billev (previously Illuzyce) by "il…" + "177…"); conditions keep every short word whole. "car t" lists C01 Cardiac Therapy again, as before step 2 (a short word before a letter stays a prefix, for "hep b"). When the typed query finds nothing, `searchWithFallback()` retries `relaxedQueries()`: without dose, form and qualifier words (mg, ml, mcg, µg with the numbers right before them; pen, tablet(s), injection, solution, vaccine, biosimilar, generic, drug, pill and plurals; a leading "anti") ("Ozempic 1 mg" → "ozempic", "HPV vaccine" → "hpv"), then also without the last word when at least 4 characters remain ("Keytruda melanoma", "adalimumab-atto"); the list then says "Showing results for “ozempic”" (a note above the options, `aria-hidden`, as the status element announces it with the count), and Enter compares labels with that query. Still nothing: the note says "No matches", or, when the query is a WHO level-5 name no medicine in the data has (`knownSubstance()`, from `atc_classes.json`), "Paracetamol (ATC N02BE01): no medicine with it went through EMA's central procedure, but it may be authorized nationally." (step 2 review: EMA also reviews nationally authorized medicines in referrals; announced with the count after one period, `UI.lookup.status()`); then "Did you mean" (`didYouMean()`: Damerau-Levenshtein (optimal string alignment, `editDistance()`) within 1 edit for 4–6 characters, 2 when longer, of the query without dose and form words; candidates: each medicine's first word and whole name, substance keys, WHO level-5 names of substances no medicine has (not at distance 0); at most 3, closest, authorized, substance before medicine before WHO name, then name; a medicine named after a substance found is left to it; a WHO name, "ATC N02BE01 · not in EMA's central procedure", runs the text search for it). **Background data** (bug hunt 2026-10-01, lookup.md #2: on slow Wi-Fi the conditions, drug classes and companies came 1.5–2.5 s after the search, which said "No matches" for "psoriasis", "L04AC", "msd", and Enter ran a text search): while one of their datasets loads (`lookup.searchLoading()`: conditions, atc, atcCounts, companies; a failed one is asked for again after a new view), the list ends with a "Loading…" line under its options (`UI.lookup.loading`, `.listbox-end`: where what comes will show; announced while nothing is found), says no "No matches" yet and shows the close names it can guess from the search index ("Did you mean: Keytruda"; review: they waited for the data); Enter opens a suggestion the query names at once (`submitChoice(…, { onlyNamed })`: "humira"), else waits (the list shown, "Loading…" announced), trying again as each dataset arrives (`refresh()`: "psoriasis" opens with the conditions, "msd" with the companies), and chooses as usual once all have arrived or failed (`lookup.searchSettled()`, `datasets.js` `settled()`, settled after their listeners ran), unless the text changed, another Enter, pick or Escape came, the list was dismissed (`close()`: a tap or click outside, Tab; review: a tap outside still opened the condition page 0.8 s later) or a history entry was pushed or popped meanwhile (main.js `navigations`). No time limit (review: a 3 s one ran the text search for "msd" on Wi-Fi a little slower than 1.6 Mbps; at 0.6 Mbps Enter waits 3.5–5.9 s): the list stays open meanwhile, its text search a tap away. The drug classes' counts (`atcCounts`: ladders, class suggestions) come from the search index (`isListedAuthorizedNow()`, as `isAuthorizedNow()`, a data-file test keeps them alike) and `ema_medicine_atc_codes.json`, no longer `ema_medicines.json` (515 KB gzipped, the last of them to arrive: 8.6 s against `atc_classes.json`'s 5.5 s at 1.6 Mbps, the search usable at 3.2 s). Nothing the open list shows moves until the next keystroke sorts it as usual (`keepShownList()` in `search.js`, pure and tested; review of the bug hunt: "Roche" (company) came above Bondenza, 70px down, "msd" put Sanofi's company page where Vorinostat MSD had been, and the "Loading…" note, above the options then, moved them up when it went): data arriving (`refresh()`), Enter's wait and "Show 5 more" keep its groups, their options and its note in place (texts as they read now: a negative opinion, a count), a group gone now as shown, the active option too; new groups come under them, where the "Loading…" line was, after the indication-text search too, even one the query names (Psoriasis, L04AC, Roche; Enter still opens it), and so does a note the list did not show ("Paracetamol (ATC N02BE01): …"), but "No matches", which the status says and a "Did you mean" heading shows. The press protection (no rebuild while an option is pressed) is unchanged. Tab closes the list before focus moves (review: Chrome let a scrolling list, "hum", take focus, which the field's blur then hid, leaving focus on the page). Each suggestion's meta counts the medicines with status Authorised ("11 authorized"; they counted every status until the bug hunt, lookup.md #8); a substance under all its spellings, as its card counts (`substanceAuthorizedCount()` in `copies.js`; review: "dasatinib" and "dasatinib (anhydrous)" said 1 each, either card 2), once `ema_substance_equivalents.json` has loaded (asked for with the search), its own spelling's until then. The list scrolls without chaining to the page (`overscroll-behavior: contain`, lookup.md #10). **Phones** (≤ 720px; Laws of UX, second pass, owner decision 2026-09-30, Hick's Law: with the keyboard up the list held up to 8 options in each of 5 groups): each suggestion group (medicines, substances, conditions, drug classes, companies) shows at most 3 options, those the query names first (named, or a label or class code equal to it, as `submitChoice()` reads them), then "Show 5 more medicines" (what the click adds: the group's options less the 3 shown, singular "1 more medicine"; not "all", as a group holds at most `MAX_SUGGESTIONS`, 8, and more can match, review of PR #39: "hum" matches 10 medicine names; `UI.lookup.showMore()`, link colour, `.option-expand`), an option like the others (arrow keys, Enter, a tap), which expands that group in place: the list stays open, focus stays in the field, the first option it added becomes the active one (`aria-activedescendant`) and "Showing 5 more medicines" is announced (`UI.lookup.expanded()`); options are matched to their listed group (`listGroup`), not to the group they open as (a "did you mean" option's `pick`); a group one longer than the limit is shown whole; "Did you mean", the indication-text search and Recently viewed are never collapsed; typing again collapses every group (`search.js` `collapseGroups()`, pure and tested, `PHONE_GROUP_LIMIT`; `search-box.js`). The announced count still counts every suggestion, and Enter without a picked option reads the whole list (`submitChoice()`), so "wegovy" + Search opens the card as before.
