Desktop unchanged. **Recently viewed** (2026-09-29; resuming unfinished work, `recent.js` pure and tested, `search-box.js` option `recent`): while the field is focused and empty (on focus, after deleting the text, or ArrowDown), the list is one group "Recently viewed (kept on this device)" (legal review 2026-09-30) (a `role="group"` labelled by its heading, options as the other suggestions', `aria-activedescendant` on the input; status "Recently viewed: 3 items"): the last 5 medicines, substances, conditions, companies and drug classes the viewer opened, newest first (a medicine, substance, condition or company by any route: search, links, a shared URL; a class only when a lookup opened it, `navigate()` with `classState()`: the Drug classes suggestion, a ladder, the Try link, a recent pick; review 2026-09-29: drilling the ATC breakdown stored every level, and a filter to one class is no lookup: `openedClass()`, `keptOpenedClass()`, a drill chain tested; `classState()` also sets `tab=classes` since F · Spacious phase 2, a view that `openedClass()` ignores as it compares `url.js` `FILTER_KEYS` only; a tab switch keeps the class shown and records nothing new; named by the class alone, as `dashboard.title()` (the document title adds the tab, `tabName()`)), each its name with its kind as the meta ("Medicine", "Active substance", "Condition", "Company", "Drug class"; `UI.lookup.recent`), the view shown now left out, and so are entries whose value no longer resolves (`lookup.title()` of `recentLookupState()` null: a medicine or substance gone from the search index, a condition or company once their data has loaded; a class is kept; they stay stored and age out); the open list is not rebuilt when background data arrives (`refresh()`), so its active option and announcement stay; picking one opens it as the suggestion of that group does (main.js `PICKS`). Recorded from `updateTitle()` once the view is named (`recentEntry()`: kind = the search's group key, value = the URL state's, label = the tab title's name; an indication-text search, the overview and filters are not recorded), when the view changes, not on every render (so a view left open after Clear does not come back by itself); a view opened again moves to the top under its newest name (`addRecent()`: deduped by kind and value, at most 5). Last option "Clear" (named "Clear recently viewed", link colour under a hairline; `action: "clear"`; on phones, ≤ 720px, every suggestion is 44px tall, its text centred): empties the list, closes it, keeps focus in the field and announces "Recently viewed cleared". Kept per device in localStorage (`approval-atlas:recent`, a JSON list of `{ kind, value, label }`; read afresh when the list opens, so another tab's views show; stored text is not trusted: `parseRecent()` keeps only valid entries of known kinds, strings of at most 200 characters, first copy, at most 5; removed when cleared; every access in try/catch, kept for the visit when storage is blocked); it never leaves the device (no request carries it); the About disclosure's privacy line says so. Every list ends, under a hairline, with "Search indication texts for “…”" (group `text`, no visible heading, named "Indication text search"; the typed text; link colour). Enter never opens a "did you mean" or text option, nor counts them as the only suggestion; named: a condition's exact entry term ("ADHD"), a substance's other name, a company's curated monogram only (companies.json `monogram_source`; a derived monogram ("ALL", "CAR", "TB", "GLP") still finds its group, ranked last and `weak`: never opened as the only suggestion; rows without the field count as derived). Other names of a substance (`SPELLING_VARIANTS`, 12 BAN/USAN/common names → EMA's INN, each checked against the data by a test: adrenaline → epinephrine, aspirin → acetylsalicylic acid, cyclosporine → ciclosporin, …): substance suggestions add the target's with "matches “adrenaline”" in the meta (named by the other name), and the text search also looks for the target (`textPhrases()`, also "glp1" as "glp 1"; dek "Also searching for “acetylsalicylic acid”, the name EMA uses."). An indication-text search with nothing to show among the statuses shown and no related condition says why instead of "None." (`emptyState()` in `lookup.js`, `UI.lookup.empty`): a lead (matches of another status: "… (Show all statuses)"; a medicine or substance name matches: "No indication text mentions “Humira”." with "Matching names:" links; a WHO substance, as the note, with its level-4 class as a link when that class has medicines authorized now (A02BC yes, N02BE no); else "Nothing in the EMA data matches “…”."), "Did you mean:" links, and, when no name matched, what can be searched, what not yet (development codes, brand names used outside the EU) and what is not in the data (national-only medicines: EMA's national registers page, an external link; EU-wide packs carry an EU/1/… number).
- **Protection and copies on the medicine card** (step 3, #7 and #8; `copies.js` and `protection.js` pure and tested; never "patent", estimates only): the Protection and copies block's lead (F · Spacious, phase 4; the answer strip's 4th cell "Protection (est.)" before; medicines with an approval date; `protectionLead()` in `lookup.js`, the value in the answer's type, then "(est.)" after a medicine's own estimate, not a copy's (`isCopy()` in `protection.js`); `protectionGlance()`: "Market protection until 2031–2032" (the market protection range's years while protected), "Market protection ended" or "Market protection unclear" (Laws of UX, second pass, owner decision 2026-09-30: "Until 2031–2032" alone read as "generics from 2031"; no "(not patents)", the estimate's section says so; `UI.protection.glance`), "Market protection" in the body type before the link (aria-hidden, a hidden copy in the link, so the link is named by the whole phrase) and the rest in the answer's type as the link, so it takes two lines at most on a 320px phone; on a copy (basis `follows_reference` or `reference_not_found`, curated copies included; morning QA 2026-09-29) "Follows {reference}" with, while the reference is protected, a muted "{reference}'s protection until … (est.)" line, or "None of its own", and the chip "Data/market protection: None of its own", never the reference's protection as the copy's own; then, muted on its own line, "Orphan exclusivity until 2033 (est.)" while one runs (its latest end, the register's on a tie; "(est.)" unless that end is the Union Register's, see "Estimated protection ending")) is a link to the protection section, in More details (`#protection`, its heading, focusable, `data-focus-key`; the click opens More details, focuses it and scrolls its section into view instead of changing the URL's hash; its name adds ", see the estimate in More details"; `a.lead-link`, 44px tall on phones, a negative margin keeping the row). Data arriving after the click (step 3 review: on slow Wi-Fi the 5 MB documents index renders above the section) re-renders the card, and `render()` scrolls the focused section back into view while that jump is pending (until the next view, or a wheel, touch or key press). In the block, under the lead, counted by the medicine's substance set with equivalent spellings joined (`ema_substance_equivalents.json`, hand-checked pairs; `substanceEquivalents()` also joins chains; `equivalentSetKey()`, `setGroups()`; R names Vysribli's substance denosumab in the search index, so the frontend has no rule of its own for it), never by the protection estimate's reference product (it names the set's first approval, so counting by it would give Xgeva none: its biosimilars follow Prolia; Humira's follow Humira since step 3's tie-break, counted from Trudexa's 1 Sep 2003): on a medicine that is no copy and has an approval date, its authorized generics and biosimilars (copies EMA does not flag, checked by hand, count as their `ema_curated_copies.json` `copy_type`: Herceptin's biosimilars include Tuznue, Bridion's generics Sugammadex Adroiq; a curated hybrid is neither, even where EMA flags it Generic (Riulvy), and every curated copy's own card reads as a copy's; `copiesSummary()` `curatedCopies`) with their company groups and the first ("10 biosimilars from 8 companies, first Amgevita 21 Mar 2017.", the name a link to its card; "No generic or biosimilar authorized yet.", left out on an advanced therapy, where it reads oddly (owner's decision 2026-09-29); `copiesSummary()`, `copiesLinePlan()`, `UI.copies.line()`), named as the substance's when the medicine is not its set's first (step 3 review: "1 generic of ruxolitinib from 1 company, first Ruxolitinib Viatris 18 Sep 2026." on Opzelura, a generic of Jakavi); a hybrid (EMA type Other; R's curated copies give it basis `follows_reference`, or `reference_not_found` when its reference is not a central one) is a copy here (`followsReference()`; step 4: Liraglutide STADA, a hybrid of Victoza, said the originator's "No generic or biosimilar authorized yet."; now "3 other authorized medicines have the same active substance (liraglutide); first central approval 30 Jun 2009 (Victoza).", Colchicine Agepha Pharma "No other authorized medicine has the same active substance (colchicine)."); on a copy's card, or when the set was first approved as another medicine more than 30 days before (Wegovy: Ozempic; a twin within 30 days, another brand of the same application, is not named and the copies read as the medicine's own: Humira's Trudexa, Plavix's Iscover; owner's decision 2026-09-29), "{N} other authorized medicines have the same active substance (semaglutide); first central approval 8 Feb 2018 (Ozempic)." (`UI.copies.same()`: the substance a link to its card when it is one, the medicine to its card), so "Since 2024" does not read as a new substance; a first approval no longer authorized says so (owner request 2026-09-28: Qdenga's "No other authorized medicine …; first central approval … (Dengvaxia)" read against itself): "…; the first central approval was Dengvaxia (12 Dec 2018), since withdrawn." (its EMA status from the search index, `statusKind()` not authorized). That first approval (`firstApprovalShown()`) is the set's first dated medicine before this one, with its own date, named by the protection row's reference only when that was approved the same day: R names one brand of the first approval's company approved within 30 days and `counted_from` is the first approval's date, not always the brand's own (step 3 review: Humira's rows count from Trudexa's 1 Sep 2003, Iscover's from its own 14 Jul 1998 while naming Plavix, approved a day later), so the card never pairs a name with another medicine's date and names nothing when this medicine came first. The lines wait for the equivalents, companies and protection files (a missing equivalents file, older data: no equivalents). The protection section: the heading with its chip, then "Estimated from EU central (EMA) approval dates only; earlier national authorizations are not counted." (`UI.protection.basisNote`, moved out of the collapsed caveats; these say the legal basis comes from EMA's generic and biosimilar flags and, for copies EMA does not flag, from EPAR pages checked by hand, and PUMAs from their public assessment reports, copies not yet checked counting as medicines of their own, and that pediatric rewards other than a PUMA's own protection are ignored), then, for basis `other_company_reference` (step 3, #6), the reason ("The first central EU approval of ruxolitinib was another company's medicine (Jakavi, 23 Aug 2012); counted from this company's own first approval (19 Apr 2023), protection would end later, so the market protection range covers both.", `UI.protection.otherCompany()`, with `own_counted_from`; no "Unclear:" lead, as the status is protected where both estimates are, e.g. Qdenga), then the dates as before, data exclusivity as a range where R gives its end counted from the company's own first approval ("Data exclusivity ends (est.) between 15 Mar 2018 and 18 Nov 2028", MenQuadfi; `data_exclusivity_end_max`, `UI.protection.dataExclusivityRange()`; one date when it is null or the same); "Counted from the first central EU approval of {substance}: {name}, {date}" names a medicine approved on `counted_from` (`countedFromName()`: the reference, else this medicine, else the set's first; the date alone when none is known). A curated copy (`copy_source` `curated`; step 4 review) of a central reference says what it is, from its `ema_curated_copies.json` row, with the same "Source" link as one with a national reference ("No protection of its own; a hybrid of Tecfidera.", Riulvy; "a generic of Bridion.", Sugammadex Adroiq; `UI.protection.curatedFollows()`; "the dates below are for its reference medicine, {name}" without that row). It is counted as its reference is, whose substance can be another, so its line names the reference, never its own substance (Riulvy, tegomil fumarate: "Counted from the approval of its reference medicine, Tecfidera: 30 Jan 2014"; `UI.protection.countedFromReference()`), or, when the reference counts from an earlier medicine, both ("As for its reference medicine Saxenda, counted from the first central EU approval of liraglutide: Victoza, 30 Jun 2009", Ablymico; `countedAsReference()`, the name from the reference's own estimate: `countedFromOf()` in `lookup.js`, never from the copy's set); one with a national reference (`reference_not_found`) names it from its `ema_curated_copies.json` row, with a "Source" link to the EMA page that says so ("No protection of its own; a hybrid of Subutex (authorized nationally, so no EU central date to count from).", Buprenorphine Neuraxpharm; `UI.protection.nationalReference()`), the generic "its reference medicine was not found among EU central authorizations" line without that row (older data: the file loads with the protection files and is optional, `{ optional }` in `lookup.js` `DATASETS`). The substance card (#8): each other spelling of the same substance ("Also listed as dasatinib (anhydrous): 3 medicines, first central approval 20 Nov 2006 (Sprycel).", the spelling a link to its card; `siblingSubstances()`, `UI.substance.sibling()`) under the dek. The answer (headline, dek, Status block: status pill, "since", company) describes the substance under all its spellings (`substanceGroup()`; step 3 review: "dasatinib" said "Since 26 Jul 2024" under "First central EU approval: 20 Nov 2006 (Sprycel)"; now "Dasatinib is in 2 medicines authorized EU-wide through EMA.", the "2 authorized" pill, "first approved 20 Nov 2006", 2 companies); the timeline and the list stay this spelling's, the list headed "1 medicine listed as dasatinib" (`UI.substance.productsListed()`) when there are other spellings.
- **Type explanations** (phase 4a; Other since the phase 4c review, the largest type: "Not a generic, biosimilar or advanced therapy." (step 3, #7: no longer "e.g. a new active substance", wrong for Wegovy, Rybelsus and Kyinsu); on the medicine card, the type facet row and the Medicine type chip): Orphan, Biosimilar, Generic and Advanced therapy explain themselves in at most 12 words (`UI.typeTips`) wherever they appear: type badges (medicines table, result tables), compact tiles, the type rows and the Medicine type chip when it names one type (`sentenceParts()` `tip`, read by `filterChips()`); the medicine card's Type fact shows each badge with its explanation as visible text (`explainedTypes()` in `lookup.js`). A CSS tooltip from `data-tip` (`::after { content: attr(data-tip) / "" }`, generated only while shown, so it never widens the page, and left out of accessible names) on hover and keyboard focus (`:focus-visible`, `:has(:focus-visible)`); on touch screens (`hover: none`) a tap shows it through `:focus-within`: badges get `tabindex="-1"` (focusable by tap, no tab stop), the four compact tiles `tabindex="0"` (the keyboard path to the Orphan explanation, which has no facet). **At the pointer** (owner decision 2026-09-29, "the hovers are not attached to the cursor": every hover tip, i.e. type, status, qualifier and flag badges and pills, MeSH and modality explainers (the trees' rows too, which opened beside the sidebar until F · Spacious), legend entries, breakdown bars and the charts' tooltips): on mouse hover a tip opens 12px below and right of where the pointer entered its carrier (a MeSH explainer: where the pointer rests when its 600 ms pause ends, following it until then), inside the viewport less 8px and within the scroll box that holds it (`.table-scroll`, `.activity-scroll`) less 8px, flipped left or above where it does not fit, else kept inside (`atPointer()`, `tipBounds()` in `tips.js`, pure and tested); it then stays put (another carrier places it anew). Fixed (`.tip-at-pointer`, `--pointer-tip-x`/`-y` set by `setupTips()`; `!important`, so it wins over every anchored placement below), relative to its fixed containing block, which a probe element finds (`.tip-probe`; the viewport on 2026-09-29, the size containers set none). Keyboard focus and touch taps, and every tip on a touch screen (`hover: none`), keep the anchored placement below; focus a mouse click gives leaves a tip at the pointer. When the pointer leaves the carrier and its tip (not on its way to the tip), the tip leaves the pointer's place, back to its carrier while that has keyboard focus (`unpoint()` in `setupTips()`; review 2026-09-29: a focused carrier the mouse passed over kept its tip, fixed, where the pointer had been, through scrolls too). The charts' tooltips (Approvals per year, Authorized over time, the results timeline's hover) take no pointer events and follow the pointer at the same offset and bounds (`placeTooltip()` in `chart.js`; `width: max-content`, so none wraps past its chart's edge); on a touch screen a tap on Authorized over time (a touch or pen press released within 10px, `isTap()` in `over-time.js`, tested) shows the month tapped, as a hover does, until a tap elsewhere (a document pointerdown, one listener per chart container: `onPressOutside()` in `chart.js`) or a pan starting on the chart (bug hunt 2026-10-01: a tap fires no pointermove and the pointer leaves on release, so it showed nothing); on Medicines by year of approval a tap shows that year's numbers with "Tap again to show only 2016." ("… every year." when it is the one year shown; `UI.years.tapAgain()`), until a press outside the chart, and a second tap on that year sets the filter as a click does (`yearTapAction()`, `isTouch()` in `year-brush.js`, tested; the brush keeps the filter's range meanwhile; a finger moving more than 10px does neither, `isTouchTap()`: a vertical swipe, which the brush keeps from scrolling the page, set the year under it before; fix-up: a tap set the filter and showed nothing, and its compat mouse click, reaching the brush drawn anew, often set it back, so the touch end is now cancelled); on either chart a mouse moving over it takes over from a tap (`keepsTap()` in `chart.js`, tested; fix-up, a touch laptop: the tapped month stayed, stale, after the mouse left); a click on the timeline dot the hover shows pins its tip where it is (a tap: at the dot, as before); a tap or click outside the chart closes a pinned tip, and one on the tip outside its link acts on the dot under it, else closes it (`dotClick()` in `timeline-layout.js`; bug hunt 2026-10-01: the tip covered dots and the list below, and only a tap in the chart closed it). The approval-years strip's bars keep their native `<title>` tooltips (the browser's, at the cursor). **One look for every explanation** (owner feedback 2026-09-29: an ATC badge's native `title` looked and behaved unlike the Orphan badge's tip): the explanations that were native titles are data-tips too: the ATC badges of the medicines table and the result tables (one text for both, `atcBadgeTip()` in `atc.js`: the level names, why the code is incomplete, how it differs from EMA's, the source, a line each: `.tip-lines`, `white-space: pre-line`, as wide as the MeSH explainers; on the `.code` carrier with tabindex -1, so a tap on it shows it anchored, a tap on a medicines table segment too, once it has filtered (the table puts focus back on the segment, `:focus-within`; review of PR #15: most badges are all segments, so a tap had nowhere else to show it; it goes when focus moves or on Escape), and keyboard focus on a segment; the segment buttons, their clicks and the toolbar's one tab stop are unchanged, and a click on the tip or its bridge where they lie over a segment reaches the segment, as for any tip at the pointer), the medicine card's "incomplete" chip, the medicines table's Union Register flag, the conditions card's medicines cells and the medicines table's terms' MeSH branch names until the notes load (a term's fallback, on its name only, not over its branch chips, with the explainers' pause), the EMA holder name's "The holder name as EMA publishes it", the company breakdown bars' EMA holder names and the activity table's filter toggles ("Show only …", after a row's EMA holder names; a static column's full name on its header text); the bars and toggles with the explainers' pause (`.mesh-tip`: 600 ms on hover, no tip on a tap). Their buttons' names and descriptions already carry that text, so none adds an `aria-describedby`. The activity table's row header whose tip shows is stacked over the sticky row headers below it (`z-index: 2`). Kept native (`title`): external links' destinations ("EMA website (www.ema.europa.eu)": they repeat the link's screen-reader note), the icon links' names ("Open condition page: …", "Open company page: …": an icon-only control's name, as its `aria-label`, after bars and activity rows whose own explainer is a data-tip; none inside a tree row that has an explainer, whose icon then shows the row's explainer alone: `facet-tree.js` sets the title only while the row has none, review of PR #15, as the area rows' condition page icons showed both), the theme button, the introduction's and the sheet's close buttons (control hints their names and descriptions already give; a help cursor and a tip at the pointer would misread them), and SVG `<title>`s (the approval-years strip's bars, above, and the "Authorized over time" range band: an SVG element has no `::after`). WCAG 1.4.13 (`setupTips()` in `main.js`): hoverable with a mouse (the tip is part of its carrier, `pointer-events` on under `hover: hover`; an anchored tip's 4px `::before` bridges its gap; a tip at the pointer's `::before` bridges the band from where the pointer opened it to the tip's near edge, as wide as the tip (beside a tip level with the pointer, across to its near side; `pointerBridge()` in `tips.js`, pure and tested; `--bridge-x`/`-y`/`-w`/`-h`, fixed as the tip; a MeSH explainer's waits for its pause, so it takes no pointer before the tip shows; review 2026-09-29: without it a row entered near its bottom edge had the next row between the pointer and its tip, and moving onto the tip showed that row's tip instead), and on the other ways onto it the tip holds (`.tip-hold`, shown without the pause, its bridge too) when the pointer leaves its carrier on the way to it (`towardTip()`: the ray from where it opened through the pointer meets the tip, 8px larger), until the pointer leaves that way, reaches another carrier, or 500 ms pass unless it is on the carrier or tip (then as long as it stays); a click on it only hides it, told apart from a click on its own carrier by where it shows, except where it lies over its own carrier: that click is meant for the control under it, so it hides the tip and goes on there, as does a click on its unseen bridge, which can lie over the next row or over its own carrier's controls (review of PR #15: an ATC badge entered from above had its bridge over its other segments, which swallowed their clicks: the filter was not set and focus went to the badge) (`tipClick()` in `tips.js`, pure and tested: "tip", "through" or "carrier", from the tip's and bridge's rectangles, kept in `setupTips()`, and the carrier's; the element under the point once the tip is hidden, `elementFromPoint()`, gets the click re-dispatched, as the tip's click reached the carrier, and the focus a click would have given it (`focus({ focusVisible: false })`, the element or its nearest focusable ancestor; the synthetic click moves none); within a label, its checkbox, as a scripted click on the label also focused the checkbox with a keyboard focus ring; a click where nothing but the carrier itself lies under the tip or bridge, e.g. a facet row's padding, goes on as it is; review 2026-09-29: the first click on a type row's count or an area row's condition page link only hid the tip; the owner may prefer tips that never overlap the carrier's line, an open question)); Escape hides the tips (`.tips-hidden` on `<html>`, `preventDefault()`, so a popover's Escape waits for the next press) until the pointer reaches another carrier or focus moves; on touch screens a swipe does too (a touch moving more than 10px, the page or a scroll box scrolling: `isSwipe()` in `tips.js`), until a tap on a carrier or focus moves (bug hunt 2026-10-01: a tapped ATC segment's 212px tip hung over the medicines table's rows scrolled to; a tip in a sheet's or popover's strip covers nothing and stays, see "MeSH explainers"); a pointer click on a tip only hides it (it lies over other rows: never toggles the carrier's checkbox), but for a tip at the pointer over its own carrier (above). Focusable carriers (facet checkboxes, a filter chip's button) are described by hidden elements (`aria-describedby`, ids from `typeTipId()`, created once by `main.js`); tiles carry the text visually hidden after their label; badges have no extra screen-reader text (no tab stops added). Anchored (keyboard focus and touch taps), tiles and facet rows span the tooltip over the carrier; on a filter chip it starts at the chip and in a result table's Type cell it ends at the cell's right edge (phones: starts at the stacked row's left edge), so it stays on a 320px screen, under the carrier's own line (`--tip-top`, set by `setupTips()`). Elsewhere it starts at its carrier and moves left when it would cross the viewport's right edge less 16px, or in a scroll box (the medicines table, "Who is active where") that box's less 8px, never starting left of the box's left edge plus 8px, and no wider than the box less 16px (`--tip-max`, `tipMaxWidth()` in `tips.js`, also for a tip at the pointer; review of the ATC class explanations: at 390px a column header's 352px tip in the 309px activity box started 19px left of the viewport, cutting about 52px off every line), and goes above the carrier where the box has no room below (`--tip-left`, `.tip-above`, set by `setupTips()` from the tip's width once shown, else its max-width; phase 4f; the box since step 4, see "MeSH explainers"); the tip is a border box (`box-sizing` on the `::after`, as the `*` rule skips pseudo-elements), so its max-width holds padding and border.
- **Status explanations** (phase 4f, user decision; same mechanism as the type ones): `UI.statusTips`, keyed by the raw EMA status, at most 10 words (tested, with every status in `ema_search_index.json`): Authorised "Can be sold EU-wide; availability and reimbursement vary by country." (step 2, #16), Opinion "EMA has given its opinion; EU decision pending.", Opinion under re-examination "EMA is re-examining its opinion at the company's request.", Refused "The EU refused authorization.", Application withdrawn "The company withdrew its application before a decision.", Withdrawn from rolling review "The company stopped the early (rolling) review.", Withdrawn "Authorization withdrawn, usually at the company's request.", Expired "Authorization ended: not renewed.", Lapsed "Authorization ended: not marketed for 3 years.", Suspended "Authorization temporarily suspended.", Revoked "Authorization canceled by the EU." (U.S. spelling of the user's "cancelled"). A negative opinion (status Opinion, `opinion_status` Negative: Kinselby, Qezzaqar, Xervyteg, Meplyffa on 2026-09-28) has its own, `UI.negativeOpinionTip` "EMA recommended refusal; no EU decision published yet." (user decision 2026-09-28), on that medicine's dots and pills (`statusTipText(status, opinion)` in `labels.js`: the medicines table, result tables, the medicine card's Status block, a substance's status pill when each of its medicines with that status had one); the Status rows, legend and Status chip keep the Opinion tip. Shown on the status dots and pills (the medicines table's status dots, under their "{status} since {date}" line, owner decision 2026-09-30; result tables, the cards' Status blocks, a substance's "{n} authorized" pill), the Status rows (checkbox described by a hidden copy, `statusTipId()`), the "Stack by Status" legend entries (`tabindex="-1"`) and the Status chip when it names one status (`sentenceParts()` `tip`). The `data-tip` sits on a `.status-tip` wrapper (`tabindex="-1"`), as a status's dot is its `::before`, which the tooltip's hover bridge would replace. `typeTip()` / `statusTip(status, opinion)` in `badges.js` give `{ text, id }` (a negative opinion's own since 2026-09-30); the cards' Status blocks do not clip, so the tip shows below the pill.
- **Approval qualifiers** (step 4, #9; among the authorized medicines on 2026-09-28: 45 conditional, 45 under exceptional circumstances, 399 under additional monitoring): `statusFlags(status, row)` in `badges.js` = those of `STATUS_FLAGS` (`conditional_approval`, `exceptional_circumstances`, `additional_monitoring`) that are true, for status Authorised only (row: the search-index row when it has the flags, else the `ema_medicines.json` row once loaded; none while unknown). The medicine card says a qualifier once (owner decision 2026-09-30: step 4's status sentence, "Conditionally authorized: renewed yearly until full data are provided." / "Authorized under exceptional circumstances: reviewed yearly.", repeated the chip beside it; `statusSentence()` gives an authorized medicine none, whatever its flags, and `UI.card.qualifiers` is gone, tested): its Status block shows the flags as chips in its lead, after the pill and "since {date}" ("Conditional approval", "Exceptional circumstances", "▼ Additional monitoring" with EMA's black triangle aria-hidden; on 320px phones, ≤ 359px, the short names "Conditional", "Exceptional", "▼ Monitoring", so two share a line (2026-09-30: two full names took two lines, the EPAR button of 68 authorized medicines then partly below a 320×640 screen; `UI.card.flagsShort`, aria-hidden, the full name visually hidden and still the one read, the tip unchanged); `flagChip()` in `lookup.js`; `.status-flags`: neutral since F · Spacious, phase 4 (a `--grid` hairline, `--ink-secondary` text, no fill, 12px), their text wraps, 8px corners). Result tables (condition, indication-text, substance, company pages) show compact markers under the status's date ("Conditional", "Exceptional", "▼ Additional monitoring": a word each, review of step 4, as a keyboard user cannot bring up a marker's tip and a bare "▼" explains nothing; aria-hidden, the full name visually hidden; `UI.flagMarkers`, `flagMarker()`, `.flag-markers`). Every flag chip and marker explains itself as the type badges do (`data-tip`, `tabindex="-1"`): `UI.flagTips`, at most 12 words (tested): conditional "Approved on less complete data for an unmet need; renewed yearly.", exceptional "Full data cannot be collected, e.g. very rare disease; reviewed yearly.", monitoring "Black triangle ▼: monitored more closely; report any suspected side effects.", PRIME "EMA's priority medicines scheme: early support for an unmet need.", accelerated assessment "Assessed in 150 days instead of the usual 210."; the card's chips also carry it visually hidden (the card has room, as its type explanations). The other flags (PRIME, accelerated assessment; for a medicine not authorized now, all of them) stay chips in the card's Medicine type fact. One look for every qualifier (design sweep 2026-10-01, B4: the Status lead's chips, the Type fact's chips and the result tables' markers were drawn three ways): the Status lead's hairline chip (`.chip.flag-chip`, `.flag.flag-marker`: a `--grid` hairline, 8px corners, 12px/500 in `--ink-secondary`, no fill, 22px tall); the tables' ATC flags ("incomplete", "EMA: R03") keep the raised pill. The medicines table shows no markers (not asked; possible follow-up).
- **ATC filtering at every level (phase 2; a list since phase 4a)**: state key `atc` is a list of code prefixes at any level 1–5, combined with OR ("C and H03"); a value that is not a code (older links) keeps the class-name query semantics, ORed with the codes (`splitAtcValues()`, `makePredicates()` in `filters.js`; the tree says "Also filtering by class names matching …"). A product is in a class when one of its code's levels (`atcPrefixes()`) is that class, so malformed codes (`LX1XX02`, `VO4D`) are in none, as in the tree and breakdown counts. No selected class covers another: `toggleAtcCode()` (`atc.js`) removes a selected class, or adds one in place of the selected classes it covers or is covered by; links are normalized the same way (`?atc=C&atc=C01&atc=H03` loads as C and H03, `url.js`). Set from: the ATC tree (below); table badge segments (add or remove that class, `aria-pressed` when it is selected; each badge is a `role="toolbar"` with one tab stop, the first pressed or the last segment, and Left/Right/Home/End between segments); the ATC breakdown (exactly one code selected and no name query, `drillCode()` in `main.js`: bars drill into the next level, "Up one level" + path above, a leaf shows only itself; otherwise level-1 groups, and with codes selected the groups holding one are marked toggle buttons: a marked group removes its codes, another is added); in tree and breakdown, products coded exactly at an open class (an incomplete code such as `L04AC`) follow the children as a static "code incomplete" row, so the rows add up to the parent (`atcExactCounts()`); the ATC ladder on medicine cards (one per code) and substance cards (the medicines' most common code; others listed as "{names} is/are classed {code}."); the "Drug classes" lookup group and the `L04AC` Try link. Ladders, class suggestions and the Try link open the class alone (`classState()` in `url.js`: lookup keys and other filters cleared, `atc` = [code], ATC breakdown on the Classes and areas tab (`tab=classes`, F · Spacious, phase 2); pushState), so a link's href and its click agree, and focus its headline. Controls whose badge, name and count spans would read glued together carry an `aria-label` (`UI.atc.classCount()`: "L04 Immunosuppressants, 160 medicines"; `UI.atc.ladderLink()`). With exactly one ATC code and no other filter, the headline names the class ("{N} medicines in {code} {name}, {A} of them currently authorized.") with the level path and counts below. Pure helpers: `atc.js` (`atcPrefixes`, `atcPrefixCounts`, `atcExactCounts`, `atcChildren`, `atcLadder`, `mainAtcCode`, and for the tree `toggleAtcCode`, `atcCheckState`, `atcTreeCodes`, `atcTreeChildren`, `atcTreeSearch`), `suggestAtcClasses()` in `search.js`; `ATC_CODE` / `ATC_PREFIX_LENGTHS` are defined once in `badges.js`.
- **ATC tree** (phase 4a, `atc-tree.js`, the ATC class section; the phone ATC sheet borrows it): nested lists, each node row an expand button (`aria-expanded` "true" / "false", `aria-controls` its child list, "Classes in {code}"; hidden on leaves) and a checkbox row (`.facet-row`: code badge, Title Case name, count; `aria-label` from `UI.atc.classCount()`); name and count (`.atc-text`) move under the badge when short of 7em or of their longest word, so words never break inside (level 5 always wraps in a popover or sheet). Nodes: the classes with medicines matching every other filter plus the selected ones (`atcTreeCodes()`), in code order; counts by the facet rule. Checkbox states (`atcCheckState()`): checked; under a checked class checked and disabled ("…, included in L"); above a checked class indeterminate; checking a class drops its selected descendants. Open nodes are UI state (not in the URL): the levels above a newly selected class open by themselves. "Find an ATC class" searches, not filters (`atcTreeSearch()`): the top matching classes (code prefix, or name from 3 characters; a class under a match is not one of its own) with their levels opened, everything else hidden; "{n} matches" announced, "No matching ATC classes" when none. Rows are keyed (d3 joins), so focus survives re-renders. The ATC class chip's popover focuses the first checked class, else the search (`focusTarget()`); a table segment whose button is gone after a re-render hands focus to the ATC class chip (`focusAtcFilter()`). 44px expand buttons and 48px rows in sheets. `atc-picker.js` keeps only the level path (`renderAtcPath()`, `appendCodeBadge()`). Since phase 4f the tree's DOM is `facet-tree.js` (`createFacetTree(section, spec)`: rows, expand buttons, checkbox states, search, static row, keyed joins, optional link after a row), shared with the therapeutic area tree; `atc-tree.js` is its ATC spec (code badges, `atcTree*()` helpers, the name-query note). The static row under a class reads "code incomplete", or "coded at this level" when every medicine coded exactly there has a complete code (`atcIncompleteAt()`: J07BX03's three COVID-19 vaccines under J07BN, phase 4e). **Row explainers** (owner feedback 2026-09-29: the ATC filter showed no tooltips): every row explains its class as the area rows their MeSH notes (`facet-tree.js` `spec.tip`, the same mechanism: the explainers' pause and width, at the pointer on hover, with keyboard focus across the section in a popover or sheet; on a touch screen (`hover: none`) a tap shows it while it checks the row, owner decision 2026-09-29, as the modality tree's: `tapTip: true`, `.tap-tip`, see "MeSH explainers"): since the ATC class explanations (owner decisions 2026-09-29; see "ATC class explanations" below) led by the class's explanation (levels 1–4, where it has one) and then, on a line of its own, "ATC level 4, chemical, pharmacological or therapeutic subgroup, in L04A." (owner feedback 2026-10-01: not the class's code and name, which the row or bar beside it already shows; `atcClassTip(code, classes, explanations)`; the render model's `explanations`; the row gets `.tip-lines` when its tip has two lines; the class above by its code only, owner decision 2026-09-29: shorter tips; `atcClassTip()` in `atc.js`, `UI.atc.classTip()`; the levels as WHOCC's ATC structure and principles page defines them, `UI.atc.levels`: anatomical main group, pharmacological or therapeutic subgroup, then chemical, pharmacological or therapeutic subgroup at levels 3 and 4, chemical substance (review of PR #15: WHOCC's metformin example alone had given level 2 "therapeutic subgroup", wrong for pharmacological groups such as C09; wording to confirm with the owner); no parent at level 1), then, from `atc_classes.json` `status`, `replaced_by` and `changed_year` (the render model's `classes`), "Retired 2022, now L01FA01." ("Retired 2023, with no successor." when deleted) or "On WHO's temporary list: it can still change." (L04AC28); a code without a WHO name by its code alone. Its checkbox is described by the same text (`describedTip()` in `mesh-notes.js`: one hidden element per code, `atc-tip-{code}` in `#atc-tips`; the ATC breakdown's bars share it). The static rows explain themselves too, as the modality tree's (`UI.atc.incompleteTip`, `UI.atc.codedHereTip`).
- **ATC class explanations** (owner decisions 2026-09-29; `atc_class_explanations.json`, see Architecture; `buildAtcExplanations()`, `atcExplanation()`, `explainedTip()` in `atc.js`, pure and tested): our plain-language explanation of a class at levels 1–4 leads every tip that explains the class, on a line of its own (`explainedTip()`: "{explanation}\n{the tip as before}"; the carrier keeps the break: `.tip-lines`, `white-space: pre-line`, added by `facet-tree.js` and `breakdown.js` when a tip has two lines; each explanation ends with a period, so the hidden description reads "… responses. ATC level 4, …", not glued): the ATC tree's rows (above); the ATC breakdown's bars (new: before, they had no tip; `atcClassTip()`, as the tree's rows, their description `atc-tip-{code}`; on hover and keyboard focus, not on a touch screen, where a tap drills: see "Modality"); "Who is active where": each ATC column header's filter tip and the class toggle above the table ("{explanation}\nShow only L Antineoplastic and Immunomodulating Agents"; `filterButton()` in `activity.js` with the column's `explanation`, `{ text, id }`, described by `atc-explanation-{code}` (the explanation alone, in `#atc-tips`) before the filter hint); the "Approvals per year" legend entries stacked by ATC (the explanation alone, as the modality entries'); the ATC badges of the medicines table and of the result tables (`atcBadgeTip(row, names, years, explanations)`: the explanation of the code's deepest class at levels 1–4 (a level-5 code's level-4 class, an incomplete code's own), then the level names, flags and source as before; one tip per badge, not per segment: its segments are one carrier, and nested carriers would show two tips at once; none when that class has none, never a shallower class's; each segment of the medicines table's badges is described by its own class's explanation, `aria-describedby` `atc-explanation-{code}` (levels 1–4; `createTable()` `atcClassTip`), read on focus only, as the tip is visual (review of the explanations); the result tables' badges have no focusable segments). A level-5 code (its substance's name), a class without an explanation (a retired class from an older link, L01XC; a code WHO does not name) and older data keep the tips as before. Not explained yet (no tip there before): the ATC class chip, level paths, the cards' ATC ladders and the class suggestions (possible follow-up). Sizes (2026-09-29, system font as Geist does not load in worktrees): tree and bar tips at most 7 lines (140px) at 22rem (352px) wide at 1440px, 328px wide across the 390px sheet; badge tips median 176px tall (122 before), at most 284px (15 lines). About's ATC credit adds, once `meta.json` credits "ATC class explanations", "The plain-language class explanations are Approval Atlas's own, not WHO's." (`UI.about.sources({ explained })`).
- **Therapeutic area tree** (phase 4f, user decision: one tree replaces the "Therapeutic area group" and "Therapeutic area" sections and their sentence tokens; `areas.js` pure and tested, `area-tree.js` the facet-tree spec, section `#facet-area`, a chip's popover or sheet borrows it): MeSH category › branch › level 2 › level 3 › EMA's terms as leaves, from `ema_therapeutic_area_branches.json` and `ema_therapeutic_area_subtree.json` (`buildAreaTree()`). **Categories** (owner decision 2026-09-29: the tree is grouped by MeSH's top level, a branch code's letter): A Anatomy, B Organisms, C Diseases, D Chemicals and Drugs, E Analytical, Diagnostic and Therapeutic Techniques, and Equipment, F Psychiatry and Psychology, G Phenomena and Processes, H … Z (NLM's names verbatim, from the MeSH Tree Structures: `UI.areas.categories`, a static map of all 16); only those with a branch in the data show (A, B, C, D, E, F, G, N on 2026-09-28), by letter, each a selectable row as an ATC letter (key: the letter, `?area=C`; `tree.roots`, `isCategory()`; a branch's parent is its category, so a category is in every medicine's `areaKeys` once per category it touches, above its branches in `ancestors()`, the paths ("Diseases › Neoplasms") and the checkbox states: checking Diseases includes its branches, "…, included in Diseases", and drops a selected area under it (`toggleArea()`, links too: `?area=C04&area=C` loads as `C`), a category above a selected area is indeterminate); C Diseases starts open (UI state: `facet-tree.js` `open`, `OPEN_CATEGORY` in `area-tree.js`), the others closed, and the levels above a newly selected value still open by themselves; a category has no condition page link (`areaDescriptor()` null) and, as no descriptor holds a category, no NLM scope note: its explainer is computed from the data (owner feedback 2026-09-29, "no tooltips on top-level MeSH"), "Diseases (MeSH category C): the top level of the MeSH tree; its branches here: C01–C26." ("its branch here: A02" for one; `areaCategoryTip()` in `areas.js`, `UI.areas.categoryTip()`), its checkbox described by it (`mesh-category-tip-{letter}` in `#mesh-tips`), and, unlike the other rows' MeSH notes, shown by a tap on a touch screen while the tap checks the row (owner decision 2026-09-29: `facet-tree.js` `tapTip` per row, `(key) => tree.isCategory(key)`; see "MeSH explainers"); the charts keep the branches as their top level (see "Class and filtered views" and "Who is active where"). Every level in MeSH tree order (owner request 2026-09-28, replacing phase 4f's children by name; as the ATC tree by code; tested on the real data files): categories by letter, branches by code (A02, B04, C01, C04 … F03), level-2/3 nodes by tree number, EMA's terms under a node by their descriptor's tree number under that node (a term with several numbers there: the smallest; the numbers from `mesh_descriptor_notes.json`, which loads after the first render: `tree.setNotes()` re-sorts; before it, and for terms without a number there, last by name); the static rows ("tagged at this level", "Tagged only as …") stay last, and the tree search's matches are unchanged. EMA's terms are MeSH terms: a term whose descriptor is a level-2/3 node is that node (it shows once, as the node: "Breast Neoplasms" is C04.588.180; `exact()`), a term deeper than level 3 is a leaf under each of its level-3 nodes, a term matched at a branch root ("Cancer", "Neoplasms"; 12 tags on 2026-09-28) is a tag of that branch, neither a leaf nor the branch itself (phase 4g, user decision 2026-09-28; phase 4f review: no "Neoplasms › Neoplasms" leaf): the branch ends with one static row "Tagged only as Neoplasms or Cancer" (`rootTerms()`: the heading first, then entry terms by name; `areaExactLabel()`, `UI.areas.taggedOnly()`) counting, by the facet rule, the medicines tagged only with such tags in that branch (no more specific term there; `exactOf()`: 41 of the 44 tagged Neoplasms or Cancer); selecting the branch includes them; a root tag's parent is its branch (path, "Up one level"), and a search finds the branch by it ("cancer": Neoplasms); a root tag selected on its own (older links) reads as a tag wherever it shows, "tagged Neoplasms" (phase 4g review: `?area=Neoplasms` read "Neoplasms › Neoplasms" and its pill looked like the branch's; `tree.label()` / `tree.labels`, `isRootTag()`, `UI.areas.tag()`: headline "{N} medicines tagged Neoplasms", both level paths, the breakdown's bar and title "Medicines tagged Neoplasms", sentence pill, activity column and toggle), never by its branch's name alone, and the tree names it above its rows, "Also filtering by the tag “Cancer”." (`UI.areas.tagNote()`, `#area-tags`, as the ATC tree's name-query note), as its branch row is only indeterminate; a term sits under every branch and node it has (the same term can be a node in one branch and a leaf in another: Breast Neoplasms under C17's Breast Diseases). Keys: category letters, branch codes, tree numbers, terms (the `area` filter holds any of them); a term that is a node is selected as that key wherever it is picked (`canonical()`, phase 4f review: tree leaf, breakdown bar, activity column, old link; a descriptor with two tree numbers, e.g. Alcohol-Related Disorders C25.775.100 and F03.900.100, as both), so its node and its leaves elsewhere show one state (the leaf is checked, not "included", when its nodes are selected), the breakdown path and "Up one level" go through the node's levels (Anemia: Hemic and Lymphatic Diseases › Hematologic Diseases › Anemia), and a bar or column of such a leaf opens its node (Breast Diseases' Breast Neoplasms: Neoplasms › Neoplasms by Site › Breast Neoplasms). A medicine's keys (`areaKeys`, `buildProducts()`): its terms and every category, branch and node above or at them; the filter matches any (OR); the tree counts a medicine once in every key it touches (by the facet rule: every filter but the area one), so children need not add up, and a node with children and medicines tagged at the node itself ends with a static "tagged at this level" row (copy review 2026-09-30: "not more specific" was false for 73 medicines also tagged with a narrower term; `areaExact`; a branch: its root tags' row). With exactly one area selected and no other filter (phase 4g, user decision, as the ATC class headline), the headline names it as that one names its class, by its name alone (phase 4g review: the whole path ran to about 7 lines at 390px and repeated the level path below it): "{N} medicines in Breast Neoplasms, {A} of them currently authorized." (`UI.headline.area()`, one and none handled; a root tag "{N} medicines tagged Cancer, …"; a category "{N} medicines in Diseases, …", its level path "Diseases"), with the level path and counts below (`renderAreaPath()` with `all: false`, "Levels of this therapeutic area"; counts by the facet rule). Checkbox states over every path (`areaCheckState()`): checked; under a checked node on some path checked and disabled ("…, included in Neoplasms by Site"); above a checked value indeterminate; `toggleArea()` adds a value in place of the selected ones it covers or is covered by. "Find a therapeutic area" searches (not filters; `areaTreeSearch()`, folded, from 3 characters): the top matching nodes (by their name or a term that is them) and the terms matching where their parent is not already a match, with their levels opened; a category matches by its name only when nothing below it does ("anatomy": Anatomy with its branches; "diseases" still lists the branches and nodes named so, under Diseases opened); a MeSH tree number typed as a prefix, any case, from the first character (owner decision 2026-09-29, as the ATC tree's code prefix), finds the rows whose number starts with it ("C04.588": Neoplasms by Site; "c": Diseases; a term by its number under each parent, shown only under the parents where it matches). Each row ends with its condition page link once known (`areaDescriptor()`). Only rows with children get a child list id and `aria-controls` (`facet-tree.js`), so terms (spaces, several parents) never make invalid or duplicate ids. **Tree numbers** (owner decision 2026-09-29, reversing the overnight call to show them only in the MeSH explainer): each row starts with its MeSH tree number as a mono code badge, as the ATC tree its codes (`.code-badge.tree-number`: the slate hue, shaded by depth as the ATC codes by level, `areaNumberLevel()`: category 1, branch 2, level-2 and level-3 nodes 3 and 4, terms 5): a category's letter ("C"), a branch's code ("C04"), a node's tree number ("C04.588", "C04.588.180"), an EMA term's descriptor's tree number under that row's parent, the one its place there comes from (`tree.number(key, parent)`: strictly below the parent, so a root tag has none; from `mesh_descriptor_notes.json`, so a term's appears once the notes load, and none without one there); `facet-tree.js` `spec.number` (updated on every render, unlike `decorate`; `setNumber()`). A term's number runs to 35 characters on 2026-09-28 (Niemann-Pick Disease, Type C: C10.228.140.163.100.435.825.700.875; 492 of the leaves' are 15, the rest longer): the badge keeps beside the checkbox (`max-width`), shrinks and wraps after its dots only (`<wbr>`; digits and dots are one unbreakable word otherwise), never inside a part; as in the ATC tree (review 2026-09-29: without its longest-word floor, branch names split mid-word beside their badges in the 320px sheet, "Musculoskel|etal"), name and count move under the badge when short of 7em or of their longest word beside it (level-2 and deeper rows at the default 320px, nearly every row in the 320px sheet), so words never break inside; unlike the ATC tree's they are never wider than the row, terms included (`.area-tree .atc-text`: `width: min-content`, `min-width: min(7em, 100%)`, `max-width: 100%`), so only a word longer than the whole row still breaks, never overflows it (a deep row: "Polyradiculoneuropathy" in the 320px sheet, "Gastrointestinal" at the 260px sidebar). A row's expand button and condition page link are level with its checkbox line, the first (review 2026-09-29: centred on a row whose name had moved under its badge, the chevron sat on the name's line): that line (22px, the badge's) starts at the top of the row, centred as in a one-line row (32px; 48px in a sheet, so a two-line row there is 59px, not 48px with its 44px controls astride both lines), and its items at its top, so a badge wrapping onto two lines leaves the checkbox and a name beside it on the first (`.area-tree .atc-row` `align-items: start`). Tree only: the breakdown's area bars and the level paths show no numbers (a term's number, up to 35 characters, fits neither a bar label nor a path item cleanly, and numbers on the branches and nodes alone would leave a drilled level-3 view without them). In the phone sheet each level indents 8px, not 14px (review of the categories, 2026-09-29: with the category level and the sheet's 44px expand and link columns, EMA's terms under a level-3 node had 94px for their names at 320px; now 118px, more than the 108px before the categories; `.sheet .area-tree .atc-tree`). Rows are named by the name first, then the tree number shown, then the count, "Neoplasms by Site, C04.588, 284 medicines" ("{name}, {n} medicines" while a term's number is unknown; `UI.areas.numbered()`, `UI.areas.count()`; owner decision 2026-09-29); the Therapeutic area chip's popover focuses the first checked row, else the search.
- **Branch chips** (owner decision 2026-09-29; `area-chips.js`, the pure parts `termBranches()`, `branchChips()`, `branchSelected()`, `branchIncludedIn()` in `areas.js`, tested; `UI.areas.chips*`): in the conditions card's Condition column (one condition per row, so no ";"; see "Conditions card") and, in the medicines table's Therapeutic area column, the substance card's result table's and the medicine card's "Therapeutic areas", the conditions are one flow with a visible ";" between them, each a group (`.term`, an inline block, so a condition and its chips wrap together unless it is longer than the line; `fillTerm()`, the ";" kept with the chips so it never starts a line), its name first (the condition page link, as before), then small mono chips for its MeSH branches (`ema_therapeutic_area_branches.json` `branch`: distinct, in code order; a root tag's ("Cancer"): its branch; a term without a branch: none) in the area tree's branch shade (`--slate-2`, `--slate-text`): at most 2, then "+n" (not a button: an image, `role="img"`, named "Also in C18 Nutritional and Metabolic Diseases; C19 Endocrine System Diseases" (conditions card review 2026-09-29: focused, it was an unnamed generic), that text its `data-tip`, tabindex -1 so a tap shows it; its visible part `.area-more-fill` at least 24px square, the focus ring round it (it was 13 by 15px, its centre 10px from the chip beside it)). Adrenoleukodystrophy: [C10] [C16] +2. The two shown are those within the area filter first (chips review 2026-09-29: `?area=C18` left C18 unpressed behind "+2"; `branchChips(codes, selected)`: selected themselves, then included through their selected category, then the others, each list in code order), so `?area=C18` shows [C10] [C18] +2 (C16, C19 behind it); `markAreaChips()` shows other chips when the filter changes without a rebuild (each toolbar's codes kept in a WeakMap). The chips and "+n" keep together and wrap below the name when it leaves them no room; a line of chips is 26px (top-aligned, 2px clear of the next line's); the table's minimum width is unchanged. Review 2026-09-29 (the first version put each condition on its own line: lists up to 2.8 times taller, the product information button pushed below the first screen): table heights before chips / one per line / one flow, adalimumab's substance card 1,555 / 4,293 / 2,293px at 1440, 4,738 / 7,400 / 7,204 at 1024 (its Therapeutic area column is at its 122px min-content there, the table already 40px wider than its card before chips, so nothing to gain; since the chips review it stacks there, 4,633px: see "Condition pages"), 4,946 / 8,088 / 6,396 at 390; the medicines table's first page at 1440 10,508 / 13,008 / 12,755 (the column near its 10rem floor, as the table fills the 1024px scroll area at min-content; a wider floor, e.g. 16rem, would make it scroll sideways): open question for the owner. On the medicine card the chips are in More details, with every condition (`areaLinks()` in `lookup.js`), and on the first three conditions of its Indication block (owner decision 2026-09-30, "area names without chips … align": F · Spacious, phase 4 showed them by name only in the Status block; with their chips they moved to the Indication block, as in the Status block they pushed the product information below a 320×640 screen; `areaNames()`, `termLinks(…, { chips: true })`, the same component as elsewhere), then "and 7 more" (`UI.card.moreAreas()`: the rest of its name, " therapeutic areas", visually hidden; a `.toggle` reading as a link, 44px tall on phones, where it takes a line of its own starting under the chips' 6px gap, so its box never covers a chip's 24px, axe target-size), which opens More details and focuses the first condition it did not show (before phase 4: on phones the first three with their chips, the rest behind "Show 7 more"). Phase 4 history (the chips' review, 2026-09-29; since 2026-09-30 see "Medicine card in blocks"): the product information button's top at 390×844 before chips / one per line / one flow: Wegovy 808 / 847 / 832px (its two conditions with their chips take one line more than the names alone), Rinvoq 801 / 1,038 / 759; at 320×640 Wegovy 876 / 900 / 904, Rinvoq 847 / 1,060 / 805. Each chip is a filter toggle as the ATC badge's segments: one `role="toolbar"` per condition ("MeSH branches of Adrenoleukodystrophy") with one tab stop (its first pressed chip, else its first; `markAreaChips()`), Left/Right/Home/End between its chips and "+n" (`toolbar.js`, shared with the ATC segments, whose keys are unchanged; chips review 2026-09-29: "+n" had no keyboard path to its tooltip, so it sits in the toolbar as a `.toolbar-item`: the arrows focus it, `:focus-visible` shows its tip, and it keeps tabindex -1, never the tab stop); named "Filter by therapeutic area C10 Nervous System Diseases", the branch's name as its tooltip (`data-tip`); `aria-pressed` when the branch is within the area filter, a branch under a selected category too (`branchSelected()`: as `inAreas()` for a branch, without the tree, tested against it on the real data), shown as the ATC segments' accent underline (`--accent` on `--slate-2` ≥ 3:1, `palette.test.js`; forced colors: an underline). A chip included only through a selected category (`branchIncludedIn()`, as the tree's "included" row, tested against `areaCheckState()`) is pressed and disabled, as that row (review 2026-09-29: a click narrowed `?area=C` to the branch while the chip stayed pressed): `aria-disabled`, described by a hidden "included in Diseases" (`#area-chip-included-C`, made once per category; `UI.areas.chipIncluded()`), the tooltip "Nervous System Diseases, included in Diseases" (`UI.areas.chipIncludedTip()`), the help cursor, and a click does nothing (`chipTogglable()`, in both handlers). Any other click toggles the branch as the tree does (`toggleArea()`: added in place of the areas it covers or is covered by; pressed again, removed). In the medicines table and the conditions card the filter applies in place and focus returns to the same chip of the same condition, or that condition's "+n" when the chip went behind it (a chip unpressed that `?area` had brought forward), else the same chip of any row, else the Therapeutic area chip. **On the lookup's cards** (the medicine card's Indication block and More details, the substance card's result table; Laws of UX, second pass, owner decision 2026-09-30, reversing 2026-09-29's filter toggle there: a tag-like chip that swapped the card for the filtered overview broke the lookup's flow) a chip is a link to its branch's condition page (`?cond=` the descriptor of the branch's name, `conditions.uiByName`, as the area tree's "Open condition page" for a branch and `areaDescriptor()` in `main.js`; every one of the 46 branches has one on 2026-09-30), named "Open condition page: C10 Nervous System Diseases" (`UI.areas.chipLink()`), the branch's name its tooltip as before; never pressed or disabled, and the first two in code order (no filter brings any forward); a branch without a page would be a plain label (`span.area-chip`, `role="img"`, `UI.areas.chipName()`, tabindex -1). Keyboard: the toolbar stays (`area-chips.js` `areaChips(…, { link })`; `toolbar.js` takes links as items), its first link the one tab stop and the arrow keys to the others and "+n": "+n" keeps a keyboard path to its tooltip, as in the dashboard, and a card with many conditions does not get two tab stops per condition before its protection estimate; screen readers still list every chip link. `createLookup()` has no `onAreaChip` or `areaFilter` any more. The branch names' fallback tooltip (a data-tip, as every explanation: see "Type explanations") sits on the name only (`.term-name`), never over the chips' own tips. The visible chip is the button's fill (`.area-chip-fill`, 24px, WCAG 2.5.8; the focus ring round it); on phones the button's transparent padding (10px above and below, 6px beside; the negative margin keeps the line) makes a hit area 44px tall and about 42px wide; "+n" likewise (conditions card review 2026-09-29: a transparent rim round `.area-more-fill`, 44px tall and 38px wide, starting where the last chip's rim ends; the rim is positioned, for its tip, but not raised, its visible part is). The rims take only free space (review 2026-09-29: they took taps meant for the condition name above, the ATC segments and "Show indication"): on phones every fill (a chip's or "+n"'s), condition link, "Show n more" and, in the medicines and result tables, every link, ATC badge, `.toggle` and tip carrier stands above every rim (`position: relative`, `z-index: 1`; 5 while hovered or focused, so no fill covers its tip). A condition link (`.term a` in the medicines table and the lookup cards; not the conditions card, whose 25px one-line rows size their own) is an inline block 24px tall at every width (padded up to 24px, `max(0px, (24px - 1lh) / 2)`, a negative margin keeping the line, 2px where the `lh` unit is unknown; audit 2026-09-30, M3, axe target-size; a fixed 2px until 2026-09-30 made it 28px on the medicine card's 24px lines), so a name that wraps is a box of its own and its chips start on the line under it, not beside its last line, where the name's line above covered them (1440, 1024, 320). On phones the tables' "Show indication" is 24px too (2px padding, a negative margin), a line of chips is 6px clear of the next (30px apart; 29px until 2026-09-30, which left the medicine card's rims 22px, e.g. Rinvoq's C06 of "Colitis, Ulcerative" at 320 and 390, and the table's 24px exactly) so the fills and links of the line above and the rims of the line below leave a rim at least 26px (axe target-size 0 at 320 and 390 on the medicines table's first page (default, every status, and Withdrawn, Refused, Opinion and Expired) and on Rinvoq's, Humira's, Keytruda's, Soliris's and Wegovy's cards with More details open, the top bar static, with Geist and with the fonts blocked; the phones' tables about 0.6% taller, 1440 unchanged), and a result row's areas start 4px under its PI and EPAR links. Checked with `elementFromPoint` at 320 and 390 (the medicines table's first page, adalimumab's table, Wegovy's and Rinvoq's cards, collapsed and expanded, and the conditions card's first 10 rows with its sort buttons and "Show 10 more"): no point of another visible control, nor of another chip's fill, reaches a chip; 82% of a chip's rim still reaches it. Again with the "+n" rims (review 2026-09-29; Rinvoq's, Wegovy's and adalimumab's cards, the medicines table's first page and the conditions card at 320 and 390, the conditions card also at 640, 667 and 700): every point of a "+n" fill but its rounded corners reaches its "+n", a "+n" rim takes points only from other rims (a chip's on the line below), and its fill, as a chip's, stands above a condition link's padding where the chips wrapped under the name, never over a link's text, a badge or another fill.
- **MeSH explainers** (owner request 2026-09-28; `mesh-notes.js`, pure and tested but `meshTip()` / `addMeshTip()`; `UI.mesh`): wherever a therapeutic area term or tree node shows (area tree rows in a popover or sheet, the medicines table's area links, the medicine card's areas and the result tables' area column, the condition page's heading, narrower conditions and matching conditions, the breakdown's area bars, the conditions card, the company page's area mix), a tooltip "{MeSH heading} (MeSH {tree numbers: three, then "and {n} more"}): {the scope note's lead}" (`meshTipText()`; lead = `scopeNoteLead()`: whole sentences up to 200 characters, a period then a capital ending one outside parentheses and not after an abbreviation (St., Dr., vs., e.g., i.e.; step 4 review: Mosquito-Borne Diseases was cut at "(e.g., Japanese and St.", Protein C Deficiency at "(Stedman's Med."), else the first 200 cut at a word with "…"), from `mesh_descriptor_notes.json` (`buildMeshNotes()`: by descriptor and by tree number; `areaNote()`: a term by its descriptor, a branch code or tree node by the descriptor holding that number); none without a scope note, or until the notes load (lazily: `lookup.need("meshNotes")` after the dashboard's first render or on a card; carriers get it in place: the tree and bars on the next render, the table through `update(…, notes)` without a rebuild). The same data-tip mechanism as the type and status tips (WCAG 1.4.13; `style.css`), with the text in a hidden element per descriptor (`#mesh-tips`, id `mesh-tip-{descriptor}`) describing focusable carriers (`aria-describedby`: links, bar buttons, tree checkboxes; their names unchanged), and tabindex -1 on the condition heading's name so a tap shows it. `.mesh-tip`: wider (22rem), and on hover shown only after 600 ms (a `visibility` animation), so a pointer moving down a table column or the bars does not open one per row; keyboard focus shows it at once. Step 4 review (the tips trapped the pointer: one covered the next 3–4 rows, and as it is hoverable a pointer moving down stayed in it, where a click only hid it): a pointer click on a carrier hides its tip until the pointer reaches another carrier (`setupTips()`; focus that a click or a script moves, e.g. to the label's checkbox or a drilled breakdown's first bar, and pointerover at the click's own position, which the page changing under a resting pointer fires, leave them hidden). A MeSH category row, which no descriptor holds, has an explainer from the data instead (owner feedback 2026-09-29; see "Therapeutic area tree"); every category, branch and level-2 or level-3 node of the tree has one (a data-file test in `mesh-notes.test.js`: a branch by the descriptor holding its code). The area tree's explainer is on the whole row (`.atc-row`: expand button, checkbox label and condition page link; the checkbox's description as before). On mouse hover the tip opens at the pointer, in a popover too (owner decision 2026-09-29, see "Type explanations"; it used to go beside the sidebar, far from the pointer, with a hover bridge from the row and a hold of its own, which the tip at the pointer's bridge (`--bridge-*`, `pointerBridge()`) and hold replaced). With keyboard focus, in a chip's popover or a sheet, the tip spans the tree's section under the row (`#facet-area` positioned, the row static; `--tip-top`; F · Spacious, phase 1: the sidebar and its beside-the-sidebar placement, `besidePanel()`, are gone). On touch screens (`hover: none`) a tap on a control carrying an explainer (links, bar buttons, tree rows) shows no tip: the tap acts (opens, checks, drills) and the tip would stay over the rows below until focus moves (the condition page shows the whole note); the condition heading's name keeps its tap tip, and so do the modality tree's rows and bars (`.tap-tip`, see "Modality"), and, owner decision 2026-09-29, the ATC tree's rows and the area tree's MeSH category rows (short tips; `facet-tree.js` `spec.tapTip`: `true`, or a function of the row's key for some rows only; the tap still checks the row; the area tree's other rows keep no tap tip). On a touch screen a tip in a sheet or popover (a tree row's, a type or status row's, a static row's) is not drawn at its carrier but in a strip above the foot (`.tip-strip`, aria-hidden, as the carrier's description says it; `updateStrips()` in `setupTips()`; bug hunt 2026-10-01: under a row, A02's hid A03 and A04 in the ATC sheet; above it, in the first fix, the search field and the parent row): the strip takes its height from the body, so it covers no row; a sheet keeps the height it had when its strip first showed until it closes (it grows upward from the bottom, so its rows would move under the finger); a tapped row the strip leaves under the body's visible bottom is scrolled back into view (`revealBy()` in `tips.js`); the strip changes in the frame after a tap, never between its mousedown and mouseup (a body shrinking there would send the click elsewhere, to the sheet's backdrop handler even); a swipe leaves it. In a touch tablet's popover at its 240px minimum a four-line tip leaves the body about 45px (iPad Pro 11, 2026-10-01). Keyboard focus keeps the tip under the row. A tip in a scroll box (the medicines table's `.table-scroll`, `.activity-scroll`) stays inside it, within its scrollbars and the viewport: at the pointer by `tipBounds()`; anchored by `tipShift()` (measured from the carrier's first line box, as a link can wrap), going above its carrier where there is no room below (`.tip-above`, `tipAbove()`; `tipHeightEstimate()` while the tip cannot be measured). Condition links and area rows keep their own cursors (links and checkboxes the hand, not "help"); the condition heading's name keeps "help". The table's term name drops its branch-names tooltip once its link has an explainer. `setupTips()` falls back to the tip's own max-width when it cannot measure it.
- **Companies** (companies part 2, user decisions 2026-09-28; `companies.js` pure and tested, `company-tree.js`, `holders.js`): holders grouped by company group (the current owner) and company (spellings folded), EMA's holder name always visible. **Badges**: `companyBadge()` (`holders.js`) shows a group's monogram from `companies.json` (1–4 characters, the badge growing with them; in lists (tree rows, breakdown bars, activity rows, the per-year legend) and the tables' Company · Holder columns (medicines table, result tables) every badge as wide as a 4-character monogram, so the names after it line up; no logos) on its hue's fill (`.company-badge`, Geist Mono); the 20 groups the user named (Roche, Novartis, Pfizer, AstraZeneca, Sanofi, GSK, MSD, Novo Nordisk, Lilly, BMS, J&J, AbbVie, Bayer, Boehringer Ingelheim, Takeda, Gilead, Amgen, Teva, Sandoz, Viatris) and, among the 25 largest by medicines, Krka (Pantone 355 green), CSL (red) and Gedeon Richter (deep blue) in a damped version of their brand colour (Intas/Accord Healthcare, #4, and Recordati, #24, still take hashed hues: their brand colours could not be confirmed; open question) (`BRAND_HUES` in `badges.js`: tokens `--co-{name}-1`, `-mid`, `-text` in both modes, `.hue-co-{name}`; `palette.test.js` checks text on fill ≥ 4.5:1 and mids and text shades as chart marks ≥ 3:1), every other group one of the damped hues but slate, stable per group (FNV-1a hash of its key: `companyHue()`); a joint venture's badge is outlined with a dashed line; in these colours wherever it shows, dashboard cards included (owner decision 2026-09-30), but for the medicine and substance cards' blocks, where it is the slate hue (Laws of UX, second pass, owner decision 2026-09-30: Von Restorff, the status pill stays the block's one colour; see "Medicine card in blocks"); always next to the group's name: aria-hidden, no tooltip of its own (it would hide its carrier's: activity rows, bars, path buttons). **Holder display** (`holderDisplay()`, wherever a medicine's holder shows: the medicines table's "Company · Holder" column, the result tables' (condition, text, substance, company pages), the medicine card's Status block (its "Company" fact, as the table's column; on phones without the holder line, which More details shows: Laws of UX, second pass, 2026-09-30) and the "Company" fact in its More details (then, small, the provenance where the data has it (older files: none): "Sponsor: {sponsor_note}." or a "Sponsor source" link for a curated sponsor, "Why {group}: {group_note}." for a medicine a per-medicine row put under its group (`source` `curated_medicine`: `companies.entry()` `moved`), else, for a `group_note` that only notes a medicine's later ownership (`curated_medicine_notes()`, the medicine not moved, e.g. Udenyca), "Ownership: {group_note}.", each followed by a "Source" link to its evidence (`markExternal()`; user decision 2026-09-28, as the Source link is the sponsor's older document); on phones (≤ 600px, decided when rendered) a note over 120 characters sits behind a disclosure, "Why Theramex?", "Ownership" or "Sponsor" (44px summary, `noteLine()`); `companies.entry()` `holder` null, never "Not stated"), "via a regulatory representative: {firm}" for a curated sponsor (after the sponsor's name when its group has another name: "Avanir Pharmaceuticals · via a regulatory representative: Jenson Pharmaceutical Services Limited" under Otsuka; "EMA: {name} · EU register: {firm}, a regulatory representative" when the Union Register decided the holder, Zokinvy), "· a regulatory representative, holding for another company" for a representative's own group (`UI.companies.holderLine()`); the results timeline's tooltip "{group} ({EMA name})". **Company tree** (section `#facet-mah`, the Company chip's popover or sheet; `facet-tree.js` with `limit`/`more`: the top 8 groups by count, then "Show 20 more", checked or mixed rows always shown, and during a search every match (review of companies part 2: "pharma" announced 167 matches and showed 8)): group › company › EMA holder name, a level left out where its one row has the same name as the row above ("4SC AG" shows once; Krka's one company "Krka": its holder names under the group); a holder name among others that repeats the name of the row above it shows as a muted "(same name)" (`--ink-secondary` on a checked row's accent wash, where `--muted` is under 4.5:1; `palette.test.js`) (`companies.repeatsParent()`; its checkbox named "Roche Registration GmbH (same name), 34 medicines"; the company page's list does the same, the link named by the name); rows keyed by path (`g.roche`, `g.roche/c.roche`, `g.roche/c.roche/Roche Registration GmbH`: a company can sit under two groups (a per-medicine owner), a holder name under two companies when the Union Register moved some of its medicines), counts by the facet rule per row (`companies.countKeys()`), children most medicines first; a row's filter value (`rowValue()`) is the value it shows when the row holds all of that value's medicines, else the row's path (see URL keys), so its checkbox, count and filter agree; medicines EMA names no holder for have no holder row ("Not stated" is no holder name): they count at their company's row, with a static "No EMA holder name" row after its holder names (`noHolderKey()`; a company whose one holder name is its own is then not folded); checkbox states per row (`checkState()`: checked when its value is selected; included under a selected value above it on its path; mixed above one); a row toggled there stays listed (unchecked, past the top 8) until the search or "Show 20 more" changes, so its focus is kept (`facet-tree.js` `kept`, all trees); groups with their badge, groups and companies with a company page link (`.cond-link`, closing a sheet as the area rows'); "Find a company" searches (`companyTreeSearch()`, from 3 characters, by word start as the Companies suggestions (review: "roche" found "Neurochem Luxco II SARL"): names, a company's other spellings (member holders no row shows) and old names (its `ownership` notes; a group's old names no company row shows, the group), a group by its monogram from the first character), the tree's note names the groups' as-of date. **Company page** (`?co=`, a lookup view like `cond`: pushState, focus to its heading, `document.title` its name; `companyPage()` in `lookup.js`): kicker "Company", headline "[RO] Roche: 41 medicines, 31 currently authorized." (its badge sized with the headline, 0.8em tall, centred on its cap height: design sweep A2, 2026-10-01) (every status; currently authorized: status Authorised with an approval date), a joint venture's partners ("A joint venture of [BMS] Bristol Myers Squibb and [PFE] Pfizer.", links) or a group's joint ventures, a company's groups, those its medicines are with ("Part of [VTRS] Viatris."; a company split between groups names each with its medicines there, most first: "Part of [VTRS] Viatris (10 medicines) and [BCB] Biocon Biologics (2 medicines).", `UI.companies.groupCount()`; a company all of whose medicines a per-medicine row moved is not part of their group: "Its medicine is under [GBT] Guerbet." (`companies.allMoved()`: Mallinckrodt Deutschland, Elan Pharma, Wyeth Consumer Healthcare, Sanofi Pharma Bristol-Myers Squibb); the headline's badge is the group's, or a company's own group (`companies.json` `group_key`), so a company split between groups has one too: [VTRS] Mylan S.A.S.), a representative's note, the as-of note ("Grouped by current owner as of …, not the owner at approval…"; a company page: its groups' latest curation date, not the company row's snapshot date); "Companies and EMA holder names" (each with its count, a link opening the overview filtered to exactly those medicines, on its Medicines tab (`tab=medicines`, F · Spacious, phase 2), with every status (`?status=all`, owner decision 2026-09-29: the page counts every status, the overview shows authorized ones by default; so do the ATC groups' links): `structure()` `values`, a group page's rows or a company page's rows in every group; then, muted, "No EMA holder name {n}"), the approvals timeline, "ATC groups" (level-1 letter badges and bars; each opens the overview filtered to the group and the class, on its Classes and areas tab), "Most common conditions" (top 8 terms, condition page links), the medicine list (`resultTable()`; user decision: authorized ones first, as condition pages, counted as the headline counts them (`authorizedFirst()` in `approvals.js`: an Authorised medicine without an approval date is listed under "Show all statuses", so "Authorized medicines (24)" matches "24 currently authorized"), "Authorized medicines (n)", every status with "Show all statuses", "Medicines of every status (n)"; a company with no authorized medicine shows every status and no toggle, review of companies part 2: c.mylan showed "Medicines (0)") and "Sources" (the LEI as a link to its GLEIF record, "search.gleif.org" named "GLEIF website";
