The shown tab: ink, 600, a 2px accent underline; the others `--ink-secondary`, 500; each tab as wide as its name in 600 (a hidden bold copy of it, `::after` from `data-label`, set by main.js, no part of its name), so the strip does not shift when the selection changes (design sweep A10, 2026-10-01: 1.8px); 44px tall; a hairline under the strip; underlines are inset shadows and focus rings inset, as on phones (≤ 720px) the strip scrolls sideways across the gutters (no page scroll, scrollbar hidden as the chip row's; the tab shown is scrolled into the strip when it changes, and a tab the arrows focus by the browser). Checked 2026-09-30 in Playwright at 1440 and 390 (production build): the tablist and its six tabs in the accessibility tree with the one selected, the keyboard walk above, back and forward, a direct link to each tab, axe clean on every tab, no console errors. **State**: URL key `tab` (`DEFAULT_STATE.tab` "overview", written only when not the Overview; an unknown value is dropped and reported as other keys; links from before, without it, load on the Overview); a view like `by`, not a filter: `url.js` `FILTER_KEYS` (every state key but `by` and `tab`; `main.js` and `intro.js` read it) leave it out of the filter count, Clear filters and the filter announcement; for the intro a tab other than the Overview leaves the untouched overview (review of phase 2: the card has had its turn; see "Landing"). Forced colors: the strip's hairline is a GrayText border and the shown tab's underline a 2px text underline (the inset shadows are dropped there); the previews' bars are CanvasText in a GrayText outline. Only a chart whose width changed since it was drawn re-renders on resize (`chartWidths` in `main.js`; a hidden one, width 0, is skipped), so showing a tab renders once. **Panels** (`.tab-panel`, `data-tab-panel`, one shown): Overview: "Authorized over time" with the type tiles, the Union Register and undated notes, then three previews (below); Protection: "Estimated protection ending"; Classes and areas: the breakdown card (ATC / Therapeutic area / Company / Modality) and the conditions card; Companies: "Who is active where"; By year: "Approvals per year"; Medicines: the table with Download CSV (the page header's Download CSV stays on every tab; `tableRows` is kept on every render). The headline, dek and chip bar stay above the tabs on every tab; under a lookup result the tabs show under "Explore EMA medicines" as the dashboard did. **Links land on their tab**: a drug class opened alone (`classState()`: search suggestion, card ladder, Try "L04AC"), a modality (`modalityState()`) and the intro's "Explore cancer medicines" (`areaState()`) open Classes and areas (`tab=classes`); a company page's "Companies and EMA holder names" links open Medicines (`tab=medicines`: the list of the medicines they count; `UI.companies.namesHint` says so) and its "ATC groups" links Classes and areas (the class's breakdown for that company); the wordmark opens the Overview; filter controls inside a tab (activity cells and headers, breakdown bars and drills, table segments, branch chips, the class path) keep the tab. **Overview previews** (`overview-previews.js`, `renderPreview()`; `previewShares()` and `topGroups()` pure and tested; as the F overview mockup): three small cards side by side from a 900px main column (`.previews-frame` is a size container), else one under the other: "Companies with the most medicines" (the 5 company groups with the most medicines shown: badge, name as a company page link, bar, count), "Conditions with the most treatments" (the top 5 of the conditions card's ranking, `conditionRows()` with its inputs, conditions with no authorized treatment left out: name as a condition page link, bar, count; "Loading…" until the lookup's conditions data), "Market protection ending (est.)" (the protection card's counts per year, `protectionEnding()` / `calendarBuckets()`: this data year, the next four, "{year} or later"; bars to the scale of the busiest single year, the later bar drawn broken when longer, `.pc-clamped`; a one-line caption; "Loading estimates…" until the protection files, which load once the page is idle); under each title its tab's link ("Companies tab", "Classes and areas tab", "Protection tab"; `UI.previews.tabLink()`; under, not beside it as in the mockup's two cards: three cards in a row at 1024px left the titles 3-4 lines beside it), whose click shows the tab and moves focus to its tab, scrolled into view (the preview link goes with the Overview; a modified click opens its `href`, which keeps the rest of the view). Links that open another view on a tab (a drug class, a modality, a company page's links) focus that view's headline, as before. Rows 32px, 44px on phones; each row's link a block filling its row's height and its label column (padded to 32px, 44px on phones; a wrapped name's row taller, 52px, 64px on phones, whose bar and count sit level with its first line, as the badge does (design sweep A5, 2026-10-01: the row's items at its top, the bar and count offset by the label's padding, `--preview-pad`; they sat 10px lower, on the two lines' centre); bug hunt 2026-10-01: a 20px link in a 44px row, so a tap under "Novartis" opened the next row's Intas); a company's badge inside its link, the link a flex row, its name in a span carrying the underline, top-aligned with the badge (`labelOf()` in `overview-previews.js`; fix-up: a tap on the badge opened nothing, one in the gap beside it near the row's edge the next row's page); the tab links at least 24px tall, 44px on phones and on touch screens (`pointer: coarse`: a phone in landscape, 844px, has no phone rules); the unit after each count visually hidden ("77 medicines"). **Doherty Threshold**: `renderDashboard()` renders the headline, chips, facet sections and counts every tab shares, then only the tab shown's cards; a tab's cards render when it is shown (every tab change renders). Measured 2026-09-29 in the dev build (Chrome, 1440px, real data; the longest animation-frame callback, i.e. `render()`, after a type checkbox and after "Include withdrawn, refused and pending"; rAF patched to setTimeout in a same-origin iframe, as the pane was hidden): before (every card rendered) 101–280 ms; after, per tab: Overview 20–51, Protection 6–34, Classes and areas 21–164, Companies 17–56, By year 16–31, Medicines 130–177 ms; a tab change 32–82 ms. `render-budget.test.js` measures the shared data part of a filter change on the real data in node (2.7–16 ms on 2026-09-29; budget 150 ms, the rest of the 400 ms left for the DOM, which node cannot measure). **View options** (Hick's Law; `view-options.js`, copy `UI.viewOptions`): each card opens in one view (its defaults, unchanged), and its secondary controls sit behind one "View options" disclosure at the right of its head (a native button, `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls` its panel, described by the card's title; a sliders icon, a chevron that turns while open; 32px, 44px on phones; the breakdown's mode switch beside it 32px too, design sweep A10: it was 35px): the breakdown's Sort (its ATC / Therapeutic area / Company / Modality switch stays in the head: the card's main choice), "Who is active where"'s Columns (ATC groups / Therapeutic areas, now with a visible label) and Column order, "Approvals per year"'s Stack by. The panel (a hairline above it, each control a visible label and a segmented control) stays open or closed across renders (UI state, not stored). The conditions card's sort buttons (its column headers) and the activity table's row sorts are the tables' own and stay. Measurements elsewhere in this file that name a 320px (or resizable) sidebar predate phase 1.
- **Top bar** (`header.topbar`, every width, on the surface with a hairline): the wordmark (`#home-link.brand`: the icon, "Approval Atlas", and under it `UI.dataDate()`, "EMA data as of 29 Sep 2026"; no longer an `h1`; named by its text, a visually hidden ", home," after the name, so the date is read too: "Approval Atlas, home, EMA data as of 29 Sep 2026", review of phase 1), the lookup search (`#lookup`, moved into the bar, same behaviour; the field 40px on desktop, 44px below 1024px; the suggestions drop down under it, `z-index` over the page), centred and 560px at most, then "What is this?" and the theme button (`.topbar-actions`) (design sweep A4, 2026-10-01: the bar's surface and hairline span the page, its content the page column, 1200px at most with the column's 32px and 16px gutters, as it sat 8px outside the column at 1280px, 88px at 1440px and 16px at 721-1023px; equal side tracks centre the search on the column, which at 1024-1099px makes it narrower than 560px, 496px at 1024, as the wordmark needs about 195px; at 1440px it was 29px right of centre); a grid, 64px tall on desktop. Below 1024px two rows (wordmark with the buttons, then the search full width) and sticky, its first row scrolling away so the search stays at the top (`top: calc(-1 * var(--topbar-hide))`; `placeTopbar()` in `main.js`, on a ResizeObserver and every render, sets `--topbar-hide` (the search row's top less 8px) and `--topbar-shown` (what stays: the page's `scroll-padding-top`, WCAG 2.4.11), which is 0 while the search has focus (`html:has(#lookup-input:focus)`; bug hunt 2026-10-01, lookup.md #5: Chrome scrolls the caret into view outside that padding on each keystroke, which the stuck field never reaches, so typing from a scrolled page moved it up 53px per character; a scroll-margin on the field does not apply to the caret)); while the wordmark, "What is this?" or the theme button has keyboard focus (`:focus-visible`) the bar shows its first row (`top: 0`; audit 2026-09-30, M1: scrolled, they took focus above the viewport), which a tap or click never does. Until its data loads the search keeps its place (`.lookup[hidden]` is `visibility: hidden`, not removed), and until the dashboard first renders `main` is at least a screen tall (`min-height: 100svh`; not once the data failed to load, `.load-failed` from `showMissingData()`, which also shows the search, disabled), so the footer is not pushed down in the first screen (audit 2026-09-30, S1: CLS 0.27 desktop and 0.81 phone, now 0.01 and 0.02). `#result` is its own formatting context (`display: flow-root`), so its "Loading…" line's margin cannot move its top when the answer arrives (a condition page: CLS 0.75 desktop, 0.77 phone before). At 320px the wordmark is smaller (24px icon, 14px name, the date may take two lines, the break before the date: main.js renders `UI.dataDateRuns()` with the date in a `.brand-day` run that does not wrap, design sweep A10, where it broke inside the date, "30 Sep / 2026") so the wordmark, "What is this?" (13px) and the 44px theme button share one line.
- **Page header** (`.page-head` in `#app`): `h1#page-title` "Approved medicines", and with the status filter widened (every status, or a choice of statuses; Laws of UX, second pass, owner decision 2026-09-30: "Approved medicines" stood over refused and pending ones) "Medicines in EMA's list", one line at every width from 320px (`UI.page.title(SOURCES, { widened })`: each source's `list` joined as the scope line joins theirs; `document.title` does not use it) (30px, 26px on phones; the page's only `h1`; "EU medicines" until 2026-09-30, when the owner chose a source-neutral heading with a source line, as national agencies' and FDA data may follow), tight under it the scope line (`#page-scope`, 13px, muted; 12px below 400px): by default (authorized only) "Human medicines authorized EU-wide through EMA" (one line at every width, 283px of 288 at 320), and with the status filter widened (every status, or a choice of statuses other than the default; owner decision 2026-09-30) the neutral "Human medicines, EU-wide through EMA's central procedure" (one line from 375px, 332px at 12px; two balanced lines at 360 and 320, `text-wrap: balance`) (`UI.page.scope(SOURCES, { widened })`: `SOURCES` in `labels.js` holds one `{ key, scope, neutral }` per source the data has, `scope` for the default line and `neutral` for the widened one, each joined with commas and "and", so another regulator joins the line without a redesign; only sources the data has, never a planned one; set by `renderAbout()` before any data loads and by `render()` on every render (before the dashboard's data loads, from the URL's `status` values); hidden while a lookup result is open, whose heading names EMA itself), under it the answer headline (`#headline`, now a `p` with `tabindex="-1"`, focused as before for a drug class or area) as the lead paragraph, 15px, 16px from 721px, the number in the accent, then the dek (`.page-dek`, 14px, muted) and the class path; Download CSV at the right (see "Medicines table"); then the filter chips (see "Filter chips") and `#url-note`. The scope line adds 22px to the page header at every width (2026-09-30, Geist, 2026-09-28 data, overview with the default status: 186 → 208px at 1440 and 1024, 278 → 300 at 390, 300 → 322 at 320). While a lookup result is open the heading reads "Explore EMA medicines" (`UI.explore.title`) and is an h2, the result's headline being the page's h1 (`title()` in `lookup.js`, `.card h1`; review of phase 1: one h1 at the top, `#page-title` swapped between h1 and h2 in `render()`), and `#explore-note` replaces the lead (the old `#explore` block is gone). The intro card and Try line stay above the page header.
- **Popover** (review of phase 1: pressing the open chip closes it, as focus moving to its chip on mousedown no longer closes it first (`leavesPopover()`, pure and tested, skips the chip for focusout and pointerdown alike); its max-height is the room under its chip less 24px, 240px to 70vh (`popoverMaxHeight()`, `--popover-max-h`), and with less room the page scrolls it into view; while it is open the intro card stays as shown (`introVisible()` `held`), so the chip bar does not move up under the pointer when the first filter hides it; `popover.js`, `createPopover()`; `#filter-popover` in `.filter-bar`; desktop, ≥ 1024px): a chip opens its section in a non-modal `<dialog>` opened with `show()` (the page stays usable), named by its title ("Filter by ATC class", `UI.filters.popoverTitle()`) and described by "Counts: medicines matching the other filters." (`UI.facets.counts`); it borrows the section from the store as the sheet does and puts it back when it closes. Placed under its chip (`--popover-x`, `--popover-y`, CSSOM; `popoverLeft()`, pure and tested: at the chip's left edge, moved left to end inside the bar), again after every render (a chip changes width) and on window resize; 380px wide, 440px for the trees (ATC, therapeutic area, company, modality: `WIDE_CHIPS`), at most 70vh tall with only its body scrolling, `--input` background, 14px corners, a shadow; Clear (the chip's keys) and Done at its foot. Filters apply live. Focus moves into it on open: the trees' first checked row, else their search (`focusTarget()`); the type and status lists' first checkbox; the years' Start thumb (`FOCUS_TARGETS`). Closing: Escape (unless a tip or a search with text takes it first), Done, or a click on its chip (`nextOpenChip()`, pure and tested) hand focus back to the chip; a click outside closes it and gives the chip focus only when the click moved focus nowhere; tabbing out of it closes it without moving focus; Back or Forward (navigation fixes 2026-10-01) close it, focus going to the view's heading when in view, else to the page (see "Scroll"). One popover at a time (another chip's click takes over); it closes below 1024px, a sheet from 1024px. MeSH, ATC and modality explainers in its trees span the section under the row (on a touch screen: in its strip above the foot; see "MeSH explainers").
- **Colours**: every new pair is in `palette.test.js` (both modes): an inactive chip's dashed `--field-border` on the page, an active chip's `--accent` on `--accent-wash` (text and hairline), the count badge's `--on-accent` on `--accent`, and the popover's texts and controls on `--input` (`--ink-secondary`, `--muted`, `--link`, `--link-hover`, `--accent`). Filter chip classes are `.filter-chip`, `.filter-chip-add`, `.filter-chip-active` (`.chip` is the medicine card's neutral pill).
- **With "Authorized by default"** (#22, rebased under phase 1 on 2026-09-29): the Status chip is inactive in the default state and counted only when widened (see "Filter chips"); the headline's quiet sentence and "Include them" sit inline after it in the lead paragraph, as the mockup shows (see "One dashboard, authorized by default"); the Status section's "Include withdrawn, refused and pending" / "Authorized only" button is in the Status chip's popover and sheet (see "Layout"). `facets.js` has no count of its own: "N active filters" is `url.js` `activeFilterCount()`, as the headline's.
- **Medicine card in blocks** (phase 4, `feature/redesign-f4-card`; primary use case: a phone lookup during a talk; Laws of UX: Miller's Law / chunking, Peak-End, Von Restorff, Law of Common Region; `lookup.js` `medicineCard()`, `cardBlock()`, `blockFact()`, `protectionLead()`, `areaNames()`, `indicationBlock()`, `openMore()`; copy `UI.card.blocks`, `UI.card.more`, `UI.card.since` / `approvedOn` / `firstApproved` / `noDate`, `UI.card.estimate`): under the kicker, the answer headline (the h1) and any namesake, four framed blocks in reading order (`.card-block`, an uppercase `h2.block-title`, under the card's h1 (the page's h1 while a result is open, review of phase 1); unnamed sections, not landmarks): **Status** (a lead line on one baseline (design sweep A10, 2026-10-01: centred, the pill's text sat 6-7px above "since"; the pill's baseline is its text's, its dot still centred, and a -1px bottom margin keeps the line the date's height, so the buttons do not move): the status pill (its tip as before), "since 6 Jan 2022" (ended: "approved 1 Jul 2016"; never approved: no date; authorized without an approval date, 6 on 2026-09-29, e.g. Lyvdelzi: a muted "no approval date", review of phase 4) and the qualifier chips; the status sentence (why a medicine is not authorized; none for an authorized one, whose qualifiers the chips say, 2026-09-30); then "Company" (the holder display); the Union Register chip and note when it disagrees), **Documents** (the product information, EPAR and overview buttons; a medicine without them, never authorized, has its documents list there instead, each link 44px tall on phones: bug hunt 2026-10-01, "EMA medicine page" was 21px), **Protection and copies** (the lead, "Market protection until 2028–2029 (est.)" ("(est.)" after the "until …" form only, not "ended", "unclear" or a copy's value: `glanceIsEstimate()` in `protection.js`), then the copies lines; left out when it has neither: never approved and no set) and **Indication** (coordinator review: what it is for is part of the primary use case): the indication's lead, the full text behind "Show full indication" (24px tall, 44px on phones, its lower 11.5px in the next part's 12px margin: bug hunt 2026-10-01, it was 21px), shown there only (Loading… until `ema_medicines.json` arrives), then "Therapeutic areas" (the first three with their branch chips, "and n more"; none without a text or areas). The order since 2026-09-30 (owner: "Phone 320x640: the product information button is not above the fold (390x844 is) → fix and align"; phase 4 had Status, Protection, Documents, Indication, and the areas by name in the Status block): opening the SmPC or EPAR is the primary use case, so Documents follows the answer, and Protection comes right after them, the conference's "is it still protected?" (review: with Indication before it, its lead was about 990px down at 390); a CSS `order` on phones only would have made the focus order differ from the visual one. One column on phones; from a 720px frame (`.card-blocks-frame`, a size container) two columns in reading order, Status beside Documents (its buttons one under the other), Protection beside Indication, the two of a row as tall as each other, a last block alone in its row across (`.card-block:last-child:nth-child(odd)`: a medicine never approved, the substance card). Then one **More details** disclosure (`details.more-details`, `data-key` kept across re-renders; one framed region, open or closed (design sweep 2026-10-01, B8: the summary and each section were framed apart, so opened they read as more blocks): the details carries the frame, the summary is its top row and the sections sit unframed inside, each under a hairline with its h2; their fact labels the blocks' (13px/500, `--ink-secondary`; B10: they were 14px/600), on their value's baseline in the two columns; summary "More details" with a short muted hint of what it holds, `UI.card.more.hint(documents)`, "documents" only where it holds them): "About this medicine" (every therapeutic area with its branch chips, substances, company with its provenance notes, type with its other flags, modality, ATC ladders), the protection estimate (`#protection`, as before) and "All documents", each an h2 section. The substance card's list and ATC headings are h2 too (under its h1 and Status block); the condition and company pages' sections are h2 too (audit 2026-09-30, S2: they jumped from h1 to h3; the company page's Ownership part an h3). Why one disclosure at the end, not one per block: a disclosure per block adds a 44px row before the buttons for each (about 90px on a phone, the EPAR button below the first screen for Wegovy), and what it hides is reference material read after the answer, not while scanning it; the protection lead and "and n more" open it at their place (`openMore()`, by their target's focus key: the estimate's heading, `protection`, and the first area the Indication block does not show, `areas-rest`; the section's top, or the "Therapeutic areas" label, is scrolled into view, the target focused; review of phase 4: data arriving afterwards gives the target focus back and scrolls it into view again until the next wheel, touch or key press, `pendingJump`, `JUMPS`, as the protection lead did before). Von Restorff: the answer is the card's strong type and colour: the headline's "authorized" in `--status-authorized` (a `tone-authorized` part, as "not" and "not yet" are toned; `palette.test.js` checks it on every background), since when and protected until at 20px/600 (`.answer-value`); the rest is quiet: the qualifier chips neutral (hairline, secondary ink, no fill), company monograms in the slate hue inside the blocks (`.card-block .company-badge`, the substance card's Status block too; Laws of UX, second pass, owner decision 2026-09-30: brand-coloured, they competed with the status pill; brand or hashed colours everywhere else), branch chips in the slate hue as everywhere. Phones (≤ 720px), the Status block's company in one line (Laws of UX, second pass, 2026-09-30): its "Company" label beside the group, and the holder line under it (the name EMA publishes, or the Union Register's and a representative's) only in More details, whose Company fact has the same holder display (`.medicine-card .block-status`; the substance card, which has no More details, keeps it). Phones (≤ 720px): tighter blocks (12px under the headline, 10px 14px padding, 10px gaps), copies lines at 14px, the buttons' lines at 1.3 with 6px padding. 320px phones (≤ 359px, 2026-09-30): the page's top 16px (24px), every lookup card's kicker 4px above its headline (8px) and its headline 26px (30px; medicine, substance, condition, text and company cards alike), the blocks 12px at their sides and 8px above, 10px below and apart (their titles and the Status lead 2px closer to what follows; Laws of UX, second pass, 2026-09-30), the Status lead's pill 14px, "since" 14px and its date 17px (20px), so the pill and "since 16 Dec 2019" (or "approved 26 May 2016") share a line as on wider phones, and its qualifier chips by their short names (see "Approval qualifiers"). First screen (the top of the product information button / the bottom of the second button, CSS px, mobile emulation, data of 2026-09-29, with the Indication block and rebased on the review of phase 1; measured against the layout viewport, so a real mobile browser's address and tool bars can leave less of it visible), before → after: 390×844 Wegovy 714/869 → 673/805, Rinvoq 665/820 → 630/762, Keytruda 641/796 → 678/810, Humira (product information and overview: its assessment is an older scientific discussion, in the documents list) 689/844 → 674/806, Zinbryta (withdrawn) 703/858 → 679/811: both buttons above the fold for all five (before: not for Wegovy, Humira, Zinbryta); 320×640 Wegovy 768/923 → 765/918, Rinvoq 669/824 → 693/846, Keytruda 693/848 → 717/870, Humira 717/872 → 737/869, Zinbryta 731/886 → 791/944: the buttons stay below a 640px screen, before and after (the top bar and a two-line headline take 290px there). 2026-09-30 (Documents second, Protection third, the areas with their chips in the Indication block, no qualifier sentence, the 320px sizes; data of 2026-09-29 without the primary-documents and ATC-explanation files, whose buttons the documents index gives alike), before → after, and the protection lead's top after: 390×844 Wegovy 672/804 → 482/614 (lead 734), Rinvoq 629/761 → 454/586 (706), Keytruda 675/807 → 454/586 (706), Humira 671/803 → 454/586 (646), Zinbryta 676/808 → 488/620 (739); 320×640 Wegovy 784/937 → 466/619 (739), Rinvoq 692/845 → 438/591 (711), Keytruda 714/867 → 438/591 (711), Humira 734/866 → 438/570 (630), Zinbryta 788/941 → 472/625 (744): both buttons above the fold at both sizes for all five, and at 390×844 the protection lead too (on phase 4's order it was at about 485px, above the buttons). Every authorized medicine (1,573; one has a single button): at 390×844 the second button is above the fold for all, the protection lead for all but 10 (median 706px); at 320×640 the second button is partly below the fold for 156 (231 before the short qualifier names and the 12px less above the headline): 74 whose headline takes three lines or more, 60 whose Status block is over 200px tall (a long company or holder line), 8 of them both, and 30 with a namesake note under the headline (Valdoxan, Multaq, Mylotarg); the product information button is below that screen for 10. No sideways scroll at 320, 390 or 1440, light and dark (and none in either sweep). axe on the card, More details open: at a scrolled position axe counts content under the sticky top bar as obscured (target-size: the audit's Soliris ATC ladder link and Humira area chip at 390, on main too); with the top bar static it still reports target-size on More details' C06 chip of "Colitis, Ulcerative" (Rinvoq at 320 and 390, Humira at 390), its rim overlapped by the line above and by the next line's chip: pre-existing (the same on main), fixed 2026-09-30 (see "Branch chips": the link padded up to 24px, not 28px, and chip lines 30px apart). Real phones (Laws of UX, second pass, 2026-09-30; data of 2026-09-30, mobile emulation, the layout viewport as tall as Safari leaves visible with its bars: 390×664 and 320×568), second document button's bottom before → after the company line and the 320px spacing: 390×664 Wegovy 614 → 573, Rinvoq 586 → 545, Keytruda 586 → 545, Humira 586 → 545, Zinbryta (withdrawn) 620 → 579: all above the fold, before and after; 320×568 Wegovy 619 → 564, Rinvoq 591 → 536, Keytruda 591 → 536, Humira 570 → 515, Zinbryta 625 → 570 (2px under the fold): four of five above the fold, none before. Every authorized medicine (1,574; one has a single button), second button partly below the fold: 390×664 56 → 19; 320×568 1,552 → 241 (the product information button not fully visible 61 → 18). Of those 241, 82 have a headline of three lines or more, 124 a Status block 150px or taller (a Union Register note, a long company name), the rest mostly a namesake line (Valdoxan, Multaq, Mylotarg); 2 end within 10px of the fold. No sideways scroll in either sweep. The substance card has the same Status block (the medicines authorized now, or their statuses, "first approved" and the first central approval's date (review of phase 4: "2 authorized since …" read as if both dated from then), company and modality); the answer strip, its CSS (`.strip`, `.strip-4`, `.strip-frame`) and `UI.card.strip` are gone. Condition, text and company pages keep their layout; their section headings are h2 since the audit of 2026-09-30 (S2). **Pivotal results** (2026-09-30, phase A2 of "Pivotal results"; see Architecture; `efficacy.js` pure and tested: `groupEfficacy()`, `formatEffect()`, `formatArms()`, `teaserText()`, and for the section `populationNote()`, `regimenLine()`, `endpointLine()`, `analysisLine()`, `efficacySourceUrl()`; `lookup.js` `efficacyTeaser()`, `efficacySection()`; copy `UI.efficacy`): the Indication block ends its text with a teaser before the therapeutic areas, the first indication's lead result ("Pivotal trial KEYNOTE-024: progression-free survival HR 0.50 (95% CI 0.37–0.68) vs platinum chemotherapy"; single-arm "…: response rate 37.1 (95% CI 28.6–46.2), single-arm"; indications in SmPC order), a `a.lead-link` opening More details at the section (`openMore("efficacy")`, `JUMPS.efficacy`, as the protection lead; its name adds ", see the results in More details"), then, muted, "and results for 6 more indications"; wrapping in full, never clamped (ruling R16: an ellipsis could cut an interval or a comparator mid-number; 14px on phones; each interval's en-dash followed by a word joiner, U+2060, so "0.37–0.57" never breaks after the dash); none until the file has loaded (no "Loading…" line in the block). More details › "Pivotal results" (none while the file loads, so no "Loading…" flash for the medicines without rows; `h2#efficacy`, the jump's target, after "About this medicine", before the protection estimate): "Extracted automatically from the product information; check the source." and "Results come from different trials, populations and comparators; not a head-to-head comparison.", then per indication (an h3 with its text, "Indication not stated" without one) its lead result and "More results (n)" (a disclosure: later and exploratory analyses, the whole trial, other endpoints); each result: the trial (bold), its population with a chip where it differs ("subgroup (matches EU indication)", "whole trial (EU indication is narrower)", "population differs from the EU indication"), "{regimen} (n = 279) vs {comparator} (n = 277)" (or "…, single-arm"), the endpoint ("progression-free survival (PFS), assessed by investigator, primary endpoint": "primary endpoint" only when the SmPC names it one), the effect as printed in 17px/600 in the ink (never coloured or ranked; the CI level as printed, "range" for a range; a single-arm median with the unit its `arm_measure` states, "median 11.1 months", else none, and none added to a value that already names one, "13.9 months"; a rate difference in "percentage points" only where the arms' measure says % or a quote prints the value with %, never for a proportion such as "0.12"; no effect line, and no effect in the teaser, without a value), the arms ("median 18.9 vs 10.2 months"), the analysis ("Primary analysis: {the source's words}", "Later analysis", "Exploratory analysis"), "Source: product information, p. 41" (the PDF at `#page=41`, an external link with the PDF marker) and, for a stale row, "From the product information of 4 May 2026; EMA has updated it since."; long names break rather than widen the card (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`), "More results" 44px on phones. Nothing on result tables, the dashboard or condition pages. Checked 2026-09-30 in Playwright (production build, the pilot's 76 gold SmPC rows of 18 NSCLC medicines mapped to the site file, not committed): the document buttons and the protection lead where they were, before and after, at 390×844 (Wegovy 482/614, lead 734; Rinvoq 454/586, 706; Keytruda 454/586, 706; Alecensa 482/614, 734) and 320×640 (Wegovy 466/619, 739; Rinvoq 438/591, 711; Keytruda 438/591, 711); the teaser in full (fix round 1): Keytruda's 3 lines (72px) at 1440, 4 (96px) at 390 and 5 (120px) at 320, Alecensa's 2 / 3 / 4, Lumykras' and Lazcluze's 2 / 2 / 3; the Indication block follows the buttons and protection lead, so their positions do not move; a click opens More details with `#efficacy` focused and its section at the top (under the sticky search on phones, 73px); no sideways scroll at 1440, 390 or 320, light and dark, More results open; axe clean on the card with More details and every More results open (Keytruda, Alecensa, Lumykras, Lazcluze, Wegovy); without the file no teaser, section or "Loading…".

## Downloads and request budget (per daily run)
EMA (≤ 1 request / 2 s, shared throttle): medicines JSON — plain GET when the cached copy in `.cache/ema/` is > 24 h old (not persisted by CI); EPAR documents index (`documents-output-epar_documents_json-report_en.json`, 29 MB) and orphan designations (`medicines-output-orphan_designations-json-report_en.json` — note the hyphen before `json`) — conditional GET (ETag/Last-Modified) at most once per 20 h (just under the daily cron, as CI restores sidecars with old mtimes). EC (≤ 1 request / s, own throttle): Union Register `ods_products.json` (BETA; fields pinned), same rule. NLM MeSH and ChEMBL: only when changed. Caches: `.cache/downloads/{mesh,ema-documents,ema-orphans,union-register}/` with `source.json` sidecars; `.cache/downloads/chembl/` holds `status.json` and the release folder.
- **EMA product information PDFs (SmPC ATC codes)**: at most `APPROVAL_ATLAS_SMPC_BUDGET` per run (default 8; `0` = none), ≥ 20 s apart (on top of the EMA throttle), only for products whose EMA code is incomplete, malformed or missing, never checked or whose product information changed since (URL or `last_updated_date` in the documents index), or checked more than 180 days ago as `no_text` or `not_found` (in case that was passing), or checked by an older reader (`reader_version` below `smpc_reader_version`) that found no complete code (`no_code`, `incomplete`): Authorised first, then the newest documents. Bump `smpc_reader_version` when `extract_smpc_atc_codes()` learns to read more (version 2, phase 4e: "ATC" and "code" on two lines, "(ATC) code", a letter O for a zero in a digit position ("VO4CX"); its 19 re-checks ran on 2026-09-28 in two local runs). A 429, a response that is not a PDF (no `%PDF-` header, e.g. a firewall page with status 200) or any other failure ends the step with a warning (nothing recorded), never the build; a 404 is recorded as `not_found`. Each result is saved at once to `.cache/downloads/ema-smpc/checks.json` (seeded from the committed `ema_medicine_smpc_atc.json` when the cache is empty), so runs resume. PDFs go to a temp file and are deleted; PDF text needs `pdftools` (poppler; CI installs `libpoppler-cpp-dev` before setup-r). Local backfill: `APPROVAL_ATLAS_SMPC_BUDGET=25 Rscript scripts/run-pipeline.R`, repeated (about 10 min per 25); afterwards commit only `site/public/data/ema_medicine_smpc_atc.json` (CI seeds its checks from it) and restore the other data files (`git restore` them).
- **EMA product information PDFs (efficacy)** (Pivotal results; on demand only, never in CI or the daily pipeline): `Rscript scripts/extract-efficacy.R` after a pipeline run (it reads the cached EMA medicines and documents index), on the owner's machine with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in `.Renviron` (gitignored). Candidates: Authorised medicines with a product information PDF never extracted, or whose PDF URL or `last_updated_date` changed since (`data-raw/efficacy-extractions.json`), at most `APPROVAL_ATLAS_EFFICACY_BUDGET` per run (default 25); `APPROVAL_ATLAS_EFFICACY_ONLY` (product numbers, comma-separated) extracts exactly those, even when done (unset it afterwards, or every run bills them again). PDFs through the SmPC fetcher (`R/smpc-atc.R`: ≥ 20 s apart on the EMA throttle, a 429 or a response that is not a PDF ends the fetching, a 404 recorded as `not_found`); their text (layout kept) cached in `.cache/downloads/ema-efficacy/`, the PDFs deleted. One Message Batch per run (`APPROVAL_ATLAS_EFFICACY_MODEL`, default `claude-sonnet-5-5`; half the list price, $2 / $10 per million input / output tokens for Sonnet 5.5, so $1 / $5 in a batch; up to 24 h, usually less); the batch id is saved before polling (`pending-batch.json`), so a run that stops or gives up waiting (30 min without a status) is resumed, never paid twice. Each run writes both `data-raw` files (one record per line, LF): commit them; the daily pipeline turns them into the site file. Cost (spec): the whole run over every Authorised medicine about $50 with Sonnet 5.5 in batches and about 9 h of EMA fetching over resumable nights. Then `Rscript scripts/efficacy-review-sample.R` for the owner's review (flagged rows and a 10% sample). A0: `Rscript scripts/eval-efficacy-gold.R` (a few dollars; one batch per model, resumable the same way; `APPROVAL_ATLAS_GOLD_MODELS`) in the checkout that holds the pilot's files in `.remember/efficacy`.
- **GLEIF** (`api.gleif.org`, on demand only, never in CI or the daily pipeline): `Rscript scripts/match-gleif.R` after a pipeline run (it reads the cached EMA and Union Register files), ≥ 1.2 s apart (GLEIF allows 60 a minute), own realm, no retry on 429, at most 170 requests per run (about 150 for a full run: names 10 per request with `include=ultimate-parent`; misses by the name without legal form in the holder's country; brand words among group top entities (`ultimateChildCount>=1`, hints only); parents' records by LEI, 100 per request). Spaces must be `%20` (a `+` silently finds nothing). Responses cached by URL in `.cache/downloads/gleif/` (`index.json`, with each URL's file and fetch date (an entry without a date is dated by its file), and `responses/`), so a rerun resumes; a response older than 30 days is fetched again (`run_gleif_matching(max_age_days =)`, `Inf` never), and the matches (and `meta.json`) carry the oldest response date used, not the run date. Commit `data-raw/gleif-holder-matches.json`; review matches below 0.95 before setting `reviewed`.
- **ChEMBL molecules** (modality, `R/chembl-molecules.R`; `www.ebi.ac.uk/chembl/api/data/molecule.json`, on demand only, never in CI or the daily pipeline): `Rscript scripts/match-chembl-modality.R` after a pipeline run (`run_chembl_matching()`: every key the run classifies, read from its `site/public/data` files through `modality_inputs()`). Per key, stop at the first hit: `pref_name__iexact`, then `molecule_synonyms__molecule_synonym__iexact` (`limit=50`, `only=` molecule_chembl_id, pref_name, molecule_type, structure_type, max_phase, biotherapeutic, molecule_hierarchy, atc_classifications, molecule_synonyms), then the same for its alternates in order (the one other active substance of its medicines, curated equivalent keys, the name before a comma, "hydrogen carbonate" → "bicarbonate", the WHO name of its medicines' one level-5 non-vaccine code); pick: synonym type INN < EMA < BAN/USAN/JAN/USP < FDA/ATC/BNF < MERCK_INDEX < OTHER < TRADE_NAME < RESEARCH_CODE, then parent before salt, higher `max_phase`, lower ChEMBL ID (all candidates kept when more than one); the parents of salt matches by `molecule_chembl_id__in`, 50 per request; for an unmatched key that is no vaccine description, its variants (spelling, qualifier, salt, isotope; at most 8, the first that finds any) and then `pref_name__istartswith` "{key} " (keys of 6+ letters) as candidates for review. 1.1 s apart (`throttled_request(realm = "chembl")`), no retry on 429 (the run stops, the cache kept), at most 2,000 requests per run. Responses cached by URL in `.cache/downloads/chembl-molecules/<release>/` (`index.json` URL → file and fetch date, `responses/`), never refetched within a release, so a rerun resumes and needs none; a new ChEMBL release starts a new folder. The prototype's 1,648 responses (2026-09-28) were converted into this cache (URLs rebuilt with the canonical fields; its matches, `.remember/modality/chembl-matches.json`, seeded the file), so the first R run needed 170 requests (the R1/R2 keys and alternates); the pipeline warns when the file's release differs from ChEMBL's (`status.json`) and lists keys missing from it. Commit `data-raw/chembl-substance-matches.json`; review ambiguous rows and variant candidates before setting `reviewed` (with `molecule_chembl_id` filled by hand for a variant).
- **EMA EPAR pages, copy scan** (step 4 review; on demand only, never in CI or the daily pipeline; `R/epar-copy-scan.R`): `Rscript scripts/scan-epar-copies.R` after a pipeline run (it reads `ema_medicine_protection.json` and `ema_medicines.json` in `site/public/data`; changes no data): the EPAR page (`medicine_url`) of each Authorised product with basis `own` and status `protected` or `unclear` (not `ended`) not yet curated or checked, by product number (464 protected and 44 unclear on 2026-09-29); at most `APPROVAL_ATLAS_EPAR_BUDGET` pages per run (default 30, about 10 min), ≥ 20 s apart on top of the EMA throttle. A 429, another failure or a page without the medicine's name (a firewall page with status 200) ends the run with a warning; a 404 is recorded as `not_found`. Each result is saved at once to `.cache/downloads/ema-epar-copies/checks.json` (not committed), so reruns resume. It lists the pages with a paragraph saying "hybrid medicine", "generic medicine" or "biosimilar medicine" or "biosimilar medicinal product" (not the "Generic and hybrid medicines" link), with that paragraph: check each by hand before adding a row to `curated_copy_medicines()`.
- **WHOCC** (`atcddd.fhi.no`, ≤ 1 request / 2 s, own realm): the yearly lists `atc_ddd_new_and_alterations_{year}_final.xlsx` of the index year (falls back to last year's on 404 in January) and of the year before (phase 4d: ChEMBL lags about a year, so both lists' new codes and name alterations are read, the newer first), and `temporary_atc_and_ddd.xlsx` — conditional GET at most once per 20 h; the cumulative ATC alterations page (no validators) — at most every 30 days; single ATC/DDD Index pages only for codes no other source names (the level-4 parent of a level-5 code), ≤ 10 per run, cached parsed in `.cache/downloads/whocc/index/{code}.json` for the index year (older pages rechecked at most monthly). Never crawl the index (it is sold, not published). WHOCC only names and maps codes, so it never stops the build: a failed download (network error, 429, 5xx, a removed file) uses the cached copy with a warning, or, without one, goes without that list (its names, moves and `meta.json` entry; the curated retired codes and names still apply); an index page that fails ends that run's lookups with a warning. A list that no longer parses still stops the build. Caches: `.cache/downloads/whocc/{updates,updates-previous,temporary,alterations,index}/`.

## EMA source facts (profiled 2026-09-26)
- Medicines JSON regenerated twice daily (06:00 / 18:00 Amsterdam); no ETag; If-Modified-Since works. `meta.timestamp` is Amsterdam local time labelled `Z` → use only its date part.
- EMA rate-limits: HTTP 429 after roughly 9–46 requests at 1–2 s spacing. Any per-product EMA fetching must be slow, budgeted and resumable.
- 39 columns, all strings, missing = `""`. 10 Yes/No flags. 11 `dd/mm/yyyy` date fields.
- Text: entities `&nbsp;` `&lt;` `&gt;` only (plus truncated `&nbsp`), literal U+00A0; no HTML tags. 122 indications have lossy `?` for ≥/≤ upstream.
- `;` fields: items contain commas (MeSH) — never split on comma. ATC: placeholders, L2–L4 codes, 2 malformed (`LX1XX02`, `VO4D`).
- Date gaps: 85 Withdrawn + Expired/Lapsed/Suspended rows without an end date; 6 Authorised without MA date; Xevudy end date < MA date; Enzepi's suspension date is really a withdrawal date.
- Reuse terms (EMA legal notice): acknowledge EMA as the source in every copy; no EMA logo; don't imply endorsement.
- IRIS-era procedure numbers (`VR/…`) have no II/IA/IB/X prefix.
- Product information PDFs (profiled 2026-09-27): ETag, `Last-Modified` = the documents index's `last_updated_date`; 0.5–3 MB, one PDF per product holding every SmPC (one per strength/form), Annex II and the labelling. Section 5.1: "Pharmacotherapeutic group: …, ATC code:" with the code often wrapped onto the next line ("L01XL12."); some say "not yet assigned" or give an incomplete code; "BATCH" contains "ATC" (match the label with a word boundary). Old PIs may be image-only (`no_text`).
- ATC gaps on 2026-09-27: 148 Authorised products with an incomplete code (36 at level 2, 21 at level 3, 91 at level 4) and 20 with none, all with product information; 419 products of any status, 214 with product information. EMA still uses retired codes (level 5, and L01XC/L01XE at level 4).

## Other sources
- MeSH: `https://nlmpubs.nlm.nih.gov/projects/mesh/MESH_FILES/xmlmesh/desc{YYYY}.gz` (ASCII discontinued 2026; year detected from the listing; new year ~December). NLM terms: acknowledge NLM, no endorsement, state the MeSH version.
- ChEMBL API `https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chembl/api/data/` (release from `status.json`; CHEMBL_37 on 2026-05-01). CC BY-SA 3.0; cite Mendez et al. 2019 (per ChEMBL's REQUIRED.ATTRIBUTION); keep ChEMBL IDs and show the release.
- WHO ATC names (via ChEMBL and the WHOCC site): © WHOCC — use requires reference to the WHOCC, no commercial distribution, no modification → verbatim, excluded from our licence ([terms](https://atcddd.fhi.no/copyright_disclaimer/)).
- WHOCC ATC/DDD Index `https://atcddd.fhi.no` (ISO-8859-1 HTML). Index pages (`/atc_ddd_index/?code={code}&showdescription=no`) have no validators (`Last-Modified` is always now); the ancestor path (`<b><a href="./?code=…">`) names every level above, the table lists the code or its children; a retired or unknown code has no row of its own (L01XE: ancestors only; L04AC28: empty), so the parser checks the code is present. "Last updated: 2026-01-20" = the index version. Yearly list (xlsx, ETag + Last-Modified; sheets "New ATC 5th levels", "New 3rd and 4th levels", "ATC level alterations", "ATC level name alterations" (code, previous name, "New ATC level name"), DDDs; same sheets in 2025 and 2026; names carry stray zero-width spaces); temporary list for next year's index (sheet names with stray blanks; codes may still change). Cumulative ATC alterations (`/atc_ddd_alterations__cumulative/atc_alterations/`, 2005–2026, ~355 rows: previous code, substance, new code or "deleted", year; footnotes as `<sup><a title>`: "Previous ATC level name: …", "Split of code …", "… only …" = the old code stays in use). ChEMBL lags the index by about a year (none of the 2026 changes are in ChEMBL_37) and spells some names differently; a one-off cross-check of every code the data uses against the index (2026-09-27; not repeated at runtime) found 13 names that differ and, besides B06C and L03AD (EMA codes WHO never had), no other problems.
- GLEIF LEI records (API `https://api.gleif.org/api/v1/lei-records`, profiled 2026-09-28): CC0; `filter[entity.names]` is a case-insensitive "contains" over legal, other and transliterated names, commas separate names (so a name's own commas become spaces); `fuzzycompletions` returns nothing. Most big-pharma EU MA vehicles have no LEI (Roche Registration, Novartis Europharm, Pfizer Europe MA EEIG…); parents go stale after mergers and spin-offs (Sandoz GmbH, lapsed, still names Novartis AG; Upjohn → Pfizer); former names find renamed entities (Genzyme Europe B.V. = Sanofi B.V.); homonyms abroad (Orion, Otsuka), opaque holdings (Chiesi → Valline, STADA → Nidda German Topco, Boehringer → C.H. Boehringer Sohn). Hence curated groups, GLEIF as provenance.
- Union Register holders (profiled 2026-09-28): every row has `MAH` {URI, Name, Address} (null for most nationally authorised rows); `MAH.URI` is one record per name and address, not per legal entity; the country is the address's last comma part in the local language (a small map in `R/orphan.R`, unknown → null); one "name" is an address (Deciphera); orphan-designation rows can name natural persons, so only Human rows are read.
- Internet Archive copies of the 2020 index (`web.archive.org/web/2020062…/https://www.whocc.no/atc_ddd_index/?code=L01XC|L01XE`): the only source for retired level-4 names; checked once by hand, never fetched at runtime.

## R conventions
- tidyverse / r-lib; always `package::function()`; no `library()`.
- `purrr::map()` + `purrr::list_rbind()`, never `map_dfr()`. `dplyr::case_when()` with `.default =`.
- `\(x)` only for one-liners, never in pipes; `function()` for anything multi-line.
- Descriptive unabbreviated names; small single-purpose functions; comments only for the "why".
- Loud failures via `cli::cli_abort()` listing offenders. Dependencies via renv (`snapshot.type = "explicit"`, `snapshot.dev = TRUE`; lockfile repo = dated Posit Package Manager snapshot; on CI `setup-r` overrides it with P3M `latest` Linux binaries — versions stay pinned by `renv.lock`).
- Tests: testthat 3e, fixtures of real records in `tests/testthat/fixtures/`; no network in tests. Coverage ≥ 80% overall, 100% for `R/validate-ema.R`.

## Commands
```sh
Rscript scripts/run-pipeline.R                       # fetch (cached) → validate → write site/public/data
Rscript scripts/match-gleif.R                        # on demand: GLEIF matches → data-raw/gleif-holder-matches.json
Rscript scripts/scan-epar-copies.R                   # on demand: EPAR pages naming unflagged copies, for review
Rscript scripts/match-chembl-modality.R              # on demand: ChEMBL molecule types → data-raw/chembl-substance-matches.json
Rscript scripts/extract-efficacy.R                   # on demand, paid (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY): pivotal results → data-raw/efficacy-rows.json, efficacy-extractions.json
Rscript scripts/efficacy-review-sample.R             # after an extraction: flagged rows and a 10% sample → .remember/efficacy/review-{date}.md
Rscript scripts/eval-efficacy-gold.R                 # on demand, paid (phase A0): models scored against the pilot's gold rows
Rscript -e "testthat::test_local()"
Rscript -e "covr::package_coverage()"
Rscript -e "pkgload::load_all(quiet = TRUE); lintr::lint_package()"
npm ci && npm run dev                                 # http://localhost:5173 (uses the data in site/public/data)
npm test && npm run build                             # output: site/dist
```
Node: CI uses 24 (switch to 26 once LTS, 2026-10-28). First MeSH download is ~17 MB (313 MB unpacked on disk; parse peaks ~1.9 GB RAM).

## CI (`.github/workflows/pipeline.yml`)
Daily 06:37 UTC + manual + push to main. `build`: lint → R tests → pipeline on live data → `npm audit --audit-level=high --omit=dev` → npm test → build → Pages artifact + data artifact. Then, on `main` only: `deploy` (`pages: write`, `id-token: write`, environment `github-pages`) and, on scheduled/manual runs, `commit-data` (`contents: write`; see "Data committed"). One run at a time (`concurrency: pipeline`), 120 min timeout. `.cache/downloads` persists between runs via actions/cache (rolling key `downloads-v1-…`, saved even on failure; bump `v1` to force a refetch). Top-level `contents: read`, write grants per job only; no stored secrets. Every action pinned by full commit SHA (tag in a comment); `.github/dependabot.yml` updates actions and npm monthly. Failures email whoever last edited the cron line (enable GitHub Settings → Notifications → Actions → "Only notify for failed workflows").

## Trust baseline (with hosting; instead of ISO 27001 / SOC 2)
User wants credibility for a public site. ISO 27001 / SOC 2 certify organizations, not a static site with no backend or user data, so they are out of scope. Do instead:
- Security: Content-Security-Policy meta tag (GitHub Pages cannot set headers); no third-party scripts/fonts/trackers; Dependabot alerts + secret scanning + push protection; branch protection on `main`; CI actions pinned by SHA; `SECURITY.md`. Status of each: `docs/mvsp.md`.
- Privacy (GDPR): no cookies, no analytics; privacy note (GitHub logs IPs and page addresses, which hold the search, as host; offline mode stores only site data on the device; per-viewer conveniences in localStorage (theme, intro seen/closed, recently viewed lookups with a Clear in the search) stay on the device and are never sent).
- Accessibility: WCAG 2.2 AA target; axe-core in the headless check; manual keyboard pass.
- Intended use: informational only; not medical or legal advice; not a medical device; data may lag EMA.
- MVSP (mvsp.dev) self-assessment in `docs/mvsp.md` (control → status → evidence; review yearly): `SECURITY.md` with scope, safe harbor, GitHub private vulnerability reporting, fix within 90 days, notice within 72 h (1.1, 1.7, 3.4); data inventory (public regulatory data only, no personal data) + data-flow diagram (3.1, 3.2); Dependabot + `npm audit` gate (2.6); test forbidding `innerHTML`/`.html()` in `site/src` (2.5, 3.3); 2FA + branch protection (4.2); sub-processor list: GitHub (hosting, CI) (4.3); recovery = rebuild from repo + public sources (4.4). N/A: SSO, passwords, auth logging, physical access, pentest, training (no accounts, no backend, solo project).
- GitHub Pages cannot set response headers: no HSTS on custom domains, no `frame-ancestors`/X-Frame-Options (verified: jekyllrb.com on Pages sends none). Accepted risk (no logins or state-changing actions); alternatives are HSTS preload on the vrognas.com apex (Netlify) or hosting on Netlify with `_headers`.

## Hosting
Target: `approval-atlas.vrognas.com` on GitHub Pages (site served from `/`). DNS is Netlify DNS (NS1). Steps 1–3 and 5 done (2026-09-26); step 4 pending.
1. Verify `vrognas.com` in GitHub account Settings → Pages (TXT `_github-pages-challenge-vrognas` in Netlify DNS).
2. `gh api -X POST repos/vrognas/approval-atlas/pages -f build_type=workflow`; set custom domain in Settings → Pages (no CNAME file needed with Actions).
3. Netlify DNS: CNAME `approval-atlas` → `vrognas.github.io` — must be a CNAME, not A records (apex CAA only allows Netlify's Let's Encrypt account; `vrognas.github.io` publishes its own CAA).
4. Enforce HTTPS once the certificate is issued.
5. upload-pages-artifact@v5 + deploy-pages@v5 `deploy` job (`pages: write`, `id-token: write`, `environment: github-pages`) and data commit-back (60-day rule) — see "CI".
